The hold only delays the trigger callback, not ingestion. If a new trigger from the same sender arrives during a hold, the pending trigger is flushed immediately.

**Consequence — a trigger-bearing event reaches `handleInbound` twice:** once on the immediate `trigger: undefined` emit and once on the post-hold emit with the trigger populated (same `event.id`). Every `handleInbound` step on this path is idempotent except steering: a reply to an active session (DM replies are *always* triggers; a bare group reply to a bot message is *now* one too, via the resolver) would otherwise be injected into the session twice. `steerReplyToActiveSession` therefore dedups by `event.id` (bounded FIFO of recently-steered ids) — it steers at most once per event but still returns `true` on the redundant delivery so the reply never falls through to the spawn path.

### Outbound

Messages with both text and attachments are sent as separate Matrix messages: the text goes first as its own message (preserving long text), then each attachment is uploaded separately. The text event is the primary delivery receipt.

### Room-label resolution

The Matrix client exposes `channelContext({ roomId })` (`src/matrix/native-client.ts`), which resolves both a human room label (`displayName ?? canonicalAlias ?? roomId`, suffixed ` (parentSpaceName)` when the room has a name-resolvable parent space) and the room's DM flag from a single `channelInfo` call; `channelLabel({ roomId })` is a thin wrapper keeping only the label. This is a homeserver-backed call. Three consumers map a per-room timeline key → account + room id → that account's client:

- The **diary header** (§9c), which needs a label per job — via the shared `resolveChannelLabel` resolver in `app.ts`.
- The **`<runtime_state>` channel descriptor** (§ "Satellite block and final user turn"), via the builder's injected `resolveChannelContext` hook in `app.ts` (label + DM flag, never-throws, null on an unresolvable timeline).
- **`RoomLabelCache`** (`src/matrix/room-label-cache.ts`), which caches resolved labels in `room_metadata` so the observability console room list shows real names. Because resolution is network-backed it must never block the inbound hot path: `ensureLabel(timelineKey)` — called for every non-edit inbound event in `handleInbound` — does a cheap synchronous freshness check (is a label cached and younger than the 6-hour TTL?) and, only when a re-resolve is due and not already in flight, kicks off the resolve + persist in the background. Concurrent calls for the same timeline coalesce via an in-flight set; a failed resolve is logged and leaves the prior label (or the room-id fallback) in place. To stop a persistently-failing room from firing one homeserver call per inbound message, failures are recorded in an in-memory negative cache and the freshness gate (in both `ensureLabel` and `backfillAll`) skips a re-resolve until a `failureCooldownMs` cooldown elapses (default 5 minutes); a later success clears the entry. A resolve that returns an empty/whitespace label is treated the same as a failure — not persisted (the store assumes non-empty labels: `room_metadata.display_name` is NOT NULL but does not reject `''`, and `listConsoleRooms` only falls back to the room id on NULL) and it engages the same backoff. At startup, after `provider.start()`, a throttled `backfillAll()` (fire-and-forget, spaced to avoid a homeserver thundering herd) resolves labels for already-known — possibly idle — rooms so they get names without waiting for their next message.

---

## 6a. Undecryptable (UTD) events

Encrypted events whose room keys haven't arrived can't be decrypted. Rather than dropping them, the system surfaces them like a human Matrix client does — a "🔒 unable to decrypt" placeholder showing sender + timestamp but no body — everywhere (live sync and backfill), and replaces them with the real event once keys arrive.

Keys arrive two ways: shared live by other devices (the common case for momentarily-late keys), and **fetched per-session from the server-side key backup on decryption failure** — the client is built with `BackupDownloadStrategy::AfterDecryptionFailure`, and `restore_recovery` imports the backup decryption key from secret storage on every start (see section 6, "Rust native core"), so each UTD event triggers a paginated fetch of just its megolm session from the backup; any session that previously backed up its keys thus makes that history decryptable on this device as the event (live, or a stored UTD row re-driven by the sweeper) is re-attempted. A key "never arrives" only for events whose megolm session was never backed up by any of the account's sessions (e.g. history from before the account joined the room).

### Native surfacing

The native module uses raw matrix-sdk callbacks + `room.messages()` (not the `matrix-sdk-ui` Timeline), so there is no automatic re-decryption signal. UTD is surfaced at two points:

- **Backfill / `messageSummary`** (`events::summarize_timeline_event`): when `event.kind.is_utd()`, it returns a `MatrixMessageSummary` with `body=""`, `undecryptable=Some(true)`, `session_id` from the SDK's UTD info, a `utd_reason` code (see below), and event_id/sender/ts from the raw `m.room.encrypted` envelope — instead of `None`. This covers `read_messages`, pinned events, and `message_summary`.
- **Live sync** (`client/mod.rs` raw `Raw<AnySyncTimelineEvent>` handler): the SDK re-injects decrypted events under their decrypted type, so an event still typed `m.room.encrypted` in the raw handler is a live UTD. It is pushed as a `MatrixInboundEvent` placeholder via `normalize_utd_inbound_event`. `last_successful_decryption_at` is **not** bumped (no decryption happened). `utd_reason` is **always `None`** here — the raw handler receives only the encrypted envelope, not the SDK's structured `UnableToDecryptInfo`; the reason becomes available only when the sweeper later re-fetches the event through the summary path.

Both `MatrixMessageSummary` and `MatrixInboundEvent` carry `undecryptable: Option<bool>` + `session_id: Option<String>` + `utd_reason: Option<String>` (camelCase `utdReason` over the JSON bridge), mirrored in `src/matrix/native-types.ts`. A UTD event has empty body and empty media. `utd_reason` is a stable lowercase code mapped from the SDK `UnableToDecryptReason` (`missing_megolm_session`, `unknown_megolm_message_index`, `malformed_encrypted_event`, `megolm_decryption_failure`, `payload_deserialization_failure`, `mismatched_identity_keys`, `sender_identity_not_trusted`, `unknown`); diagnostic only, surfaced into the canonical `undecryptable.reason` by both the live (`inbound.ts`) and backfill (`classifySummary`) producers.

### Storage & rendering

UTD events are stored through the normal path with `enrichment_status='skipped'` (nothing to enrich — no body/media). The canonical `undecryptable` field rides inside `event_json`. A generated column `is_undecryptable` (VIRTUAL, from `json_extract(event_json, '$.undecryptable')`) with a partial index makes UTD rows cheap to find for the sweeper without scanning JSON (it persists across restarts, unlike an in-memory set).

A UTD event's thread/edit relation is megolm-encrypted at store time (`relatesTo` is undefined), so it can't be filtered by thread membership. During **initial backfill** of a thread timeline, `classifySummary` therefore lands UTD events on the **room** timeline rather than dropping them (issue #5) — mirroring the live UTD path (which always stores UTD on the room/DM key) — so the re-decryption sweeper can recover and re-home them to the thread once keys arrive. The UTD's canonical id (`matrix:<account>:<eventId>`) is independent of the timeline key, so dedup against any later live/backfill arrival still holds. The canonical id format is injected via `CommonContext.buildId: (externalId) => string` (§11.3 provider-agnostic classify.ts) — Matrix callers pass `(id) => \`matrix:${accountId}:${id}\`` — so `classifyForTimeline`/`classifyForRoom` do not hardcode the `matrix:` prefix.

The renderer (`src/context/renderer.ts`) has a UTD branch in **both** `renderCompactMessage` and `renderRichMessage` that emits the lock placeholder with sender + timestamp, ignoring body/attachments — so a UTD event reaching the model looks exactly like what a human client shows, and the body can never leak. Because summarization input is assembled by the same context builder over the same stored rows (`getTimelineEventsBetween`), UTD placeholders automatically appear in summary **input** for any range they fall in (they need not appear in the summary output).

### Re-decryption sweeper (`src/redecryption/`)

A background loop (wired in `app.ts`, started after the provider, stopped first on drain) periodically retries stored UTD events. Each tick:

1. `TimelineStore.getUndecrypted(limit)` returns due UTD rows (oldest first), each paired with its persisted `redecrypt_attempts` count, filtered by a per-event exponential backoff (1 min base, capped at 1 h) so events whose keys may never arrive don't hammer the homeserver. **RC8 gate:** `#probe` checks `event.provider !== "matrix"` first — non-Matrix providers have no megolm concept, so their events must never reach `retireUndecrypted`; the sweeper logs `redecryption_skipped_non_matrix` and returns early, leaving the row in place for a future provider-specific path. **Give-up ceiling:** the query excludes rows whose `redecrypt_attempts` have reached `MAX_REDECRYPT_ATTEMPTS` (and the `REDECRYPT_RETIRED` sentinel), so permanently-dead UTD rows (sent before the bot joined → keys will never arrive) leave the oldest-first candidate window and can't starve newer, decryptable rows. Each failed probe persists an attempt (`recordRedecryptFailure`); the in-memory backoff map is pruned every tick to the ids still in rotation so it stays bounded, and on a cold start the backoff is reseeded from the persisted attempt count so a long-dead row isn't probed immediately on boot.
2. For each, it re-fetches the summary via the native `messageSummary` primitive (which re-runs `load_or_fetch_event` + summarize) — resolving the room id from the event's timeline key. A UTD row with no resolvable room/event id can never be re-fetched, so it is **retired** (`retireUndecryptedEvent` stamps the sentinel) and leaves rotation in the DB rather than sitting front-of-queue forever. Across multiple bot accounts sharing a room, `resolveMultiAccountRetry` tries every account and prefers a decrypted result (a megolm key one account lacks may be known to another), falling back to a still-UTD summary only when none decrypts; if every account throws (room unknown / fetch failure) it rethrows so the failure is treated as transient (not collapsed to a non-message signal).
3. The probe outcome is one of four:
   - **decrypted `m.replace` edit** (`summary.relatesTo.relType === "m.replace"`) → the decrypted UTD is an edit (edits can arrive UTD in E2EE rooms). Rather than convert the placeholder into a standalone message, `#applyDecryptedEdit` applies the replacement to the target via the shared `TimelineStore.applyEdit` primitive (same path as the live edit — see section 6, "Message edits") and then **deletes the placeholder row** (`deleteUndecrypted`) so no standalone duplicate lingers. If the target is missing, the replacement is **parked** in `pending_edits` for replay when the target lands (issue #12), and the placeholder is still deleted. Enrichment/captions are re-armed only per the stored status, with the same inactive-timeline gating. (Handled before the normal decrypted-summary branch, so `decryptedCanonical` never sees an `m.replace`.)
   - **decrypted, non-UTD summary** → the megolm session is now known: `TimelineStore.replaceUndecrypted(eventId, updater, computeStatus)` rewrites `body`/`event_json` with the decrypted content (flag cleared, real attachments) and sets `enrichment_status` to the value `computeStatus` returns. The status mirrors the live append path and respects the timeline lifecycle (`postDecryptStatus`): for an **inactive** timeline it is `'inactive'` (decryption still happens, but enrichment/captioning is deferred to the activation bulk-flip — neither pool is nudged, per spec §3); otherwise it is `needsEnrichment(updated) ? 'pending' : 'skipped'`. `replaceUndecryptedEvent` reads the (possibly re-homed) timeline's live `timeline_state` inside the same write transaction and returns the chosen `status`, so the sweeper's notify decisions match exactly what was stored — `notifyEnrichment` only when `'pending'`, captions only when the decrypted event has attachments **and** the timeline isn't inactive. The `updater` (`decryptedCanonical`) also recovers the now-decrypted **relation** from `summary.relatesTo`, mirroring the backfill `classifySummary`: a bare in-reply-to (no `relType`) sets `replyTo`; an `m.thread` relation sets `threadId` **and re-homes the row** to the thread timeline key (`matrix:<acct>:room:<roomId>:thread:<root>`) — a stored UTD always lands on the room timeline because its relation was encrypted at store time, so the true placement is only knowable after decryption. (`m.replace` edits are handled by the branch above, so they never reach this merge.) `replaceUndecryptedEvent` persists the new `timeline_key`/`threadId` (the canonical id — the dedup key — never changes) and a re-home is logged as `redecryption_rehomed_to_thread`.
   - **`null` return** (the native primitive returned `Ok(None)` — fetched & decrypted, but a non-renderable message: sticker/poll/reaction) → the live append path never stores these, so the placeholder is **deleted** (`deleteUndecryptedEvent`, guarded to act only while the row is still UTD) to match live parity. The row leaves rotation; no enrichment/caption nudge.
   - **still-UTD summary** (`undecryptable === true`) **or a thrown fetch failure** → a transient miss: persist an attempt and back off. (A still-UTD summary is distinct from a `null` return — the native UTD path always yields a summary with `undecryptable: true`, never `None`, so `null` is an unambiguous "decrypted non-message" signal.)
   - `replaceUndecryptedEvent` returns `{ event, replaced, status }`. When the row is already non-UTD (the sweeper raced backfill or a `message_summary` touch that decrypted it first), it writes nothing and reports `replaced: false`; the sweeper then skips re-arming enrichment/captions and the `redecryption_replaced` log.

**Residual limitation:** a thread message that *never* re-decrypts (its room keys never arrive) stays permanently on the **room** timeline, since its `m.thread` relation is forever encrypted and the sweeper can never learn it belongs to a thread. Only re-decryptable thread UTDs are re-homed.

Configured under `[timeline]`: `redecryption_sweep_interval_ms` (default 60000; 0 disables the sweeper) and `redecryption_sweep_batch` (default 50; max UTD events probed per tick).

## 6b. User Identity Store

Some providers (Discord) use stable internal user IDs that are not human-readable, and users can change their visible name over time. The user identity store tracks the current username and display name for each `(provider, user_id)` pair, as well as a bounded history of prior usernames, so the system can render events under the sender's current name and expand retrieval queries to also search under aliases.

### Tables

Two tables form the identity store (defined in `USER_IDENTITIES_SCHEMA`, included as a sub-constant of `SCHEMA`; added by the **v4→v5** migration `addUserIdentityTables` for existing databases):

- **`user_identities`** — `WITHOUT ROWID`, primary key `(provider, user_id)`. Columns: `username` (current), `display_name` (nullable), `first_seen` / `last_seen` / `updated_at` (all unix ms). Indexed on `(provider, updated_at DESC)`. One row per user, mutated in place on each upsert.
- **`user_identity_aliases`** — prior username values. `INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT` (never reuses rowids, so "delete lowest rowid" always evicts the genuinely oldest alias). Columns: `provider`, `user_id`, `username` (the previous name), `display_name` (snapshot at rename), `recorded_at`. Indexed on `(provider, user_id, rowid)`. Bounded at `USER_IDENTITY_ALIAS_BOUND = 16` rows per user; the oldest (lowest rowid) are deleted when the count exceeds the bound.

### Upsert rule (`Storage.upsertUserIdentity`)

Called from the shared ingest path in `app.ts` (`handleInbound`) immediately after `router.route()`, inside a fire-and-forget promise chain (never blocks the hot path; errors are logged as warnings and swallowed). The hook runs only when `inbound.event.sender.username` is truthy — **Matrix events never set `sender.username`**, so the hook is never called for Matrix, guaranteeing zero writes for the current sole Matrix deployment.

Three cases, all executed inside a single `write()` call:

1. **First sighting** — no existing row: insert `user_identities` with `first_seen = last_seen = updated_at = observedAt`.
2. **Username unchanged** — existing row with same `username`: update `last_seen` and (if changed) `display_name` only. No alias row is created.
3. **Username changed** — existing row with a different `username`: insert the old `(username, display_name)` into `user_identity_aliases` with `recorded_at = observedAt` (the timestamp of the event that first observed the rename), then update `user_identities` with the new name and `updated_at = observedAt`. After inserting, evict any aliases beyond `USER_IDENTITY_ALIAS_BOUND` by deleting the rows with the lowest rowids for that user.

`isSelf` senders are included — the bot's own identity is tracked the same as any other sender.

### Render-time current-identity resolution (`ContextBuilder`)

Before the context window is handed to the renderer, `ContextBuilder` runs a batch identity lookup over all events in `compactionInput` and `triggerEvents`:

1. `buildIdentityMap` collects distinct `(provider, sender.id)` pairs from the combined event list and calls `Storage.getUserIdentityMap`, which does a single parameterized `SELECT` scoped to those PKs (returning a `Map<"provider:userId", CurrentIdentity>`).
2. If the map is non-empty, `applyIdentityOverrides` shallow-copies each array, patching `event.sender` with `username: current.username ?? event.sender.username` and `displayName` from the stored value (a stored `null` becomes `undefined` to match `SenderInfo.displayName?: string`).
3. **If the map is empty** (which it always is for a Matrix-only database), neither array is touched — the output is byte-identical to what it would have been without this code. (The map lookup itself still runs: one empty-table PK SELECT per distinct sender, nanosecond-class; only the override pass is skipped.)

This means old events are always rendered under the sender's current name: if a user renamed from `alice` to `xoxo_alice`, messages stored under the old name surface as `xoxo_alice` in the LLM context.

### Retrieval alias expansion (`ContextBuilder.buildTriggerUsers`)

The user lane of the retrieval query (`src/context/builder.ts`, `buildTriggerUsers`) collects the set of names to match against the embedding/FTS index. For any trigger-window sender that has a `username`, the builder fetches up to 4 prior alias usernames via `Storage.getUserIdentityAliases(provider, userId, 4)` and adds them to the names set alongside the current `username`. This means a renamed user's older messages (indexed under a prior name) are still found. The maximum expansion is **current name + 4 aliases = 5 names per user**. Matrix senders (no `username`) contribute only their `displayName` to the names set, which is the pre-existing behaviour.

### Matrix zero-write guarantee

Matrix events never carry `sender.username` (the `SenderInfo` field is absent for the Matrix normalizer). The ingest hook gates on `sender.username` being truthy, so for a Matrix-only deployment:
- `upsertUserIdentity` is never called → both tables remain empty
- `getUserIdentityMap` returns an empty map → `buildIdentityMap` returns an empty map → `applyIdentityOverrides` is skipped entirely
- `getUserIdentityAliases` returns `[]` → no extra names are added to the retrieval query

The resulting context and retrieval output are byte-identical to what they would have been before this feature was added.

## 6c. Discord Provider

`src/discord/` implements the Discord gateway provider using discord.js v14. It splits into a pure normalizer module (`normalizer.ts`) and the live provider class (`provider.ts`).

### Normalizer (`src/discord/normalizer.ts`)

A pure-function module with no live discord.js Client references. The provider extracts plain data from discord.js objects and passes them here, making all normalization logic unit-testable without a gateway connection.

**Key shapes** (spec §4.1):

| Context | Timeline key |
|---|---|
| Guild text channel | `discord:<accountId>:room:<channelId>` |
| DM channel | `discord:<accountId>:dm:<channelId>` |
| Thread / forum post | `discord:<accountId>:room:<parentChannelId>:thread:<threadId>` |

Thread detection uses `THREAD_CHANNEL_TYPES = {10 (AnnouncementThread), 11 (PublicThread), 12 (PrivateThread), 15 (Forum)}`. Forum post-channels appear as type-15 parent channels with thread children; their posts are therefore routed as `room:<parentId>:thread:<postId>` — identical to ordinary thread routing.

Event id grammar: `discord:<accountId>:<messageSnowflake>`.

`buildDiscordTimelineKey(accountId, channelId, channelType, parentChannelId?)` and `buildDiscordEventId(accountId, messageId)` are the canonical factories; all other code imports them rather than constructing keys inline.

**Markup translation** — `translateDiscordMarkup(content, {users, roles, channels})`:

| Discord token | Rendered as |
|---|---|
| `<@id>` / `<@!id>` | `@username` / `@displayName` (nick form for `!`) |
| `<#id>` | `#channel-name` |
| `<@&id>` | `@role-name` |
| `<:name:id>` / `<a:name:id>` | `:name:` (static / animated custom emoji) |

Unresolvable IDs (not in the passed maps) pass through verbatim so no information is destroyed.

**Attachment normalisation**: `attachmentToMeta` maps `DiscordAttachmentData` to `AttachmentMeta`, inferring `mediaType` from MIME prefix and setting `asVoice`/`durationMs` when `isVoiceMessage=true` (Discord `IS_VOICE_MESSAGE` message flag 8192). Stickers are converted via `stickerToAttachment`, which maps the `StickerFormatType` enum (set by the provider in `extractMessageData`) to MIME type and filename extension: PNG (1) / APNG (2) → `image/png` / `.png`; GIF (4) → `image/gif` / `.gif`; Lottie (3) → `application/json` / `.json` with `mediaType: "file"` so the captioning worker (which only captions `image|video|audio`) skips it.

**Embed → link previews** — `embedsToLinkPreviews(embeds)`: only embeds with a URL are converted. The same trailing-punctuation strip used by `DirectLinkPreviewClient` (`.replace(/[.,;:!?)"'\]}>]+$/, "")`) is applied so embed URL exclusion matching works correctly at enrichment time. Result `sourceKind` is `"discord_embed"` so the enrichment worker can skip re-scraping those URLs.

**Trigger detection** — `detectDiscordTrigger(msg, ctx, isSelf)`:
- DM channel (type 1) → `{ type: "dm" }`
- Direct user mention of the bot snowflake → `{ type: "mention" }`
- Role mentions and @everyone/here do NOT trigger
- Own messages (`isSelf`) → `undefined`
- Reply-to-bot trigger is resolved separately by the provider via `host.resolveReplyTrigger` after storing the event

**Poll fallback**: when `msg.poll` is present and body content is empty, body is set to `[poll] <question> — <answer1>, <answer2>, ...`.

**Reply context**: `buildReplyContext(ref)` populates `replyTo` fully at ingest time from the `referenced_message` payload included in the gateway event. Reply enrichment via REST is not needed for Discord (unlike Matrix where the SDK doesn't include it).

**Emoji observations** — `extractEmojiObservations(content)`: captures `<:name:id>` and `<a:name:id>` pairs from the raw content string, deduplicated by id, and returned as `{name, id, animated}` triples. The provider calls `runtime.emojiCatalog.observeEmoji(obs.id, obs.name, obs.animated)` for each triple at ingest time.

**`DiscordNormalizeResult`** is the return type of `normalizeDiscordMessage`: `{ inbound: InboundChatEvent, emojiObservations, embedPreviews }`.

### Provider (`src/discord/provider.ts`)

`DiscordProvider` implements `IChatProvider`. One discord.js `Client` instance is created per configured account at `start()` time; reconnect and WebSocket resume are managed by discord.js.

**Capabilities**:

| Capability | Value | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `maxMessageChars` | 2000 | Discord API limit |
| `maxAttachmentsPerMessage` | 10 | Discord API limit |
| `formatting` | `"markdown"` | |
| `typing` | `true` | |
| `reactions` | `true` | `messageReactionAdd/Remove/RemoveAll/RemoveEmoji` gateway events routed to `host.onReaction`/`host.onBulkReactionClear` |
| `reactionKinds` | `["unicode","custom"]` | No text-shortcode reactions on Discord |
| `customEmojiScoped` | `true` | Guild-scoped sendability |
| `edits` | `true` | |
| `deletes` | `true` | |
| `threads` | `true` | |
| `pins` | `true` | |
| `pollCreate` | `true` | `DiscordChannelClient.createPoll` |
| `pollVote` | `false` | No bot vote endpoint in Discord API |
| `history` | `true` | `DiscordHistoryClient` returned by `history()` |
| `mediaUpload` | `false` | Local-file attach is the upload path; no Matrix-style MXC |
| `encrypted` | `false` | |
| `linkPreviews` | `"none"` | Embeds are handled at ingest, not via provider API |
| `singleAttachmentPerMessage` | `false` | Discord allows up to 10 per message |
| `membershipRoster` | computed | `true` iff any account has `member_intent = true` in config |
| `voiceMessages` | `true` | ogg/opus send via ffmpeg + 256-sample waveform; `as_voice` wired in `send()` |

**`accountIds()`** returns `Object.keys(this.config.accounts ?? {})` — config keys, not the runtime map — so it works correctly before `start()` is called (e.g. for enrichment pool registration).

**`ownsUserId(id)`**: `/^\d+$/.test(id)` — any all-digit string is treated as a Discord snowflake. Used by the budget predicate `isUserIdentity`.

**`channelClient(target)`**: returns a `DiscordChannelClient` when the target resolves to a running account. Resolves `guildId` from the discord.js channel cache (`runtime.client.channels.cache`), with a fallback guild-cache scan. Returns `undefined` for foreign targets (unknown accountId, channel not resolvable).

**`enrichment(accountId)`**: returns an `EnrichmentCapabilities` object when the account is running. `downloadMedia` throws (Discord attachments always have `remoteUrl`, so the enrichment worker never calls this path). `messageSummary` returns `null` (reply context is fully populated at ingest from `referenced_message`). `resolveLinkPreviews` is absent (triggers `DirectLinkPreviewClient` fallback in the worker — see §7a). `memberInfo` performs a live lookup against the guild member cache: iterates allowed guilds, calls `guild.members.cache.get(userId)`, and returns `nickname ?? globalName ?? username`; returns `{ displayName: undefined }` on cache miss.

**`history(target)`**: returns a `DiscordHistoryClient` for the resolved channel. Used by both `read_messages` (via `DiscordChannelClient.readMessages`, which delegates to the same client) and the initial-activation backfill (`runInitialBackfill` in `app.ts` calls `provider.history?.(target)` for non-Matrix providers).

**Gateway intents**: `Guilds`, `GuildMessages`, `DirectMessages`, `MessageContent`, `GuildMessageReactions`, `DirectMessageReactions`, `GuildExpressions`; plus `GuildMembers` iff any account has `member_intent = true`.

**Cache limits** (discord.js `makeCache`): `MessageManager / GuildMessageManager / DMMessageManager: {maxSize: 50}`, `GuildMemberManager: {maxSize: member_intent ? 200 : 0}`, `GuildEmojiManager: {maxSize: 500}`, `ThreadManager: {maxSize: 200}`. The timeline store is the source of truth for history; the client cache only needs what thread-parent resolution, member lookup, and emoji resolution require.

**Inbound pipeline** (spec §8):

1. `messageCreate` → `handleMessageCreate` → `extractMessageData` → `normalizeDiscordMessage` → `host.onEvent` (synchronously enqueues FIFO event insert) → optional `storeIngestEmbeds` callback (after `host.onEvent` so the `link_previews.event_id` FK is satisfied). On the immediate path (no trigger hold), FIFO enqueue order guarantees the event row is committed before the preview rows. On the held path, `embedPreviews` are carried inside the `PendingTrigger` structure and `storeIngestEmbeds` is called inside the hold flush callback **after** `host.onEvent`, preserving the same FK ordering.
2. Trigger-hold (spec §8.4): mirrors the Matrix provider's hold mechanism. `trigger_hold_ms` delays trigger dispatch; a second trigger in the same channel resets the timer (bounded at `4 × trigger_hold_ms` from the FIRST trigger's `holdStartedAt`). Implemented with `TRIGGER_HOLD_MAX_MULTIPLIER = 4`. Each held event's `embedPreviews` are stored paired with that event's id and are flushed only at timer expiry, never before `host.onEvent` fires.
3. Reply-to-bot: after trigger detection, if no trigger was found but the message is a reply, `host.resolveReplyTrigger` is called with the referenced message id; if it confirms the reply target is a bot message, the trigger is injected before `host.onEvent`.

**Reply context extraction** (`extractMessageData`): gated on `message.reference?.messageId` alone (`message.mentions.repliedUser` can be null on partial payloads and must not suppress context). The referenced message body is read from `message.channel.messages.cache.get(messageId)` — the channel message cache (discord.js v14 does not expose `.referencedMessage` as a field). On cache miss, a stub is built from `message.mentions.repliedUser` if available (provides author info but empty body and zero timestamp).

**Edit vs late-embed discrimination** (spec §8.3): `MESSAGE_UPDATE` fires for both user edits and Discord's async embed resolution. The discriminator is `message.editedTimestamp !== null`:
- `editedTimestamp !== null` → user edit: normalize and route as `InboundChatEvent` with `edit: { targetExternalId: messageId }`. Trigger is always cleared on edits.
- `editedTimestamp === null` → late-embed: call `callbacks.mergeLateEmbeds` with the new embed previews. The event is NOT routed to `host.onEvent` — doing so would re-trigger the agent (double-processing hazard).

**Deletes**: `messageDelete` constructs a synthetic tombstone `CanonicalChatEvent` with a `nanoid`-suffixed id and routes it via the same `edit: { targetExternalId }` marker that Matrix redactions use.

**Send** (`send(target, message)`):
- `@username` mention resolution (`resolveMentionTokens`): before chunking, the outbound body is scanned for `@username` tokens (word-boundary; `@everyone`/`@here` always skipped). Each unique token is resolved against the guild member cache first, then via `guild.members.search({ query, limit: 10 })` REST on cache miss. DMs resolve only the DM recipient. Resolved tokens are replaced with `<@id>` Discord mention syntax in the body, and the user ids are added to `allowed_mentions.users`. Unresolved tokens pass through unchanged as plain text.
- Body is chunked at 2000 characters using `chunkMarkdownText` (fence-aware) on the resolved body.
- `reply: { messageReference: target.replyToId, failIfNotExists: false }` on the first chunk.
- `allowed_mentions: { parse: [], users: [...resolvedIds], repliedUser: Boolean(replyRef) }` — `parse: []` prevents automatic @everyone/role pings; `users` carries only ids from `@username` resolution above; `repliedUser` controls whether the replied-to user receives a ping.
- Attachments (local-path files) are attached to the last chunk only (or the single chunk if there is only one).
- Returns a `DeliveryReceipt` with `externalId` pointing to the first sent message and `externalIds` listing all chunk message ids.

**Typing** (`setTyping`): calls `channel.sendTyping()` and re-schedules itself every `TYPING_REFRESH_MS = 8000 ms` (Discord clears the indicator after ~10 s). Cancelled cleanly on `setTyping(false)` or `stop()`.

**Guild allowlist**: per-account `guilds` config field (string array) restricts which guild ids the account listens to. DMs are allowed separately via `dm_enabled` (default `true`). The `isAllowed` filter applies on every inbound event before any processing.

### `DiscordProviderCallbacks`

Five callbacks are injected at `DiscordProvider` construction time (not via `IChatProvider.start`) because the storage layer is initialized before the providers map is built in `app.ts`:

- `mergeLateEmbeds(provider, externalId, timelineKey, previews)` — upserts late-embed link preview rows for an already-stored event. Called by `handleMessageUpdate` when `editedTimestamp === null`.
- `storeIngestEmbeds(eventId, previews)` — writes `discord_embed` link preview rows at ingest time. The `link_previews.event_id → timeline_events(id)` FK (enforced with `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON`) is satisfied on both ingest paths: on the **immediate path** (no trigger hold), `host.onEvent` synchronously enqueues the event write in the FIFO single-writer queue and `storeIngestEmbeds` then enqueues the preview writes behind it; on the **held path**, `embedPreviews` are carried inside `PendingTrigger` and `storeIngestEmbeds` is called inside the hold flush callback only after `host.onEvent` fires, so the event row is always written first.
- `upsertUserIdentity(input)` — called from `handleMessageCreate` (for every inbound message author) and from the `guildMemberUpdate` listener (when `member_intent = true`). The call is fire-and-forget (`void … .catch(() => {})`); failures are non-fatal.
- `setChannelMetadata(timelineKey, meta)` — called from `handleMessageCreate` to upsert `display_name`, `server_id`, and `server_name` into `room_metadata`. For guild channels, `display_name = "#channel-name (Guild Name)"`, `server_id = guild.id`, `server_name = guild.name`. For DMs, `server_id` and `server_name` are omitted. `resolved_at` is bumped only when `display_name`, `server_id`, or `server_name` actually change (SQL CASE change-guard), so the hot per-message path avoids spurious writes when nothing has changed. Also fire-and-forget.
- `onSelfResolved?(accountId, selfId)` — optional; called from the `ready` handler after login completes and `readyClient.user.id` is known. `app.ts` uses this to mutate the pre-declared `botSelfIdsForLimits` Set and `gapBackfetchSelfIds` Map (declared before the providers IIFE so mutations are visible to downstream consumers).

### Emoji catalog (`src/discord/emoji-catalog.ts`)

`EmojiCatalog` is a per-account in-memory store for Discord custom emoji. It maintains three maps:

- **`guildEmoji`**: `Map<guildId, Map<emojiId, CatalogEmojiInfo>>` — guild-scoped emoji. Populated at READY from every allowed guild's emoji list, updated on `guildEmojisUpdate` and `guildCreate`, cleared on `guildDelete`.
- **`appEmoji`**: `Map<emojiId, CatalogEmojiInfo>` — application emoji (sendable in any guild). Populated at READY via `GET /applications/{id}/emojis` iff `application_id` is configured.
- **`observedPairs`**: `Map<emojiId, CatalogEmojiInfo>` — emoji observed inline in incoming message content (`extractEmojiObservations`). NOT sendable; not returned by `getSendableEmoji`. Exists so the normalizer can render `:name:` for emoji not yet in the catalog.

**`getSendableEmoji(guildId?)`**: returns guild emoji (for the given guild, alphabetically) followed by app emoji (alphabetically). Observed pairs are excluded.

**`resolve(token, guildId?)`**: parses the token as either a raw Unicode emoji glyph or a `:shortcode:` string. Unicode glyphs pass through directly (`kind: "unicode"`). `:shortcode:` is matched first against the target guild's emoji (priority), then against app emoji; returns `{ kind: "custom", id, name, animated }` on match, `null` on miss. Observed pairs are not resolvable (not sendable).

**`nearMatches(query, guildId?)`**: substring search over sendable emoji names; returns up to 5 `:shortcode:` strings.

**Static helpers**: `EmojiCatalog.formatForApi(id, name)` → `"name:id"` (Discord API reaction emoji format); `EmojiCatalog.normalizedKey(id)` → `"discord:<id>"` (normalized key stored in the reactions table).

**Gateway lifecycle**: the `guildEmojisUpdate` listener signature is `(guild, _oldEmojis, newEmojis)` per discord.js v14 (the guild is always the first argument). `guildCreate` loads emoji for newly joined guilds. `guildDelete` calls `clearGuildEmoji` so stale entries don't persist.

### Reaction routing

Four gateway events map to `host.onReaction` and `host.onBulkReactionClear`:

| Gateway event | Handler | Action |
|---|---|---|
| `messageReactionAdd` | `handleReactionAdd` | `host.onReaction` with `action: "add"` |
| `messageReactionRemove` | `handleReactionRemove` | `host.onReaction` with `action: "remove"` |
| `messageReactionRemoveAll` | inline | `host.onBulkReactionClear` with no `normalizedKey` |
| `messageReactionRemoveEmoji` | inline | `host.onBulkReactionClear` with `normalizedKey` |

All four are gated on `isAllowedByGuild(runtime, guildId)` before dispatch.

**`isAllowedByGuild(runtime, guildId)`**: returns `true` if `runtime.allowedGuilds` is unset (allow all), the `guildId` is in `allowedGuilds`, or `guildId` is `null` (DM — allowed when `dmEnabled`).

**Reaction primary key**: `discord:<messageId>:<normalizedKey>:<userId>`, where `normalizedKey` for custom emoji is `discord:<emojiId>` and for unicode is the emoji glyph string. This PK is deterministic: the same add and remove events for the same (message, emoji, user) tuple produce the same key, so the remove tombstones the add row in the reactions table.

**`handleReactionAdd`** constructs the PK, builds the `timelineKey` from `reaction.message.channelId` + `channelType` + optional `parentId` (for threads), and calls `host.onReaction` with `action: "add"`, `kind` (`"unicode"` or `"custom"`), `display` (glyph or `:name:`), `shortcode` (for custom), `normalizedKey`, `targetEventId`, and `reactedAtMs`.

**`handleReactionRemove`** reconstructs the same PK (using the same `buildReactionNormalizedKey` helper) and calls `host.onReaction` with `action: "remove"` and no `kind`/`display`/`targetEventId` (tombstone shape).

### DiscordChannelClient (`src/discord/channel-client.ts`)

`DiscordChannelClient` implements the `ChannelClient` interface using discord.js REST through the account's `Client.rest`.

| Method | Notes |
|---|---|
| `react(externalId, emoji)` | `EmojiCatalog.resolve()` then `PUT /channels/{id}/messages/{msgId}/reactions/{emoji}/@me`. Unicode glyphs are URL-encoded; custom emoji use `name:id` format. |
| `unreact(externalId, emoji)` | `DELETE /channels/{id}/messages/{msgId}/reactions/{emoji}/@me` |
| `listReactions(externalId, limit?)` | Fetches the message to get the reaction array; then for each emoji fetches users via `GET /reactions/{emoji}`. Returns `ReactionListing` with per-emoji `{ display, users }` entries. |
| `editMessage(externalId, body)` | `PATCH /channels/{id}/messages/{msgId}` |
| `deleteMessage(externalId)` | `DELETE /channels/{id}/messages/{msgId}` |
| `readMessages(req)` | Delegates to an internal `DiscordHistoryClient` instance |
| `readMessage(externalId)` | `GET /channels/{id}/messages/{msgId}` → `HistorySummary` |
| `memberInfo(userId)` | Guild member cache lookup; REST `GET /guilds/{id}/members/{userId}` on miss (only if `memberIntentEnabled`) |
| `members()` | REST `GET /guilds/{id}/members` with pagination (`limit=1000`, `after` cursor); only present iff `memberIntentEnabled` |
| `channelInfo()` | Cache hit → `channelInfoFromCached`; cache miss → `GET /channels/{id}`, result cached. Returns `ChannelInfo` with `id`, `displayName`, `isDirect: false` (always false for guild channels), `topic`, `threadCount` |
| `pins()` | `GET /channels/{id}/pins` |
| `pinMessage / unpinMessage` | `PUT / DELETE /channels/{id}/pins/{msgId}` |
| `emojiList(limit?)` | `EmojiCatalog.getSendableEmoji(guildId)` → up to `limit` entries as `EmojiEntry[]` |
| `createPoll(req)` | `POST /channels/{id}/messages` with `poll: { question, answers, duration, allow_multiselect }` Discord object |

`URLSearchParams` objects are used for all REST `query` parameters (discord.js v14 requires `URLSearchParams`, not a plain object).

### DiscordHistoryClient (`src/discord/history-client.ts`)

`DiscordHistoryClient` implements both `HistoryClient` and `BackfillReadClient`. It uses before-snowflake backward pagination via `GET /channels/{id}/messages`.

**`readMessages(req)`**: accepts `req.limit` (1–100, clamped), `req.before` (explicit snowflake), and `req.cursor` (previous page cursor — also a snowflake). `before` takes priority over `cursor`. Response messages arrive newest-first; the oldest snowflake becomes `nextCursor`. Returns `HistoryPageResult` with `messages` (as `HistorySummary[]`) and `nextCursor`.

**Structural compatibility**: `DiscordHistoryClient` satisfies `BackfillReadClient` because `readMessages` shares the same signature as `BackfillReadClient.readMessages`. This lets `runInitialBackfill` in `app.ts` use the history client directly without a wrapper (non-Matrix providers use `provider.history?.(target)` as the read client; Matrix uses `makeBackfillReadClient`).

### Voice message send (`src/discord/voice-message.ts`)

When the outbound message has an attachment with `asVoice = true` and a `localPath`, `send()` enters the voice message path.

**`encodeVoiceMessage(inputPath)`** (two ffmpeg passes):
1. Pass 1 — `ffmpeg -i input -vn -c:a libopus output.ogg` (transcode to ogg/opus)
2. Pass 2 — `ffmpeg -i input -vn -ar 48000 -ac 1 -f s16le pcmfile` (extract s16le PCM for waveform)

**`computeWaveform(pcmBytes, targetSamples)`**: divides the s16le PCM into `min(targetSamples, totalSamples, 256)` chunks, computes the peak absolute amplitude of each chunk (interpreting 16-bit LE signed samples), normalizes all peaks relative to the global maximum (0 → 0, max → 255), and returns a `Buffer` of `Uint8` values. `WAVEFORM_SAMPLE_COUNT = 256` is the Discord-enforced maximum.

The provider sends the ogg file as a multipart POST using the raw REST client (discord.js attachment builder does not expose `duration_secs`/`waveform`). The payload sets `flags: MessageFlags.IsVoiceMessage` and includes `duration_secs` and `waveform` (base64-encoded waveform buffer) on the attachment object. After send, `cleanupVoiceFile` removes the temp ogg file.

### `set_profile` (`setProfile` method)

`setProfile(accountId, opts)` implements the `IChatProvider.setProfile` contract for Discord:

- **Avatar**: `PATCH /users/@me` with `avatar` as a `data:<contentType>;base64,<data>` URI. Returns the updated CDN URL.
- **Display name (per-guild nick)**: `PATCH /guilds/{guildId}/members/@me` with `{ nick }` for each allowed guild the account is in. Empty string clears the nick. Non-fatal per-guild (some guilds may block nick changes). Global username rename is deliberately excluded (globally unique, rate-limited at ~2/hr, collision risk).

### Identity and metadata upserts

**`upsertUserIdentity`** is called from two sites:

1. `handleMessageCreate` — after every inbound message, calls `callbacks.upsertUserIdentity({ provider: "discord", userId: author.id, username: author.username, displayName: <nick or globalName or username>, observedAt: message.createdTimestamp })`. The `displayName` is the most-resolved visible name: nick (from guild member cache) → `globalName` → `username`.
2. `guildMemberUpdate` listener (gated on `memberIntentEnabled`) — calls `callbacks.upsertUserIdentity` when a member's display name or username changes, using the updated `GuildMember` data.

**`setChannelMetadata`** is called from `handleMessageCreate` with `{ displayName, serverId?, serverName? }`. The `displayName` is built by `buildChannelDisplayName(message)`: `#channel-name (Guild Name)` for guild text channels, `#<channelId>` for unknown/DM channels. `serverId = message.guildId ?? undefined`; `serverName = message.guild?.name ?? undefined`. The `room_metadata` table (`server_id`, `server_name` columns — schema v6) stores the guild scope so `serverIdsFor(timelineKey)` in `app.ts` can return the Discord guild id for limits enforcement.

### Self-id disposition

Discord self-ids (the bot's own snowflake) are only known after the `ready` event fires asynchronously. To make them available to the `UserLimitEngine` and backfill coordinator at call time (not just at construction time):

- `app.ts` declares a mutable `botSelfIdsForLimits: Set<string>` (pre-seeded with Matrix `user_id` values) and `gapBackfetchSelfIds: Map<string, string>` (pre-seeded with Matrix account self-ids) **before** the providers IIFE.
- The `onSelfResolved(accountId, selfId)` callback mutates both containers: `botSelfIdsForLimits.add(selfId)` and `gapBackfetchSelfIds.set(accountId, selfId)`.
- `UserLimitEngine` is constructed with `selfUserIds: botSelfIdsForLimits`; it reads the Set by reference at each `isUserIdentity` call, so READY-time additions are visible without restart.
- `runInitialBackfill` reads `gapBackfetchSelfIds.get(accountId)` at backfill time; READY fires before the first `activating` transition, so the self-id is always available when needed.

## 7. Timeline

### Core concept

The timeline is a continuously-growing, append-only record of everything that happens in a chat context. It exists independently of agent sessions. It is the single source of truth for all conversation history.

This architecture explicitly separates **observing** (timeline ingests all messages continuously) from **participating** (agent sessions are spawned episodically by triggers).

### Timeline keys

`timelineKey` is a provider-generic string used as the universal FK across ~20 tables. Its shape is a documented, parseable grammar (`src/storage/timeline-key.ts`):

```
<provider>:<accountId>:<kind>:<channelId>[:thread:<threadId>]
  provider   [a-z0-9-]+      no colon (e.g. "matrix")
  accountId  operator-chosen config key, no colon
  kind       "room" | "dm"
  channelId  provider-native id; MAY contain colons (Matrix room ids do)
  threadId   provider-native id (no colons in practice)
```

Matrix keys are exactly this shape — no existing row needs to change:

| Context | Format |
|---------|--------|
| Room | `matrix:{accountId}:room:{roomId}` |
| DM | `matrix:{accountId}:dm:{roomId}` |
| Thread | `matrix:{accountId}:room:{roomId}:thread:{threadRootId}` |

Because `channelId` may contain colons (Matrix room ids are `!local:server`), the `:thread:` suffix is detected from the **end** (last occurrence of `:thread:` in the remainder after the `kind:` segment). Kind is always the 3rd colon-delimited segment from the start.

**Shared parser** — `src/storage/timeline-key.ts` is the single grammar module and single source of truth. Public API:
- `parseTimelineKey(key)` → `ParsedTimelineKey | undefined` (returns `undefined` for malformed/absent keys)
- `buildTimelineKey(parts)` → `string` (inverse of `parseTimelineKey`; round-trips are identity)
- `channelIdFromTimelineKey(key)` → `string | undefined` (extracts the channel id; `undefined` for malformed/absent)
- `timelineKindOf(key)` → `"room" | "dm" | undefined`
- `roomIdFromTimelineKeyOpt` — deprecated alias of `channelIdFromTimelineKey`; kept for one release
- `threadKeyLikePattern(roomKey)` — SQLite `LIKE` pattern for `<roomKey>:thread:%`

All parse sites use these shared functions. No subsystem may parse the key with a local regex or `split(":")`. A present-but-malformed key logs `"timeline_key.malformed"` (structured JSON, consistent event name) rather than silently no-op-ing; absent/undefined keys keep their current behaviour (skip / return undefined).

DM detection uses `timelineKindOf(key) === "dm"`. The `isDmTimeline` router helper delegates to the same function. For inbound events produced by a normalizer, `InboundChatEvent.channelType` (`"group" | "dm" | "thread"`) is populated at ingest; routing code that has the full event prefers `channelType` and falls back to `timelineKindOf(key)` for stored keys.

### Storage

SQLite with WAL mode. Core tables:

- **`timeline_events`** — the event log. Each row stores denormalized columns (id, timeline_key, role, sender, body, timestamps) plus `event_json` (full `CanonicalChatEvent` serialized), `enrichment_status` (`inactive` | `pending` | `processing` | `complete` | `failed` | `skipped`), and `trigger_group_id` (event ID of the trigger that owns this group, or NULL). The `inactive` status marks events stored for a not-yet-triggered timeline (see section 7b — Channel Lifecycle); the enrichment and caption pools only claim `pending`/`processing` rows, so `inactive` events are stored cheaply but never processed until activation. It also has a generated column `is_undecryptable` (VIRTUAL, derived from `event_json`'s `undecryptable` field) for cheap UTD-row lookup by the re-decryption sweeper (see section 6a) and a `redecrypt_attempts` integer (default 0) that counts failed re-decryption probes — the sweeper's candidate query excludes rows at/above `MAX_REDECRYPT_ATTEMPTS` (and a `REDECRYPT_RETIRED` sentinel for permanently-unfetchable rows) so dead UTD rows leave rotation. A nullable `last_edit_timestamp` (issue #3) holds the `origin_server_ts` of the most recently applied edit (NULL = never edited); the edit-application paths use it to enforce latest-by-timestamp wins (an older incoming edit is a no-op). Indexed on `(timeline_key, timestamp, received_at, id)`, `(provider, external_id)`, `(enrichment_status, timestamp DESC)`, `(trigger_group_id)`, and a partial `(is_undecryptable, redecrypt_attempts, timestamp) where is_undecryptable = 1` (the attempts column is in the index so the give-up filter skips exhausted rows without touching the heap).
- **`timeline_compaction_state`** — per-timeline compaction cursors (`compact_start_event_id`, `rich_start_event_id`) plus channel-lifecycle columns: `timeline_state` (`inactive` | `activating` | `active` | `backfilling`, default `inactive`) and `backfill_fence_timestamp` (reserved for deferred operator backfill). A missing row means the timeline is implicitly `inactive`. See section 7b.
- **`pending_edits`** — edits (`m.replace`) that arrived/decrypted before their target message was stored, parked for replay by the append path (see section 6, "Edit-before-target replay"). Keyed by `(provider, target_external_id, timeline_key)` (the PK; `timeline_key`-scoped for the same multi-account reason as the edit lookup). Stores the resolved replacement `body` + `attachments_json` and the `edit_timestamp` (origin time, for latest-wins). Rows are deleted as soon as the target lands and the edit is applied.
- **`room_metadata`** — cached human room labels keyed by `timeline_key` (`display_name` + `resolved_at`). Populated by `RoomLabelCache` (see section 6, "Room-label resolution") so the observability console room list shows real room names instead of raw room ids; read by `listConsoleRooms`, which falls back to the `timeline_key` when no label has been resolved yet. `resolved_at` lets the cache expire stale labels (rooms can be renamed; 6-hour TTL). Also carries `server_id` (nullable) and `server_name` (nullable) — Discord guild scope, populated by `setChannelMetadata` at ingest; used by `serverIdsFor(timelineKey)` in `app.ts` to return the Discord guild snowflake for server-scoped budget limits.
- **`metadata`** — generic key-value store.
- **`reactions`** — the passive reaction store (§9f). One row per reaction event (PK = the reaction's own event id — `$eventId` for Matrix; `discord:<msgId>:<emojiKey>:<userId>` synthetic key for Discord), carrying the `timeline_key` locality hint, the target message's external id, sender, resolved `kind`/`display`/`shortcode`/`normalized_key`, timestamps, and a nullable `redacted_at` tombstone. The `timeline_key` column is returned in `DiscreteReactionRow` so the context builder can derive the provider for identity resolution (§6.5). Deliberately **not** part of the timeline — reactions are injected only at render time and must stay out of summarization, search, diary, and recap. No FK to `timeline_events`: the target is matched by the globally-unique event id (Matrix or synthetic Discord PK), not the internal row id. Partial index on `(target_event_id, reacted_at) where redacted_at is null`.
- **`user_identities`** / **`user_identity_aliases`** — the user identity store (see §6b). `user_identities` is `WITHOUT ROWID`, PK `(provider, user_id)`, current `username` + `display_name`. `user_identity_aliases` holds prior usernames, bounded at `USER_IDENTITY_ALIAS_BOUND = 16` per user.

Enrichment tables (see section 7a):

- **`reply_contexts`** — resolved reply snapshots, one per event (keyed by the replying event's ID)
- **`media_assets`** — all downloaded media: attachments, reply attachments, link preview media, linked media in body text. Tracks download status, caption status, detected content (character cards), and workspace-relative file paths.
- **`link_previews`** — fetched link preview metadata (title, description, site name, source kind), plus the nullable `payload_json` structured payload for `source_kind = 'fx_twitter'` rows (§7a "X.com enrichment via FxTwitter"). Media associated with previews lives in `media_assets` with a `link_preview_id` FK.

### Single-writer invariant

**All writes go through a single-writer microtask queue.** No component writes to SQLite directly. Write jobs are pushed onto an array and drained atomically via `queueMicrotask()`. This eliminates contention and `SQLITE_BUSY` errors entirely. Reads execute synchronously outside the queue (WAL allows concurrent readers). Composite operations use `readAndWrite()` which wraps the callback in a transaction and submits through the write queue.

### Schema versioning & migrations

The complete schema is defined as a single canonical block (`SCHEMA` in `src/storage/database.ts`) — clean, fully-idempotent `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` / `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` statements with every constraint baked in (the `enrichment_status` CHECK including `'inactive'`, the `is_undecryptable` generated column and its partial index, `redecrypt_attempts`, `last_edit_timestamp`, the `timeline_state` / `backfill_fence_timestamp` lifecycle columns, `enrichment_retries`, `trigger_group_id`, `media_assets.caption_error`, the `summaries.diary_status`/`diary_attempts` diary-queue columns, the `media_assets.caption_attempts`/`updated_at` pipeline-monitor columns, the `agent_sessions.resume_generation`/`chat_upper_bound_ts` reply-resume columns, the `usage_events` ledger with its `logical_model_id`/per-user columns and the `usage_event_partitions` overflow-membership child table, the `user_identities`/`user_identity_aliases` identity store, etc.). It is assembled from the shared sub-schema constants (`RETRIEVAL_SCHEMA`, `CHAT_SEARCH_SCHEMA`, `SUMMARY_SEARCH_SCHEMA`, `REACTIONS_SCHEMA`, `AGENT_SESSIONS_FTS_SCHEMA`, `SESSION_INTERJECTIONS_SCHEMA`, `USAGE_EVENTS_SCHEMA`, `TOOL_INVOCATIONS_SCHEMA`, `BACKFETCH_JOBS_SCHEMA`, `USER_IDENTITIES_SCHEMA`). `SCHEMA` always builds a fresh DB directly at the final shape.

Structurally the schema is still the **genesis (v1) shape**: `SCHEMA` is edited in place (it stays idempotent so re-running it on an existing DB only fills in newly-added tables/indexes via `if not exists`), and no DDL migration exists. `LATEST_SCHEMA_VERSION = 6`, with **data-only or purely-additive** `MIGRATIONS` steps (`MIGRATIONS[v]` upgrades a v→v+1 DB): **v1→v2** (`cleanupAssistantEchoDuplicates`) is the one-off cleanup of the historical assistant-echo duplicates — for every `matrix:{account}:{eventId}` row that duplicates an `assistant:{session}:{eventId}:{chunk}` sibling with the same `(provider, external_id, timeline_key)`, it remaps references onto the assistant row (`summary_events` lineage — dropped instead when the summary already lists the sibling —, `summaries.latest_event_id`, `summarization_jobs.input_start_id`/`input_end_id`, the `timeline_compaction_state` cursors), deletes the duplicate's enrichment artifacts (`media_assets`/`link_previews`/`reply_contexts`), and deletes the duplicate row itself (its `chat_index` projection cascade-deletes via the FK + FTS triggers). See "Assistant echo enrichment" and §7c for the fixes that stop new duplicates from forming. **v2→v3** (`repairReplyFallbackOverstrip`) is the one-off repair for reply bodies over-stripped by the native reply-fallback stripper before its shape gating existed (§6 "Rich-reply fallback stripping"): for every reply (`event_json.replyTo` set) whose stored `htmlBody` begins with a `<blockquote>` (the user's own quote, surviving because `<mx-reply>` stripping left it), it rebuilds the plain body — the blockquote text as `> `-prefixed lines, a blank line, then the surviving remainder — updating both the `body` column and `event_json.body`. Rows whose body still carries the quote (fallback-sending clients, compared on lowercase-alphanumeric-folded words so markdown/HTML rendering differences don't mislead) and quotes that nest another blockquote are left alone. `reply_contexts` rows quoting a repaired event get the repaired text when their stored body exactly equals the damaged one; `chat_index` self-heals via the startup `reconcileAll` sweep (`content_sig` changes); summaries generated from damaged renders are LLM output and are not recomputed. **v3→v4** (`addUsageEventPartitions`) adds the `usage_event_partitions` overflow-membership child table (see §8e). **v4→v5** (`addUserIdentityTables`) adds the `user_identities` and `user_identity_aliases` tables (purely additive DDL, no data backfill — see §6b). **v5→v6** (`addRoomMetadataServerColumns`) adds `server_id TEXT` and `server_name TEXT` nullable columns to `room_metadata` for Discord guild scope (see §6c). The migration uses `PRAGMA table_info(room_metadata)` to check column existence before each `ALTER TABLE` — SQLite 3.45 does not support `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`, and tests simulate downgrades on a physically-v6 database, so idempotency is required. A future column/table rename or data transform that `create … if not exists` cannot express follows the same pattern: bump `LATEST_SCHEMA_VERSION` and append an ordered step.

Versioning still uses SQLite's `PRAGMA user_version`. Inside `open()`'s write callback, the runner first records whether the database is **fresh** (no `timeline_events` table yet) *before* any DDL runs (`SCHEMA` uses `if not exists`, so afterward a fresh and an existing DB look identical):

- A **fresh** DB runs `SCHEMA` *first* (builds every table/index at the latest shape — which contains no duplicates by construction, so the data-only step is irrelevant to it), then `runMigrations` only stamps `user_version = LATEST_SCHEMA_VERSION`. Both run inside a **single `db.transaction()`** so a crash mid-build is all-or-nothing — a partial set of tables can never be left behind to be misclassified as fresh on the next open (issue #7).
- An **existing** DB runs `runMigrations` *first* (applies any pending `MIGRATIONS` steps in one transaction), then `writer.exec(SCHEMA)` reconciles it to the canonical shape, creating via `if not exists` any table/index it lacks. The order (migrations before `SCHEMA`) means any future legacy-shape ALTER lands before `SCHEMA`'s latest-shape DDL.
- In all cases, `runMigrations` reads `user_version`: a version *newer* than the build supports (`> LATEST_SCHEMA_VERSION`) is a **hard error** — it refuses to open, to avoid corrupting forward-versioned data; at the latest version it is a no-op (re-opening is idempotent); below the latest version it applies each `MIGRATIONS` step from the current version up to the latest, then stamps `user_version`.

The `sqlite-vec` `memory_vec` virtual table (and its `memory_vec_*` shadow tables) are **not** part of `SCHEMA`: they are created at runtime by `VectorStore.ensure()` (`src/retrieval/vector-store.ts`) at the embedding model's dimension, so a freshly-opened DB that has not yet embedded anything will not contain them. This is orthogonal to the schema-version machinery.

### Event lifecycle

1. **Append** — upsert via `ON CONFLICT(id) DO UPDATE`, preserving original `created_at`
2. **Dedup** — `appendIfMissing()` checks existence by canonical `id` first, then by `(provider, external_id, timeline_key)`, and returns `{ event, duplicate }`. The external-id check is what dedups a **bot self-message** re-encountered by backfill/backfetch: send_message stores it under an `assistant:{session}:{eventId}:{chunk}` canonical id while re-fetched history re-keys the same Matrix event `matrix:{account}:{eventId}`, so an id-only check would insert a duplicate row. Scoping by `timeline_key` keeps two accounts sharing a room (same Matrix event id, different timeline keys) from deduping against each other
3. **Enrich** — `updateTimelineEvent()` does read-modify-write on `event_json` (for caption results, echo metadata)
4. **Echo resolution** — `ingestAssistantEcho()` finds existing assistant events by `external_id` or fuzzy body match (±5min window, max 100 candidates), then enriches or appends; `ingestAssistantSend()` is the send-side mirror (see "Assistant echo enrichment")

### Routing

`TimelineRouter` directs inbound events to the correct timeline by `timelineKey`, deduplicating and appending in a single write-queue transaction.

### Trigger group resolution

When a trigger fires, the hold-grouped event IDs and a lookback query are unified into a single **trigger group**. The lookback finds the most recent same-sender message with a media attachment within `trigger_group_lookback_ms` (default 20s) of the trigger timestamp, then includes all same-sender messages between that attachment message and the trigger — not just the single attachment message. All events in the group get their `trigger_group_id` column updated in one DB write, making their media assets visible to caption workers. `resolveTriggerGroup` is gated on `ProviderCapabilities.singleAttachmentPerMessage`; providers that allow multiple attachments per message (e.g. Discord) return early without a lookback, since each user message already carries all its attachments together.

### Trigger coordination

`TriggerCoordinator` enforces per-timeline concurrency:

- Per-timeline active session count tracked in `activeByTimeline`
- Limit is `max_concurrent_dm` for DM timelines, `max_concurrent` for group timelines
- Under limit → `"spawn"`, at limit → `"queued"` (up to `max_queued_per_timeline`), queue full → `"ignored"`
- On session completion, the next queued trigger is dequeued and launched
- No TTL-based expiry; backpressure via configurable cap and logging
- No global concurrency limit — only per-timeline

---

## 7a. Enrichment Pipeline

Enrichment is a post-persistence, provider-agnostic system that processes timeline events after they are stored. It replaces the earlier approach where enrichment happened inside the Matrix layer before persistence.

### Design principles

1. **Persist first, enrich later.** Events are stored immediately with bare metadata. If enrichment fails or the app crashes, the message is never lost.
2. **Provider-agnostic.** Enrichment workers use an `EnrichmentCapabilities` interface provided by the chat provider, not provider-specific code. Adding a new provider means implementing this interface.
3. **No inference in enrichment.** Enrichment workers handle I/O (downloads, fetches, resolution). Captioning (inference) is a separate worker pool with its own concurrency limits.
4. **Atomic results.** All enrichment data for one event is written in a single DB transaction — reply context, link previews, and media assets are either all present or all absent.

### Enrichment worker pool (`src/enrichment/`)

A pool of workers (configurable count, default 3) continuously polls the DB for events with `enrichment_status='pending'`, most recent first. Each worker claims an event (CAS to `'processing'`), then executes all enrichment for that message. The channel id for channel-bound capability calls is extracted from the timeline key by the shared `channelIdFromTimelineKey` (`src/storage/timeline-key.ts`) — **never** a naive `split(":")`, which truncates classic `!local:server` room ids at the server colon and makes every room-bound call fail on an unknown room. If the key is present but doesn't parse, the worker logs `timeline_key.malformed` and skips the channel-bound steps (1, 2) while still running the body-only steps.

1. **Download attachments** — per-attachment routing based on what each attachment needs: if the attachment has a `remoteUrl` (a pre-signed external CDN URL, e.g. from Discord), it is streamed to disk via `FetchClient.downloadUrl()`; if there is no `remoteUrl` but a channel id is available, the provider `downloadMedia()` RPC is called (Matrix path, streams directly to disk); if neither is present (malformed timeline key), the attachment is silently skipped and no row is created. Downloaded files are moved to `msg-attach/` with content-addressed filenames (SHA-256 hash, base32 prefix, 13 chars); move uses `rename()` with EXDEV fallback (`copyFile` + `unlink`) for cross-filesystem scenarios.
2. **Resolve reply context** via provider `messageSummary()` + `memberInfo()` → store sender, body, timestamp. `messageSummary` returns a **provider-neutral** shape: `{ eventId, sender, body, timestamp, attachments? }` where `attachments` is an array of `{ mediaType, filename?, mimeType?, remoteUrl? }` objects (no `msgtype` — provider-specific vocabulary is translated by the provider's `messageSummary` implementation before returning). When attachments are present, **all** are downloaded using the same per-attachment routing as step 1, creating `reply_attachment` rows for each index. A missing target (redacted/non-message — `messageSummary` returns null) or a thrown resolution failure degrades to a **stub row** (`reply_external_id` only) and is always logged (`enrichment_reply_target_missing` / `enrichment_reply_resolution_failed`) — never swallowed silently; the renderer marks a stub as unavailable (§9).
3. **Fetch link previews** for message body — a partition step: X status URLs go to the FxTwitter stage (see "X.com enrichment via FxTwitter" below); remaining URLs go to the link preview stage. `resolveLinkPreviews` is **optional** on `EnrichmentCapabilities`: when present (Matrix path), it calls the provider's Synapse `preview_url` endpoint; when absent, `DirectLinkPreviewClient` (`src/enrichment/link-preview-client.ts`) scrapes `og:`/`twitter:` meta tags from the URLs directly via `FetchClient` (plain HTML fetch, no JS rendering, entity-decoded), using `twitter:` as a fallback when `og:` tags are absent, and produces rows with `source_kind = "direct_scrape"`. Before scraping, the worker queries `getIngestLinkPreviewUrls(eventId)` to find URLs already covered by `source_kind = "discord_embed"` rows (ingest-time Discord embed previews) and passes them as `excludeUrls` so they are not re-fetched or overwritten. `persistEnrichmentResults` preserves `source_kind = "discord_embed"` rows when it deletes stale link_previews (the DELETE excludes rows where `source_kind = 'discord_embed'`), so ingest-time Discord previews are never clobbered by worker-time scraping. `enrichment.max_previews_per_message` is one shared cap across all kinds, allocated by order of first appearance in the body.
4. **Fetch link previews** for reply body (if reply was resolved) — identical partition and fallback treatment
5. **Extract and download linked media** from message body (URLs ending in media extensions)
6. **Extract and download linked media** from reply body
7. **Detect character cards** on all image assets (reads PNG tEXt chunks, no inference)
8. **Set caption eligibility** — images with successful downloads get `caption_status='pending'`, others get `'skipped'`

Steps within a job run concurrently where possible (`Promise.all`), with reply-dependent steps (4, 6) awaiting reply resolution (2). Individual download/fetch failures do not fail the whole job — each asset tracks its own status. The job fails only on unexpected errors (DB failures).

On completion, all results are written atomically (link previews first due to FK constraints, then media assets) and `enrichment_status` flips to `'complete'`. Failed jobs are retried up to `max_retries` via the durable `timeline_events.enrichment_retries` column (incremented on each failure), so retry state survives a restart and feeds the pipeline monitor's counts (§11). On startup, stale `'processing'` claims are reset to `'pending'`.

### Media asset roles

The `media_assets` table tracks every downloaded file with a `role` distinguishing its origin:

| Role | Meaning |
|------|---------|
| `attachment` | Direct attachment on the message |
| `reply_attachment` | Attachment on the replied-to message |
| `preview_media` | Media from a link preview (OG image, tweet photos) |
| `reply_preview_media` | Media from a link preview on the replied-to message |
| `linked_media` | Media URL found directly in message body text |
| `reply_linked_media` | Same, from the replied-to message body |

All media is saved as the exact original file — processing (resize, re-encode, truncate) is always ephemeral and handled by `src/media/` at inference or context-build time.

### Caption worker pool (`src/captioning/`)

A separate pool of workers (configurable count, default 2) continuously polls the DB for media assets eligible for captioning. An asset is eligible when:

1. `caption_status = 'pending'` (set by enrichment)
2. `download_status = 'complete'` (file is on disk)
3. `media_type = 'image'`
4. The event is in a trigger group, **or** `caption_all` is true, **or** the event's role is `assistant` and `caption_assistant_messages` is true

Trigger-group media is prioritized over non-trigger-group media. The `caption_assistant_messages` flag enables captioning of **all** assistant-sent media regardless of trigger group membership — this is intentionally asymmetric with user-sent media (which require a trigger group unless `caption_all` is set). Each worker claims an asset, determines its effective modality (image/video/audio), and routes to the corresponding per-modality `InferenceClient`. The inference client calls the shared `src/media/` processing pipeline before sending to the API: images are scaled to the pixel budget and iteratively JPEG-compressed (`processImageForInference`), videos are re-encoded to target resolution/duration/size (`processVideoForInference` with optional GPU acceleration and a SHA-256-keyed file cache), and audio is re-encoded and truncated (`processAudioForInference`). For animated images (GIF, animated WebP/AVIF — detected via `sharp.metadata().pages > 1`), the worker converts to MP4 via fluent-ffmpeg and processes as video, falling back to first-frame image captioning if ffmpeg is unavailable or video captioning fails. The shared default captioning model is configured via `captioning.model` (id, endpoint, api_key) with per-modality overrides available under `captioning.image.model`, `captioning.video.model`, `captioning.audio.model`. Each modality has its own prompt and max_chars; in-flight caption-inference concurrency is governed by the model's LLM rate-limit group (`max_in_flight`, §8a), not by a per-modality knob. The captioning API uses direct OpenRouter-compatible fetch calls with modality-specific content blocks (`image_url`, `video_url`, `input_audio`). Media whose duration exceeds `media.video.max_duration_seconds` or `media.audio.max_duration_seconds` is truncated; for tool-initiated captions a warning is prepended indicating the processed range and suggesting `start_time` for other segments. Failed captions are retried up to `max_retries` via the **durable** `media_assets.caption_attempts` counter — incremented inside the claim CAS (mirroring `enrichment_retries`), so retry state survives a restart and is visible to the pipeline monitor's DB-derived counts (§11 "Pipeline monitor"); every caption write also bumps `media_assets.updated_at`, the monitor's reverse-chron sort key. On startup, stale `'processing'` claims are reset to `'pending'`.

**Usage/cost capture (§8c).** `describeMedia` reads the OpenAI/OpenRouter `usage` block (`parseOpenAiUsage`: `prompt_tokens − cached_tokens → input`, `cached_tokens → cacheRead`, `completion_tokens → output`; null when the gateway omits it). The `InferenceClient` computes USD cost from per-modality rates (`computeUsageCost`; rate resolution in `resolveCaptionCost`, `src/captioning/cost.ts`) and `updateCaptionResult` persists both atomically with the caption into the `media_assets` caption usage columns. This is auxiliary (out-of-loop) accounting — a **separate lane** from §8b, never folded into `agent_sessions.usage_*` (§8c). Tool-context caption calls (`read_image`/danbooru `preview`/the media tool) compute usage on the response too but have no durable home today (§8c, forward-compat §13 of the spec).

### Assistant echo enrichment

A bot-sent message reaches the timeline twice: the `send_message` tool persists it after the provider send returns, and the Matrix sync loop echoes it back as an inbound event. The two arrivals **race**, and the invariant is **exactly one `timeline_events` row per Matrix event**, whichever side wins:

- **Send first (the common case).** `send_message` → `TimelineStore.ingestAssistantSend` finds no existing self-sent row for the `(provider, external_id)` and inserts under the `assistant:{session}:{eventId}:{chunk}` canonical id. The echo then arrives and `ingestAssistantEcho` **enriches in place**: matched by `external_id` (or fuzzy body match — ±5 min window, max 100 candidates — for a send that got no receipt id), the assistant row keeps its canonical id and body and adopts the echo's server timestamp, the min `receivedAt`, and — critically — the echo's **attachments** (the send-side event carries no attachment metadata; the echo's mxc refs are what the enrichment worker downloads from; previously they were dropped here, so assistant media enrichment silently depended on the echo winning the race and landing its own duplicate row).
- **Echo first (the race).** The echo finds no assistant row and appends a `matrix:{account}:{eventId}` row. When `send_message`'s append then lands, `ingestAssistantSend` **merges into that row** instead of appending: the stored row keeps its canonical id and server timestamp (mirroring what the echo would have contributed in the other order), keeps its attachments, and adopts the send's body/html, session attribution (`agent_session_id`/`_generation`), and timeline key (the send's target key is authoritative — a DM self-echo can derive a mismatched key). The lookup is unscoped by timeline key for that same DM-mismatch reason but only merges into a *self-sent assistant* row, so another account's received copy of the event never matches. Historically this direction just appended, creating a second row per event (the assistant-echo duplicates cleaned up by the v1→v2 migration); the duplicate could sort outside a summarization job's declared cursor range (same timestamp, later `received_at`) while the timestamp-cutoff level-1 render still included it, tripping the declared-vs-rendered input-integrity assertion (§9b).

After echo resolution, if the echo `needsEnrichment()` (has attachments, links, or reply context), the resolved row's `enrichment_status` is set to `"pending"` and the enrichment pool is notified. This ensures assistant-sent media (e.g. images from `danbooru`) gets the same enrichment treatment as user messages — downloads, link preview resolution, character card detection, and optional captioning via `caption_assistant_messages`.

### Trigger path integration

When a trigger fires, its group is resolved at the accept seam, then the session waits for that group's enrichment + captioning to be ready **after it is registered (visible-as-running) and admitted, but before its context build** (`awaitTriggerReadiness`, relocated into `launchSession` — spec CLAIM-VISIBILITY-SERIALIZATION, see §8 "Parallel sessions"):

1. **Resolve trigger group** — `handleInbound`, synchronously after `accept`/`addClaim`: persist `trigger_group_id` on all group events, notify caption pool
2. **Register the session** — `launchSession`: `createPlaceholder → markRunning → attach-claim` (now visible in `activeForTimeline`), then the missing-target + budget-admission gates
3. **Await enrichment** — wait for `enrichment_status != 'pending'` on all group events (timeout: `enrichment.trigger_wait_timeout_ms`, default 30s)
4. **Await captions** — wait for `caption_status != 'pending'` on all group image assets (timeout: `captioning.trigger_wait_timeout_ms`, default 45s)
5. **Build context** — `AgentSessionFactory.create`: the agent sees fully enriched events

The wait sits *behind* visibility so a sibling session that builds while this one is mid-captioning still sees it as running (the incident in spec CLAIM-VISIBILITY-SERIALIZATION). Timeouts produce warnings but don't block the session — the agent gets whatever data is available. The same `awaitTriggerReadiness` runs once per fresh launch, queued-drain, activation launch, `spawn_session` re-dispatch, **and** reply-resume; the activation prelude additionally pre-waits to order its `inactive → pending` bulk flip, after which the in-`launchSession` wait is a no-op.

### Context hydration

At context-build time, the context builder batch-queries the enrichment tables for all events being rendered and merges the results into `CanonicalChatEvent` objects via `mergeEnrichmentIntoEvent()`. This populates:

- `attachments` — from `media_assets` with `role='attachment'`, sorted by `source_index`
- `linkedMedia` — from `media_assets` with `role='linked_media'`, sorted by `source_index`
- `replyTo.attachments` — from `role='reply_attachment'`
- `replyTo.linkedMedia` — from `role='reply_linked_media'`
- `replyTo.linkPreviews` — from `link_previews` with `context='reply'`, with associated `reply_preview_media` assets
- `linkPreviews` — from `link_previews` with `context='message'`, with associated `preview_media` assets
- Character card metadata (`isCharacterCard`, `cardName`) from `detected_content` / `detected_metadata_json` on media assets
- Reply sender as `SenderInfo { id, username?, displayName? }` — populated from `reply_senders`; Matrix leaves `username` unset; the renderer applies the same `username ?? id` handle rule as main-message senders

The renderer receives fully populated events and works unchanged.

### X.com enrichment via FxTwitter

X status URLs get a dedicated, TS-native enrichment stage (`src/fxtwitter/`) instead of the Synapse scraper — x.com's bare Open Graph card yields a near-useless preview (account name and date, zero media), while the FxTwitter API returns the real content. The module is pure TS with no Matrix/native imports: `url.ts` (status-URL detection/canonicalization), `client.ts` (API client over `guardedFetch` + the shared proxy dispatcher), `types.ts` (tolerant API types, the persisted payload types, the `"fx_twitter"` source-kind constant, config resolution), `format.ts` (payload building, flat-text rendering, the `x_fetch` document assembly).
