"healthy"`) and `isProbeDue(key)` (`unhealthy && !probeInFlight && now ≥ nextProbeAt`) expose health to the model-fallback resolver (below) **without any new mutable scheduler state** — the resolver only reads the same `probeInFlight`/`nextProbeAt` gate the pump uses, so resolver and pump agree.
- **Outcome accounting** (`noteOutcome(group, modelKey, classification, status?, retryAfterMs?)` — the generalization of `noteResult`/`noteStatus`, which remain as group-axis-only wrappers): a clean success resets the streak (and recovers an unhealthy model); an `environmental` failure feeds the streak — **except a plain 429**, which feeds only the group's throttle backoff (budget pressure is not evidence the model is unwell; 503/529 feed *both* axes); `content`/`aborted` outcomes are neutral — neither count nor reset (one session's oversized context must not pause the model; an in-flight probe settled by a neutral outcome is inconclusive and re-probes promptly).
- **Pump selection.** The pump no longer gates the whole group on one signal: it selects, in priority-then-FIFO order, the first queued entry that is **admissible** — group slot free, group not in throttle backoff, and the entry's model healthy (or unhealthy with an open probe window and no probe in flight). An unhealthy model's waiters are *skipped over, not waited behind*, so they never head-of-line-block healthy models sharing the group. When everything queued is gated behind probe windows, a timer re-pumps at the earliest window.
- **Properties** (spec P6): during an outage, any number of waiting sessions on the broken model produce exactly **one upstream request per probe window** (every Layer-0 retry re-enters `acquire()` and queues — no redundant hammering), and the capped backoff bounds the long-outage tail at `llm_probe_backoff_max_ms`. Priority survives the outage: waiters queue at their class rank, so when the probe succeeds, interactive requests drain first. Logged via `llm_model_unhealthy` / `llm_model_probe` / `llm_model_recovered` (with outage duration and waiter count).

### Model fallback — transparent composite models

A logical model `X` with a fallback chain `[X, Y, Z]` is a **composite model** (spec MODEL-FALLBACK): a request to `X` is served by the first chain member that is up, and **the caller never sees that `X` was unavailable**. Fallback sits *below* the failure-handling layer — it is model **selection** in front of §8a's per-model health (which **is** the circuit breaker; no new breaker is built) plus a return-to-primary canary policy. Chains are declared on the model: `[models.X].fallback = ["Y", "Z"]` (logical ids in `[models.*]`); the model is the implicit head and the chain is exactly the one written (a member's own `fallback` does **not** transitively extend it). Role-specific fallback comes from pointing a role at a **virtual model** (`[models.*].inherits`, §4) — there is no per-tool/per-session-type fallback config.

`src/agent/model-fallback.ts` is the one shared helper. **Build time (once)** does only what must be fixed: a **capability pre-filter** (drop members that can't serve the request's fixed needs — image blocks → `multimodal`; never drops the head); the **min-over-chain context ceiling** (`min(context_window)` across survivors, min'd with the session-type override — one conservative value valid for *whichever* member serves); and a per-candidate dispatch pipeline (`withSchedulerAdmission(base, …)` per member, with the member's group/key/probe-cap). **Per attempt** (inside the composed `streamFn`, wrapped by `withRequestRetry`, so each Layer-0 attempt re-resolves) it chooses one member in chain order:

The **agent path** applies the capability filter too (`factory.create`): `buildModelFallback` is called BEFORE `buildContext`, so image-presence is read from the **raw session inputs** available at create time — the trigger event's own/reply image attachments and (on resume) any prefix-snapshot message carrying `imageBlocks` (`rawInputsRequireMultimodal`). Whether those images are shipped as pixels is a property of the session's **reply model** — the session-type model (`modelConfig`), the one that will actually serve the turn — and of that model **alone**: the builder gates image-block creation on `ContextBuilder.replyModelCanSeeImages` (resolved from `sessionType.model`, or the `default` registry block only when a session type declares no model override — in which case `default` genuinely *is* its model), never on `[models.default]`-as-a-fixed-reference or any fallback member's modality. So the requirement is "the reply model accepts images **and** a raw input carries one": when it holds every viable chain member must also accept image input (`input_modalities` includes `"image"`), so a fall-over never ships image blocks to a text-only fallback (the head is never dropped); when the reply model is **text-only** no pixels are created at all — the captions (always rendered) carry the image — and the trigger imposes **no** requirement, so the full fallback chain stays usable. This is a deliberate **safe over-approximation** — "any raw image, *when the reply model can see it*, ⇒ require multimodal for the whole session" — and generation (summarize/condense/diary) and proactive sessions impose no requirement (they send no pixels). **Ceiling resolved-once.** The agent enforcement ceiling is `buildModelFallback`'s `operativeContextWindow` (min over the capability-**surviving** chain); `resolveSessionContextCeiling` (text-editor read budget, console — no per-session image info) is the min over the **full** chain. The filter can only drop a text-only member, so the surviving min is **equal or larger** than the full-chain min — both are resolved once and both stay ≤ the `context_window` of every member that can actually serve, so neither overflows a serving model and the invariant holds even though the two values may differ for an image-bearing session.

- **head viable** (`modelHealth(head) === "healthy"` *and* `budget.isModelAvailable(head-logical)`) → the head (`primary`);
- **head unhealthy + probe window open + in-budget** (`isProbeDue(head)` *and* `budget.isModelAvailable(head-logical)`) → the head as the **canary** — this attempt enters admission keyed on the head and §8a admits it as the half-open probe (the *only* signal `X` is back, since transparent fallback routes all other traffic away from it). The canary is budget-gated: a successful probe on an over-budget head couldn't route traffic back anyway (the head stays non-viable until its window resets), so probing it would be wasted spend; suppressing the probe doesn't strand it, since `nextProbeAt` only advances when a probe fires, so the head is immediately probe-due again once the budget window resets;
- else the **first downstream member** that is healthy *and* in-budget (`health-fallback` when the head is unhealthy, `budget-fallback` when the head is merely over a model-scoped cap);
- else (nothing healthy + in-budget) → the head (`all-unhealthy`), so it fails, Layer-0 retries the *composite*, and the §8 per-class wall-clock budget decides whole-chain park/wait (the budgets now bound the composite). A member that lacks `reasoning` is **not** filtered — it degrades to no-thinking with a warn. The chosen member's `Model` descriptor **and** per-request `api_key` are substituted at the one seam pi-agent-core exposes (`config.model` + `apiKey` passed to `streamFn`); this is not mid-stream switching — each attempt is a whole fresh request to one model.

**Recovery is a single canary success** (no anti-flap machinery): a successful probe flips `X` healthy and all traffic resolves back at once — the 3-failure threshold dampens flapping for free, and prompt-cache retention (~5 min) makes a flip-flop cost-free. A non-head resolution (or a canary) logs one rate-limitable `model_fallback_resolved { consumer, chain, chosen, reason }`. A single-member chain degrades to the bare admitted stream/fetch (no health reads, no log). The **console scheduler view** annotates each unhealthy model with its **logical id(s)** and labels a fallback-bearing model's probe window **"canary pending / in flight"** (vs a no-fallback model's "next probe") — a config-derived `modelHealthAnnotations` map (health key → logical ids + has-fallback, `buildModelHealthAnnotations`) bridges the config-blind snapshot to the names on `GET /api/scheduler`.

**Unified registry + all consumers (spec MODEL-FALLBACK §2.3/§6).** For virtual models and fallback to apply *uniformly*, every consumer resolves its model through `[models.*]` by **name** — the non-agent inference clients (captioning, `image_generate`, `x_search`, remote embedding) no longer define models inline (their `cost`/`provider`/`rate_limit_group`/`fallback` now live on the referenced block; image-gen's per-image charge is `[models.*].cost.per_image`). The agent loop composes the **StreamFn** resolver (`buildModelFallback`); the four raw-fetch clients compose the **fetch-shaped** equivalent (`runFetchWithFallback`) — same per-attempt member selection, scheduler admission, both-axes `noteOutcome`, and canary, but each supplies a per-member fetch. Captioning additionally supplies a per-lane **capability predicate** (`input_modalities` includes the lane's modality — image/video/audio), dropping a fallback member that can't serve the lane; the other three fetch chains are homogeneous by config so they rely on that rather than a predicate. The fetch helper tries each member **at most once per call** (the consumer's own retry layer re-drives across calls, by which point §8a health has shifted), so a single-member chain makes exactly one attempt. Every consumer threads the billed member's **logical id** into its ledger row (§8f) for exact attribution; the paid-tool gates — and captioning's own pre-call gate (so the on-demand x_search/media/danbooru caption lanes refuse too, not just the worker pool) — refuse only when *every* chain member is over budget. (Remote-embedding fallback members must be **vector-compatible** — same `dim` **and** embedding space — since the index/cache keys on the head's wire id; **only `dim` (width) is enforced**, so a same-dim/different-space fallback silently degrades retrieval — an operator-config contract, not enforced because one model has different wire ids across backends.)

---

## 8b. Token-Usage Tracking & Context Limits

The harness tracks **actual** provider-reported token usage per request and per session, persists the session-level aggregate, derives a single cost, and enforces a configurable maximum context size — all on actuals, never on the `gpt-tokenizer` estimate (`token_estimate`, the frozen-prefix estimate, is kept unchanged and clearly separate; it measures a different thing — the artificial seed context — and stays useful for the room preview where no actuals can exist).

### Per-request capture at the Layer-0 commit point

Every `AssistantMessage` carries a pi-ai `Usage` (`input`/`output`/`cacheRead`/`cacheWrite`/`totalTokens` + a `cost` object with per-factor and `total` USD, computed upstream by pi-ai's `calculateCost` from the model descriptor's `cost.*` per-MTok factors). `input` is always *uncached* input across providers; cache-write charging is provider-specific and needs no special-casing (Anthropic, and OpenAI on the Responses API from GPT-5.6, bill cache writes and report `cacheWrite > 0` — the responses provider reads `input_tokens_details.cache_write_tokens` as of pi-ai 0.80.x; providers that don't charge report `cacheWrite: 0`, and `calculateCost` applies each model's own `cost.cache_write`). Two derived quantities: a request's **prompt size** is `input + cacheRead + cacheWrite`; the **context size after** a request is `totalTokens` (prompt + output), the value the *next* request grows from. Not assumed monotonic (Anthropic strips prior-turn thinking on later requests) — we always report the last observation.

`withRequestRetry` (Layer 0, §8) buffers each attempt to its terminal event and commits only on a clean `done`. That `done` branch is the **single authoritative capture point**: it fires `ctx.onRequestCommitted(message)` (best-effort, exceptions swallowed) and records the usage on the request ring's `done` row. Terminal errors carry stub-zero usage and are never captured (a token-producing attempt that then errors is a known, accepted slight undercount — the data simply does not arrive on error events). The observe-only `onAttemptEvent` tap is *not* reused for accounting (it fires for attempts that are later discarded).

### Session aggregation & persistence

`SessionUsageTracker` (`src/agent/usage.ts`) is a pure in-memory accumulator — one per created agent, owned by the factory, bound as `onRequestCommitted: (msg) => tracker.record(msg.usage)`. It holds cumulative `{llmRequests, inputTokens, outputTokens, cacheReadTokens, cacheWriteTokens, cost}` plus `contextTokens` (the **last** committed request's `totalTokens`, or null before any commit). On resume-in-place the factory seeds it from the persisted row (`CreateAgentOptions.usageSeed`, built by `usageSeedFromRow` in `app.ts`) so a resumed session continues accumulating rather than resetting.

`attachSessionCapture` subscribes `tracker.onUpdate` → `Storage.updateAgentSessionUsage(id, totals, ctx.model)` (single-writer queue, one write per committed request — negligible at single-digit-to-low-tens requests/session, no debounce), unsubscribed on `detach`. Persistence lives in **denormalized columns on `agent_sessions`** (`llm_requests`, `usage_input_tokens`, `usage_output_tokens`, `usage_cache_read_tokens`, `usage_cache_write_tokens`, `usage_cost` REAL, `context_tokens`; all nullable → a row without committed usage reads "unknown", not zero). The same write also rewrites `model_id` with the **actually-billed** model (via `coalesce`, so a null arg never clobbers a recorded value), making the session's model authoritative per request rather than a placeholder guess; `createPlaceholder` separately **seeds** `model_id` with `resolveModelId(sessionType)` at creation so even a zero-commit session is never blank. (Before this seed was added, the default/proactive chat path left `model_id` NULL — only the worker pools seeded it — so cost-bearing sessions could display no model.) There is **no per-request table** — per-request usage already persists verbatim inside each assistant message in `transcript_json`; the columns are the cheap rollup so list views and future cost queries never parse transcripts, and the request ring covers operator-facing per-attempt diagnostics. Both the chat path and the worker pools attach capture, so all session types are tracked uniformly. At detach, one greppable `session_usage` line is logged with the totals snapshot. These counters track **only** the agent loop's own context-bearing requests; provider calls made *outside* the runner (captioning, the `image_generate` tool) are accounted in a separate lane (§8c) and never enter these columns.

### Context-size limits — one model ceiling, one per-session override, one resolver

`context_window` is **the** model-level context ceiling AND the always-on enforcement base (spec CONTEXT-LIMIT-UNIFICATION). Operators set it deliberately *below* the physical provider window (the shipped 128 000 against this model's ~200 k real window) to stay clear of edge-case degradation/cost. `max_context_tokens` survives **only** on a session type (`agent.session_types.*`) as an artificial, tighter **override**; there is no model-level `max_context_tokens` knob. The **operative per-session ceiling is `min(context_window, session_type.max_context_tokens)`** considering the override only when set (`composeSessionContextCeiling`); since `context_window` is always present, it is **never null — enforcement is always wired**, including for interactive sessions (`default`/`proactive`), which previously resolved to no ceiling.

The ceiling is resolved **once per session** (`AgentSessionFactory.resolveSessionContextCeiling(sessionType)`) and fed to **every** consumer, so no call site reaches for `config.models.*.context_window` independently (the structural fix for the prior split's drift):
1. **Enforcement** — the pre-flight `checkContextBudget` (below).
2. **The pi-ai `Model` descriptor** `contextWindow:` field — fed the resolved operative ceiling (not the raw window), so any future window-keyed mechanism (compaction, SDK overflow math) triggers against the ceiling the session is actually judged against. Inert today (miku uses neither), but the descriptor is honest and the old `?? 128_000` literal fallback is gone.
3. **The text-editor read budget** (`createTextEditorTool({ contextWindowTokens })`, wired in `buildSessionTools(…, sessionType)` from the same resolver) — so a single `view` is sized against the budget it will actually consume. Worker session types build minimal tool sets without the text editor, so this only affects interactive sessions; it is a correctness fix that matters the day an interactive type gets an override or a non-`default` model.

Cross-field validation at app wiring (`validateContextTokenCeilings`) fails fast when a model a session type resolves to has **no `context_window`** (it is mandatory — `default` is always resolvable, plus each session type's own model), or when a session type's `max_context_tokens` exceeds the resolved model's `context_window`. The only legitimate consumer of the *physical* model window is recognizing a provider-side overflow rejection — pattern-matched on the error string (`isContextOverflow`), not a config number; the physical window is intrinsic model metadata, not a config knob.

Enforcement is a **per-request pre-flight** (`RequestRetryContext.checkContextBudget`, evaluated once before the first attempt — every Layer-0 attempt replays the identical context, so per-attempt re-checking is meaningless). The factory binds it unconditionally to compare the tracker's `contextTokens` (the last committed request's actual size) against the operative ceiling; a violation logs `session_context_limit_exceeded` (warn) and synthesizes a terminal error classified **`content`**, surfaced through `withRequestRetry` *without consuming retry budget*. Properties:

- **Actuals only, first request never blocked.** Enforcement uses the provider-reported truth, never the estimate. No actuals exist before the first commit, so the first request is never blocked locally — if a seeded context is already oversized, the provider rejects it and the existing content-class path handles it (now via pi-ai's `isContextOverflow` pattern set, which `classifyLlmError` consults to recognize provider-side overflow rejections from any supported gateway, broader than the old hand-rolled keyword list).
- **Graceful by construction.** The limit only blocks *issuing another request* from an oversized context; an in-flight response always completes, and a session that terminates cleanly (send_message `final:true` / `NO_REPLY` / worker finalize) never fails even if its final response pushed it over.
- **Reuses the content-class path end to end** (no new error class or session status). For interactive sessions the run parks `failed-resumable` (and a manual console resume re-fails the pre-flight deterministically — honest and harmless, no request issued); for worker sessions it routes to the job's semantic-retry path, so a fresh attempt rebuilds context from scratch — exactly the "restart a runaway diary/summary" behavior, bounded by the job's attempt budget.

### Console surfacing

`sessionMeta` adds `llmRequests`, `usage` (the four consumption values + cost, null until first commit), `contextTokens` (actual, last-observed), and `maxContextTokens` (the operative ceiling resolved from **current** config via `factory.resolveSessionContextCeiling`, **not** persisted per session — the console shows today's config). Now that `context_window` is the always-on base, `maxContextTokens` resolves to a non-null number for every session, so the `ctx X / Y` display always shows the `/ limit` denominator (the API field stays nullable for legacy/headless rows). The request ring's `usage` flows through `GET /api/llm-requests`. The session view renders a session-level actuals line (`ctx 46.3k / 80k · in … · out … · cr … · cw … · $… · N req`), the rollout annotates each committed assistant message with its per-request usage (`ctx …` = that request's `totalTokens`; rendered once per message, nothing for messages without real usage — including tentative partials, which have no usage until commit), and the scheduler request table gains `ctx`/`tok`/`cost` columns for `done` rows. Cost renders as `—` when all factors are zero (unconfigured model), never a misleading `$0.00`.

---

## 8c. Auxiliary (Out-of-Loop) Model Usage

Several model-call paths bypass the agent runner entirely — captioning (`src/captioning`, a detached enrichment worker), the `image_generate` tool (`src/tools/image-gen.ts`, a raw Gemini `:generateContent` fetch), and the `x_search` tool (`src/tools/x-search.ts`, a raw OpenRouter chat-completions fetch to Grok **plus** its inline captions). None flow through `withRequestRetry`'s Layer-0 commit point, so §8b never sees them. Their spend is tracked in a **separate accounting lane** (spec AUXILIARY-USAGE-TRACKING), parallel to §8b and **never merged into it**.

**The §4 invariant.** Auxiliary usage is *never* added to `agent_sessions.usage_*`/`usage_cost`/`context_tokens`. Those stay pure agent-loop actuals: `context_tokens` feeds `max_context_tokens` enforcement, and image-output tokens (billed on a different $/tok scale and not part of the agent's context window) would corrupt that if blended. The lanes are summed only at the presentation layer.

**Shared cost helper.** `computeUsageCost(rates, usage)` in `src/agent/usage.ts` is a pure, I/O-free calculator mirroring pi-ai's `calculateCost` (`rate/1e6 * tokens` per component) plus a flat `perImage * images` term. Both paths import it; the formula lives once, guaranteeing parity with §8b. `CostRates` (USD/1M tokens + optional `perImage`) and `RawTokenUsage` are its shared types. Rates come from config, not a pi-ai model descriptor (these paths never construct one): `[captioning.model].cost` / per-modality `cost`, and `[image_gen.costs.{pro,flash}]` (with `per_image`). Caption rate resolution (`resolveCaptionCost`, `src/captioning/cost.ts`) treats cost as a property of a **specific model** that is never inherited across models: a modality's own `cost` block wins; otherwise `[captioning.model].cost` applies **only when the modality runs the shared model** (no `model.id` override, or the same id). A modality that overrides `model.id` to a *different* model without its own `cost` block has **unknown** cost (untracked) — the shared model's rates are not borrowed for it — and startup logs a `caption_cost_untracked_model_override` warning when top-level pricing is configured (so the gap is visible). Unset/all-zero rates ⇒ usage still captured, cost 0 ("untracked"); usage *absent* (gateway omitted it) ⇒ null ("unknown"), distinct from 0.

**Captioning lane.** Captured in `describeMedia`, priced in the `InferenceClient`, persisted by `updateCaptionResult` into the `media_assets` caption usage columns (§7a) — one durable row already exists per caption job, so usage lives there. Pool aggregates derive by `SUM`/`COUNT` (`getCaptioningUsageAggregate`).

**Tool-use lane.** `image_generate` reads Gemini `usageMetadata` (`parseGeminiUsage`: `promptTokenCount − cachedContentTokenCount → input`, `candidatesTokenCount → output` (the generated image, billed as tokens), `images` = count saved), computes cost from the tier's `[image_gen.costs.*]`, and calls a per-session `recordToolUsage` callback that appends one row to the generic **`tool_invocations` ledger** (`id`, `agent_session_id`, `tool_name`, `tool_call_id`, `model_id`, `provider`, the four token columns, `images`, `cost`, `ref`, `created_at`). The tool is built per-session (`buildSessionTools`), so the factory closes over `agentSessionId` for attribution; the callback enqueues the insert on the single-writer queue, and a sink failure never fails the tool (the image is already saved). One row per billable call; no row when usage is unknown. The per-session rollup is **derived on read** (`getSessionToolUsage`: `SUM`/`COUNT` by `agent_session_id`) — no columns on `agent_sessions`. The ledger is generic over `tool_name`, so any future raw-fetch tool reuses it (spec §13). **`x_search` is the first such reuse**: one row for the Grok call (`parseOpenAiUsage` → `[x_search.cost]` per-MTok, `provider:"openrouter"`, `ref:"grok"`) **and** one per inline caption (`ref:"caption:<statusId>"`, priced by the caption client) — both written through the *same* per-session `recordToolUsage` callback `image_generate` uses (hoisted in `buildSessionTools`). This gives **tool-context captions a durable home for the first time** (previously they computed usage but had nowhere to land), exactly what the generic-over-`tool_name` ledger anticipated.

**Console surfacing.** Captioning: per-item `in · out · cr · $cost` chip in the pipeline item detail, and a pool aggregate (`N captioned · in … · out … · $X spent`) on the captioning pool card. Image-gen: the rollout's `image_generate` tool block carries an `in · out · $cost` chip (ledger rows matched into the transcript by `tool_call_id`), and the session view adds a **second, visually distinct** `tools: N calls · … · $…` line beneath the §8b actuals — the two lanes shown side-by-side, never blended (different pricing currencies; tool tokens are not context-bearing). `x_search`'s Grok + caption rows feed this same line and the `/api/cost-overview` tool lane for free (the ledger is generic over `tool_name`). A global `/api/cost-overview` (`getCostOverview`) sums the three lanes — agent-loop (`Σ agent_sessions.usage_cost`), tool (`Σ tool_invocations.cost`), captioning (`Σ media_assets.caption_cost`) — rendered side-by-side in the pipelines column footer. All reuse `formatTokens`/`formatUsd` (null → "—"; cost hidden when 0).

---

## 8d. Per-Session Cost Limits

A configurable **USD cost ceiling per session run** that bounds an autonomous session's total spend, counting **both session-scoped lanes**: the §8b agent-loop cost (`SessionUsageTracker.cost`) and this session's §8c tool-use cost (`tool_invocations`, fed by `recordToolUsage`). Captioning is excluded as an input — it is a detached enrichment pool (§7a), not attributable to a session. The §8c §4 invariant holds: the two lanes are summed **only in-memory and at the presentation layer**, never in the persisted `usage_*` columns.

### Combined accounting (in-memory)

`SessionUsageTracker` (§8b) carries the tool lane as a separate `toolCost` field — outside `SessionUsageTotals` (whose shape maps 1:1 to the agent-loop `usage_*` columns), so persistence stays agent-loop-pure. `recordToolCost(cost)` accumulates it; `combinedCost()` returns `totals.cost + toolCost` (the ceiling's enforcement basis). Two notification channels are kept distinct: `onUpdate` (agent-loop `record()` only → the persistence subscription) and `onBudgetChange` (fires on **both** `record()` and `recordToolCost()`, passing `combinedCost()` → the soft-warn watcher). **The tracker is constructed in `app.ts`** (which owns seed computation) and passed into the factory via `CreateAgentOptions.usage`, so the same instance receives both the tool-cost feed (wired into `buildSessionTools`' `recordToolUsage`, alongside the unchanged durable ledger write) and the factory's Layer-0 commits; worker pools/tests that have no tool lane let the factory construct it from the seed. On resume the tool lane is seeded from `getSessionToolUsage(id).cost` (continue mode) or 0 (fresh mode), parallel to the agent-loop `usageSeed`.

### Configuration — global default + per-type override

`[agent].max_session_cost_usd` is the global default; `[agent.session_types.*].max_session_cost_usd` overrides it per type (USD). `AgentSessionFactory.resolveSessionCostCeiling(sessionType)` resolves once per session: the override when set, else the global default, else `undefined` (unlimited). A resolved value of `0` (either level) means **no cap** — so a session type can set `0` to opt out even under a global default. `[agent].cost_warn_fraction` (default 0.8, strictly in (0,1)) is the soft-warn point. Bounds are enforced by the TypeBox schema; cost has no cross-field dependency (unlike the context window), so there is no app-wiring validator. Like the context ceiling, the cost ceiling is operator config, **not persisted per session** (the console shows today's config).

### Two-tier, agent-facing enforcement

A console-only warning is useless for an autonomous session (no operator is watching), so the soft signal is delivered **to the agent**:

1. **Soft threshold → interjection (one-shot).** When `combinedCost()` first crosses `cost_warn_fraction × ceiling`, `wireCostBudgetWarner` (subscribed via `onBudgetChange`) steers a single `<interjection>` into the running agent (`sessions.steer(id, { type: "interjection", content })`, rendered by `convert.ts`), quoting spent-vs-limit and instructing it to wind down (send its final message, avoid further tool calls). A `warned` latch makes it one-shot; logs `session_cost_warn`. No-op when no ceiling resolves. Reaches the model before its next request — including between tool-call rounds of an in-flight `continue()` — so it is effective exactly for the runaway multi-request case. The warner is wired on the **chat launch + resume** paths (interactive + proactive, the sessions that can conversationally wind down and that own the tool-cost lane); **worker-pool sessions** (summary/diary) rely on the hard cap (+ semantic-retry) alone — they have no "send final" affordance and no tool spend.
2. **Hard cap → pre-flight block (backstop).** The factory binds `checkCostBudget` into `withRequestRetry`'s pre-flight (alongside `checkContextBudget`; context is checked first, first violation wins). It compares `combinedCost()` against the ceiling and, on a violation, synthesizes a `content`-class terminal error **without consuming retry budget**, logging `session_cost_limit_exceeded`. The first request is never blocked (combined cost is 0 before any commit/tool call), an in-flight response always completes, and a cleanly-terminating turn never fails. It reuses the content-class path end to end: **interactive** sessions park `failed-resumable` (a manual resume re-fails deterministically once the seeded combined cost reloads); **worker** sessions route to the job's content-class semantic-retry. *Known, accepted imperfection:* a fresh worker attempt is a new session run whose combined-cost counter resets to 0, so total worker spend across retries can reach ~`ceiling × attempts` — chosen for consistency with the context ceiling over introducing a new terminal class.

### Console surfacing

`sessionMeta` adds `maxSessionCostUsd` (resolved from current config via `resolveSessionCostCeiling`; null = unlimited). The session view renders a **combined cost-budget line** — `budget · $0.82 / $1.00 · 82%` — the **only** place the two lanes are summed (`usage.cost` + `toolUsage.cost`); the §8b actuals line and the §8c `tools:` line above keep them separate. The indicator turns destructive at ≥100%. The `session_usage` settle log gains `combinedCost` and `maxSessionCostUsd` (the resolved ceiling, threaded once per run into the capture ctx — from the chat launch/resume wiring, where it is shared with the soft-warn watcher, and equally from the summary/diary worker pools, which have the §2.2 hard cap but no watcher; `null` when unlimited) so the greppable line is self-contained — combined spend next to the ceiling it was measured against, for every session type.

---

## 8e. Period Cost Limits (BudgetEngine)

Configurable **USD budgets over a rolling or calendar window**, enforced across the whole app and **scoped** by any combination of consumer *class*, *session type*, *tool*, and *model*. Distinct from and orthogonal to the §8d per-run ceiling (which caps a single session, no time window): §8d is unchanged, and the per-request pre-flight now evaluates **both**. Source: `src/budget/` (`engine.ts`, `window.ts`, `normalize.ts`, `zero-cost.ts`); enforcement is wired across the factory, the worker pools, the proactive scheduler, and the paid tools.

### Counting model — own-scope only, with layering

Every rule counts **only its own scope**: it sums only the `usage_events` (§8f) matching its selector within its window. A prospective spend is **allowed iff every rule whose selector covers it has headroom** (blocked if **any** covering rule is at/over cap). The "keep summaries/diary running after sessions are suspended" behavior is **not a counting mode** — it is the emergent consequence of layering a narrow rule (e.g. `session_types=[default,proactive]`) under a broad one (`global`, no selector): `default`/`proactive` stop at the narrow cap while `summarize`/`condense`/`diary` keep running until the broad `global` cap halts everything.

**Dependency cascade (structural, in code — not config).** Triggered (`default`) and proactive sessions additionally require their summarization prerequisite to be *available*: admission needs **both** (a) the session's own covering rules have headroom **and** (b) every class it depends on (`summarize`, `condense`) is not blocked. Encoded in `app.ts` as `dependencies` and consumed by `BudgetEngine.checkAdmission`. Diary depends on nothing.

**Zero-cost bypass (`§2.2`).** A model whose *configured* cost rate is zero (`collectZeroCostModelIds` scans every pricing site — agent models, captioning, image-gen tiers, x_search, remote embeddings; a paid appearance in any lane wins) has $0 spend, so `check()` short-circuits to allowed: it can never move or be blocked by a budget rule. The bypass is **budget-only** — zero-cost consumers still obey the dependency cascade. Zero-cost events are still written to the ledger (for token/request counts) but are invisible to the engine.

### The engine

`BudgetEngine` is seeded from the ledger at startup (`SUM(cost_usd)` per rule over its window) and answers admission/pre-flight/tool-gate questions **without a SQL SUM on the hot path**: each rule keeps an in-memory running total + the window's `resetsAt`. `record(event)` (which rejects any non-positive cost — NaN/0/negative — so a stray cost can't poison the total) increments every covering rule (synchronous, called by the ledger recorder before the durable insert). Every read/decision path (`check` / `isClassAvailable` / `ruleStatuses`) and `record` first calls `rollIfNeeded`, which on a passed **calendar** boundary resets that rule's total to **0 in place — no SUM** (a fresh calendar window is empty by construction), so a rule that hit cap yesterday stops reporting blocked the instant the boundary passes rather than lagging up to one tick. A periodic tick (`tickMs`, default 60 s) is the authoritative reconciler: it recomputes rolling windows from the ledger (shedding aged-out spend) and **re-SUMs** a calendar rule when its boundary passes (correcting any drift from the cheap in-place zero). `check(descriptor)` → `{ allowed, blockingRules[], primary }` (primary = earliest-resetting, for the user message); `checkAdmission(sessionType, modelId)` folds in the dependency cascade (it now delegates to `checkAdmissionChain` with the single head logical id; the launch gate calls `checkAdmissionChain` directly with the whole fallback chain so an in-budget fallback admits, spec MODEL-FALLBACK §6.1); `isClassAvailable` / `isModelAvailable` are the coarse hooks (`isModelAvailable` treats a no-`models` rule as wildcard-covering, consistent with `check`; consumed by the per-attempt model-fallback resolver to drop an over-budget chain member); `ruleStatuses()` returns one entry per configured rule (never filtered) for the console. A rolling rule's `resetsAt` is only a `now + duration` upper bound on the hot path; **off** the hot path — `ruleStatuses()` (console countdown) and `accurateResetsAt(name)` (the human-facing refusal/defer message) — it is sharpened to `min(contributing ts) + duration` (the instant the oldest contributing spend ages out) via a `minUsageTs` ledger query, so the surfaced "back at X" isn't pinned at the full duration. `logBlocked` emits one structured `usage_limit_blocked` line naming **every** hit rule (the rule `name` is the join key across logs, console, and config).

### Configuration — a centralized `[[limits]]` array

Rules live in one top-level `[[limits]]` array (not per-consumer). Each: `name` (unique), `max_usd` (≥ 0; `0` = no spend allowed for the scope), `window` (`{ type:"rolling", duration:"24h" }` or `{ type:"calendar", period:"day"|"week"|"month", tz? }` — tz defaults to `agent.timezone` then UTC), optional selector dimensions (`classes`, `session_types`, `tools`, `models` — AND across, OR within, omitted = wildcard, none = global), and an optional `trigger_rejection_message` (templated with `{resets_at}`). TypeBox covers shape; cross-field semantics (`normalizeLimits`, called from `app.ts` per the project's cross-field-validation-in-app.ts convention) enforce name uniqueness, a parseable duration, a valid **named** IANA tz (bare offsets rejected), and warn (never fail) on unknown tool/session-type/model-id references (the model warning uses the union of every configured model id, `collectKnownModelIds`; `models` stays free-form) or a `trigger_rejection_message` on a rule that cannot cover a triggered session.

### Enforcement points (six gates)

| Gate | Anchor | Behavior when blocked |
|---|---|---|
| Triggered admission | `launchSession` (`app.ts`) | Do not spawn; mark discarded, drain the next queued trigger. A human trigger posts the templated `trigger_rejection_message` (if a covering rule supplies one); proactive is silent. Not queued. **Chain-aware (spec MODEL-FALLBACK §6.1):** `launchSession` resolves the session type's whole fallback chain (`factory.resolveModelChainLogicalIds`) and admits when **any** member is in-budget (`BudgetEngine.checkAdmissionChain`), so a model-scoped cap on the primary does not refuse a session an in-budget fallback could serve; a wildcard rule covers every member and still refuses. **The dependency cascade is chain-aware too:** a prerequisite (summarize/condense) is judged unavailable only when **every** member of *its* chain is over budget (`BudgetEngine.resolveModelChainLogicalIds`, wired to `factory.resolveModelChainLogicalIds`), so a model-scoped cap on a prerequisite's head (e.g. summarization on GLM) does not refuse a dependent reply the prerequisite could still produce on a fallback (DeepSeek) — only a cap covering the prerequisite's whole chain refuses. |
| Proactive admission | `ProactiveScheduler` `budgetDeferUntil` | Clamp next-eligible to `max(normal_schedule, resets_at)` — wait out the window rather than post into a refusal. |
| Worker pools | summary/diary/caption/embed claim gate | Pool stops *claiming* while its class is blocked; resumes on the next poll after reset (parked, nothing dropped). On pause each pool emits one rate-limited (≤1/min) `usage_limit_blocked` (gate `worker_claim`) naming the hit rules. All four gates are **chain-aware (spec MODEL-FALLBACK §6):** a lane parks only when **every** member of its fallback chain is over budget (mirroring the image-gen/x_search `chain.some` tool gates), so a head-only model-scoped cap lets the per-attempt resolver fall to an in-budget fallback instead of stalling the pool. **summary/diary** serve multiple `agent_loop` session types (summarization does `summarize` + `condense`), so their `shouldPause` closures — built in `app.ts` *after* the engine exists — use `makeAgentLoopChainClaimGate`, which parks if **any** lane is stuck where a lane is a session type that can progress iff **any** member of its own chain (`factory.resolveModelChainLogicalIds`, head-first logical ids the `[[limits]].models` rule matches) has headroom; the head's upstream id rides along for provenance, an unresolvable type contributes no lane and never parks. **caption/embed** each gate a single chain: they park only when **every** chain member is over budget. **caption** gates on the pool itself (`CaptionWorkerPool.shouldPauseForBudget` reads `budget.engine` + the resolved caption chain `captionModelIds` at call time, `chain.some` over `check`, carrying its own rate-limited-log pattern); **embed** is the retrieval subsystem's `shouldPause`, the shared `makeChainClaimGate` over the resolved `embeddingChain` with a **late-bound engine source** (`() => budget.engine`) because the subsystem is constructed *before* the engine — it resolves the engine per call and never parks while undefined, so the embed lane is genuinely bounded once startup completes. |
| Dependency gate | triggered/proactive admission | Additionally require the summarization prerequisite (`summarize`/`condense`) to have chain headroom — unavailable only when **every** member of its chain is over budget (`checkAdmissionChain`'s dependency loop, chain-aware; see Triggered admission above). |
| Per-request pre-flight | `checkCostBudget` (`factory.ts`) | `check({class:'agent_loop',…})` after the §8d ceiling; over budget → a `content`-class terminal error (no retry burn), same path §8d uses. |
| Paid tool call | `image_generate` / `x_search` `checkBudget` | Returns a tool **error result** (not a throw) naming the limit + reset, before the paid call (x_search gates only the live call, never a cache hit). The agent sees it and adapts. |

**Two gate approximations to note.** Both are model-dimension simplifications; the *ledger* always records the true per-event model, so spend is attributed exactly even where the gate checks a stand-in:

- **Per-tool gate checks only the tool's primary model.** The paid-tool gate (`makeToolBudgetCheck`) checks `{class:"tool", tool, modelId}` against the **one** model id the tool passes — for `x_search`, the resolved Grok model (`config.model`/`deep_model`), and only on the live call (a cache hit is free and ungated). But `x_search` also captures **inline-caption** rows, priced under the *caption* model and recorded with `class:"tool"`, `tool:"x_search"`. Those rows are therefore governed by any rule whose selector covers `tools=["x_search"]`, `classes=["tool"]`, or the caption model under `models` — **not** by the x_search gate's model dimension (which only ever names the Grok model). A rule meant to cap x_search's caption spend specifically must target one of those, not the Grok model id.
- **Caption pool gates on a representative chain.** `captionModelIds` is the resolved fallback chain (logical ids) of the representative caption model ref (`captioning.model ?? image ?? video ?? audio`), while capture records the true per-modality model (`result.logicalModelId`). The gate parks only when **every** chain member is over budget, so a model-scoped cap on the head still lets the per-attempt resolver fall to an in-budget fallback (the pool keeps claiming); a zero-cost member short-circuits `check` to allowed, so the chain is "available" and the pool never pauses on that member. (The per-request pre-flight and the ledger are unaffected; this is only the pool's coarse claim gate.) The chain is the representative ref's only — a paid *non-representative* modality over its own budget is still governed by the per-attempt resolver + ledger, not this coarse gate.

Free enrichment (link previews, fxtwitter, ingestion) is unaffected. **Reset & resume:** background work parks and resumes by not-claiming; proactive clamps; human triggers get an immediate refusal (re-trigger later); in-session tool calls error at call time with the reset ETA; global exhaustion halts everything that spends while ingestion + free enrichment continue.

## 8f. Unified `usage_events` Ledger

A single append-only table (`usage_events`, schema **v25**) is the source of truth for all cross-cutting usage queries — the BudgetEngine seed/recompute (§8e) and the console "Usage & Cost" page (§11). One row per **billable event** across four classes: `agent_loop` (one row per committed request, at the `onRequestCommitted` hook — additive to the §8b `agent_sessions.usage_*` aggregate), `tool` (at `recordToolUsage`, **additive** to the §8c `tool_invocations` write, which is retained for its `tool_call_id` transcript-annotation link), `caption` (at the caption worker's persist funnel, alongside `media_assets.caption_*`), and `embedding` (the remote provider only — §9d; local embeddings are zero-cost and emit nothing). `model_id` (the **upstream wire id**) is always present, alongside `logical_model_id` (the **config block name**; schema **v30**, spec MODEL-FALLBACK §2.2) — distinct so virtual models that share an upstream are budgeted/grouped separately while health keys on the shared wire id. The agent loop stamps the chain member actually billed (so a request that fell to `Y` is attributed to `Y`); other lanes default `logical_model_id` to `model_id`. The `[[limits]].models` selector and the zero-cost bypass key on `logical_model_id` (v30 backfills it `= model_id` for legacy rows); the console "Usage & Cost" **by-model** grouping (the summary card + timeseries chart) instead keys on `model_id` — the upstream wire id actually billed — so real-model spend is reported as one row regardless of which virtual/logical block routed it (a virtual model such as `default` does not appear as its own bucket, and legacy rows predating the logical-id column collapse into the same real model rather than splitting by column semantics). The **session** attribution columns (`agent_session_id`/`session_type`/`trigger_sender_id`) are null for background (caption/embedding) events — but `caption` rows still carry `timeline_key` (the asset's room, recovered via the caption claim's `event_id → timeline_events` join) and `provider` (from `[captioning.model].provider`), so caption spend stays room-attributable. `cost_usd` defaults 0 so a zero-rate free-model event still records its token/request counts while staying invisible to the engine (§8e zero-cost bypass).

The §8b/§8c **storage invariant is preserved**: per-lane spend still lands in its own columns; `usage_events` is an *additional* unified write, never folded back. A single `recordUsageEvent` (`app.ts`) is the fan-in for every billable event — it increments the engine in memory (synchronous, authoritative) then appends the durable row (best-effort; a ledger failure never fails the underlying work, and the engine re-seeds from the ledger on the next tick / restart). The `usage_events` table is part of the genesis schema (`USAGE_EVENTS_SCHEMA`). Storage exposes `insertUsageEvent` (which rejects a non-finite or negative `cost_usd` at the door — a code-level guard, not a DDL `CHECK`), `sumUsageCost(filter)` (the engine's seed/recompute; the result is `Number.isFinite`-clamped as defense-in-depth), `minUsageTs(filter)` (earliest contributing `ts` for the accurate rolling-reset ETA, §8e), and the console aggregations `getUsageSummary` / `getUsageTimeseries` / `getUsageRecentSessions` / `getUsageRecentToolCalls` / `getUsageLeaderboard` (a humans-only spend ranking attributed by `trigger_sender_id`, **plus** a separate system/self actor breakdown — Summarization/Diary/Proactive — attributed by `session_type` so the spend split across the synthetic `system` sender and null-sender tool rows is captured whole; each carded entry carries its own per-bucket series, and the result also returns `userStats` mean/median over the non-zero humans; §7.1 leaderboard tab).

**Remote-embedding cost (§9d).** The remote embedding provider captures `usage.prompt_tokens` from the response (else estimates from input chars ÷ a configured `chars_per_token`, default 4), prices it at the configured `cost_per_mtok`, and emits a `class='embedding'` row. Local (zero-cost) embeddings emit nothing and run unconditionally.

**Per-user-limits columns (§8g).** `usage_events` carries four nullable columns, written only for the human agent loop (null for every other lane): `requested_model_id` — the **requested** virtual model the per-user selector chose (distinct from `logical_model_id`, the *served* chain member, under active fallback), so a sub-cap scoped to `opus-premium` still counts an outage backup of that model; `budget_partition` — the **first** rendered **shared-pool** key (a literal like `staff`/`public`, or `room:<id>`/`space:<id>`/`hs:<server>`) the event belongs to, denormalized because pool membership is a cascade outcome irreducible from intrinsic columns (an event joining **2+** distinct pools spills the remaining keys to the `usage_event_partitions` child table below); `room_id` — the bare Matrix room id derived from `timeline_key` at insert (`roomIdFromTimelineKey`), the sturdy room-scoped seed for room-matched per-user counters + per-room pools; and `space_id` — the canonical parent space id, resolved + frozen at admission (§11), supplied by the per-user recorder from the frozen ctx (it cannot be derived from intrinsic columns). Five seed indexes back them (`idx_usage_events_sender_ts`, `_partition_ts`, `_requested_model_ts`, `_room_ts`, `_space_ts`). `UsageCostFilter` gains `triggerSenderIds` / `partitionKeys` / `roomIds` / `spaceIds` / `requestedModelIds` (the last folds in null-requested legacy rows via a `logical_model_id` fallback) — the seed/recompute filters of the §8g meters. `Storage.listUsageIdentities({ since, includeRoom, includeSpace })` returns the `DISTINCT` `(sender, room?, space?)` identities present since a timestamp (non-null sender), the enumeration `UserLimitEngine.seedFromLedger()` replays to re-materialize meters at startup (§14).

**Overlapping shared pools (`usage_event_partitions`, schema v4).** A rule may declare **several** shared pools at once (e.g. a per-space cap *and* a global fleet cap) — an event then belongs to more than one model-agnostic pool simultaneously, which the single `budget_partition` column cannot hold. The store denormalizes the **first** pool key on that scalar (the unchanged fast-path — per-user-only and single-pool events write nothing extra) and **spills each additional membership** to a `without rowid` child table `usage_event_partitions(event_id, partition_key, ts, cost_usd, requested_model_id, room_id, space_id)` (index `idx_uep_partition_ts on (partition_key, ts)`), one row per overflow `(event, pool)`, carrying the seed-relevant columns denormalized so the reseed needs no join back. A shared-pool meter re-seeds by **UNION ALL** of its scalar-column matches (`usage_events.budget_partition`) and its child matches (`usage_event_partitions.partition_key`), each windowed and requested-model/room/space filtered identically (`poolReseedUnion` in `database.ts`); since a given `(event, key)` pair lives in exactly one half, the union never double-counts. This is purely additive — a pool's pre-v4 spend stays on the scalar column and the scalar half of the union finds it unchanged, so **no back-fill** is needed. The recorder stamps the full model-aware key set for **both** the agent loop and the tool lane in one place (`recordUsageEvent` → `UserLimitEngine.sharedPoolKeys`, which mirrors `record` coverage: model-agnostic pools always, a model-scoped shared sub-cap only when the requested model is in scope, so the tool lane never joins a sub-cap — issue #14). The `normalizeUserLimits` cap is `MAX_SHARED_POOLS_PER_RULE = 8` distinct shared partitions per rule, bounding the per-event child-write amplification (≤ N−1 extra inserts). The in-memory metering engine is unchanged — it already meters every covering constraint independently; overlapping pools were only ever a ledger-durability gap.

---

## 8g. Per-User Cost Limits & Model Selection

`UserLimitEngine` (`src/budget/user-limits.ts`) is a sibling to the §8e `BudgetEngine` that gates **only the human-triggered agent loop and its tools** (never background/proactive). It adds per-user spending bounds *and* per-user model selection/degradation over the unified ledger, off by default (an absent/empty `[[user_limits]]` array = feature off, zero behavior change). It is a **separate mechanism** from §8e — partitioned counters, a per-field cascade, a selection action, and a narrower enforcement surface — and the two never merge into one rule list. Spec: `spec/PER-USER-LIMITS.md` (IMPLEMENTED — `user`/`room`/`space` matching all shipped; only user model *choice* (§4.3) is deferred).

**Three orthogonal layers.** *Budget* — a user's bound is a **set of constraints** `{ models?, window, max_usd, partition }`, **ANDed** (allowed only if it fits under every covering constraint). A constraint with no `models` is the **fungible total** ("≤ $5/day on anything"); one scoped to `models = [...]` is a **sub-cap** that only *carves* the total — its sole purpose is to reserve headroom for cheaper continuation (degradation). Multiple windows are first-class (daily **and** monthly = two constraints). *Estimation* — overshoot is bounded by **capping, not prediction**: before each request the engine sizes `max_tokens` to what the remaining headroom can pay for (`max_output = floor((remaining − input_cost) / output_price)`), priced at the **requested** model's face cost. The input is the **exact** running context — the factory maintains it incrementally, tokenizing each new live message of `agent.state.messages` exactly once with the §9 primary tokenizer, applying the same `isLiveRuntimeMessage` filter the wire context uses so the counter never charges a message the wire drops. Image blocks in the live slice are charged a **flat per-image cost** (`estimateLiveSliceTokens` — `PER_IMAGE_TOKEN_ESTIMATE`, 1600, an upper bound on what providers bill per image), never their serialized base64: tokenizing the raw JSON counted every base64 character (~1 token per ~3 chars — one 200KB screenshot ≈ 100k phantom tokens), so a single image-bearing tool result (browser screenshots, `read_image`, `image_gen`, …) pushed the counter past every model's operative window and the Gate-B fits check terminated healthy rollouts at a real context far below any ceiling. The seed is the build estimate for a fresh launch and, for a **resume** (where no context is built), the resumed baseline — the last committed request's actual context size (`usage.snapshot().contextTokens`), falling back to the summed snapshot+transcript token estimates — so a resumed/recovered session's first request is capped on its real accumulated context rather than a 0-token baseline. After **every committed request** the counter additionally **reconciles to that same actual** (`onRequestCommitted` snaps the running estimate to the committed request's `totalTokens` before advancing the prompt-cache baseline), so estimator error is bounded to a single inter-commit window instead of accumulating monotonically over a rollout — the committed assistant turn lands in `state.messages` after the hook, so its re-estimate briefly overlaps the output already inside the actual (one turn's output, conservative), erased at the next commit. Within the prompt-cache TTL (`PROMPT_CACHE_TTL_MS`, ~5 min) the prior prompt is priced at `cache_read` and only the new material at `cache_write`, otherwise cache-write throughout (conservative). The estimate also reserves the **additive extended-thinking budget** the provider bills on top of the issued `max_tokens` (`additiveThinkingBudgetTokens` — non-adaptive Anthropic + Google/Gemini are additive at the pi-ai per-level budgets minimal 1024 / low 2048 / medium 8192 / high 16384; adaptive Anthropic Opus/Sonnet 4.6+ and the OpenAI effort path add nothing) so the issued base cap = `affordable_output − thinking_budget` and the post-provider wire `max_tokens` never exceeds the authorized output. Anthropic adaptivity is read from the model config's `adaptive_thinking` flag when set (`true` ⇒ reserve 0, `false` ⇒ the flat additive budget); unset falls back to a hardcoded id heuristic (Opus 4.6/4.7, Sonnet 4.6), so **declare `adaptive_thinking = true` for adaptive Anthropic models newer than that list** (e.g. Opus 4.8+) — otherwise the heuristic mis-classifies them as non-adaptive and reserves a phantom budget that can spuriously deny a within-budget user. A model whose affordable output (text portion, after reserving thinking) falls below `viable_min` (`agent.user_limit_min_output_tokens`, default 256) is **unaffordable**. *Selection* — among the models a user can currently afford, the picker chooses one; degradation is automated selection over the affordable set (the only place an order lives, isolated so a future user-choice picker is a drop-in swap).

**The partition template (§3.5)** generalizes the per-user counter to shared pools. Each constraint carries a `partition` string rendered from the trigger ctx — `{user_id}` (default; seeds off `trigger_sender_id`), or a literal (`staff`) — plus provider-scoped vars: `{channel_id}` / `{room_id}` (canonical / alias for the channel/room id), `{server_id}` / `{space_id}` (canonical / alias for the guild/space id), `{homeserver}` (Matrix-only; resolves to the MXID's homeserver suffix, empty for non-Matrix providers; `normalizeUserLimits` warns at config-load when `{homeserver}` is used but the Matrix provider is not enabled). The counter is keyed per `(rendered key, model-scope, window, room-scope, space-scope)`. The **matcher** (`user`/`room`/`space` globs, OR within a dimension, AND across; `space` matches if **any** parent space matches — a room may belong to several) scopes *whose* spend a rule counts; the **partition** scopes *which meter* it shares. **Space matching (§11)** extends `matrix-core`'s `MatrixChannelInfo` with `parent_space_ids` (all spec-legitimate parents — Reciprocal over WithPowerlevel, best-first — via the NAPI `channelInfo`), resolved + frozen on the session at admission **only when a rule references space** (`UserLimitEngine.usesSpace`, so space-less deployments pay no lookup); the canonical (first) parent is the `{space_id}` partition value + the denormalized `space_id` ledger column. A constraint whose partition renders to a value common to several users is a **group budget**: ANDed with each user's personal constraints, so when the shared pool binds, every member fails affordability on the premium model *at once* and **degrades together** rather than the agent being denied. A shared-pool constraint whose partition **variable resolves empty** (e.g. `space:{space_id}` on a space-less room — no parent space) keys no meaningful meter, so `resolveConstraint` **skips it entirely** (mirroring how an empty `space` *match* skips the rule); space-less rooms are **not** pooled together under a bare `space:` bucket, and the rule's fungible total / other non-empty constraints still apply. A rule may declare **multiple** shared pools (up to `MAX_SHARED_POOLS_PER_RULE`): each event's first pool key is denormalized on `budget_partition` and any additional keys spill to the `usage_event_partitions` child table, reseeding via the scalar+child union (see the ledger §8f note above); per-user meters reseed off `trigger_sender_id`. A malformed partition template (a lone unmatched `{`/`}`) is a normalizer **fatal** — it can never silently degrade to a literal global pool.

**Cost identity under fallback (§7): gate on virtual, count actual, estimate on face.** A sub-cap scoped to `opus-premium` owns all spend under that assignment regardless of fallback — so the per-user counters scope on the **requested** model (`requested_model_id`), the cost summed is the **actual served** cost, and the estimate prices the **requested head's** face cost (conservative, since chains are non-increasing in cost). This is why per-user selection stays a distinct axis from the MODEL-FALLBACK outage chain: an outage of `opus-premium` first fails over within its own chain (same-tier alternate), and the per-user preference order only catches it as a last resort once the inner chain is exhausted.

**A sub-cap bounds agent-loop model SELECTION only — never tool usage of the same upstream model.** Tool spend has no requested model, so it counts the user's fungible **total** and any shared **pool** (both model-agnostic) but is excluded from every model-scoped sub-cap — both in memory (`recordUsageEvent` passes no coverage model for a `class = 'tool'` event, so `UserLimitEngine.record` skips sub-caps) and in the ledger reseed (the `requestedModelIds` null-fallback in `usageCostClauses` is gated to `class = 'agent_loop'`, so a null-requested tool row never seeds a sub-cap). The consequence is deliberate: once a user exhausts their sub-cap on model X, the agent loop degrades **off** X (the sub-cap's purpose — reserving cheaper-continuation headroom), but tools hardwired to X (e.g. `x_search`→Grok) keep using X, bounded only by the user's total / pools. Charging a sub-cap for tool spend that merely shares a model name would be an over-restriction footgun (it does not serve the sub-cap's degradation-headroom purpose). Attribution is preserved regardless: a tool row keeps its own `model_id`/`logical_model_id` for §8e per-model aggregates and console top-models, and still draws down the total (by sender) and pools (by partition).

**Enforcement.** *Gate A* (`resolveUserLimitGate`, before the §8e gate): build `ctx = { userId, roomId }` from `inbound`, resolve the per-field cascade (the model-budget block cascades atomically; `trigger_rejection_message` independently), refuse a banned / fully-out-of-budget user with the optional templated reply (richer tokens than §8e: `{display_name}` from the inbound sender, `{limit}`/`{window}`/`{resets_at}`/`{resets_in}`), pick the initial model (so §8e gates on the **selected** model's chain, not the default), freeze the resolution on the session, and tighten the §8d ceiling to `min(static, user-total-headroom)`. Gate A is shared by **every** human agent-loop entry point — a fresh launch (`launchSession`), a reply / follow-up **resume** (`runResumeSession`), and the manual console **recovery** of a parked session (`resumeSessionRun`) — because a resume is the same event from the per-user view (same replying user/room/budget), merely continuing an existing session; only proactive/background paths skip it. *Gate B* (`checkCostBudget`, per request inside `factory.create`): the factory builds **one `BuiltModelFallback` per preferred model** at create; before every request the resolver re-picks the first model satisfying **affordable ∧ healthy ∧ fits** (each a cheap per-model check — affordability vs the live partitioned counter, health via `chooseChainMember` over the model's own chain, fits vs its operative context window), stashes it + the budget-derived `max_tokens` for the outer-selector `StreamFn`, and terminates (content-class, no retry burn) only when **no** preference qualifies — so budget *and* outage degradation both happen transparently mid-rollout instead of guillotining the session. When the capability pre-filter empties the whole preference set (an image-bearing trigger whose **reply model** ships pixels, §8a, against an all-text-only user model set — so no preferred model can serve the pixels), that is itself a **terminal per-user content-class deny** (spec §4.2) — never a fall-through to the ungated session-type default; the session still launches so per-user counting and the §8d ceiling apply, but the first request denies. The initial pre-pick also seeds a **defensive `max_tokens`** on the first selection (the first model's affordable cap at a ≈0-context estimate, mirroring Gate A) so that even if the per-request pre-flight throws — `withRequestRetry` swallows it — request 1 ships capped rather than uncapped. A budget-capped (`stopReason: "length"`) turn is **failed, not delivered** (§5.4): its real spend is recorded, then `request-retry.ts`'s `onBudgetTruncation` hook re-selects the next-cheaper model and re-issues — discarding the truncated content so the rollout finishes a *complete* turn on the degraded model instead of shipping a half answer (the re-drive is bounded by the preference-set size and wired only for per-user sessions; a `length` stop is read as a budget cap vs a legitimate long answer by comparing the issued cap against the **requested** model's own `max_tokens` — not the served fallback member's — and a stop at the requested model's full `max_tokens`, or with no cheaper model left, is delivered normally). Recording is centralized in `recordUsageEvent`: it increments every covering meter (agent loop **and** tool lane) for the session's frozen resolution, keyed on the requested model, by the actual cost — live for fresh, resumed, and recovered sessions alike.

**Meter seeding (§14).** Per-user meter keys depend on the runtime trigger sender / room / parent space, so — unlike the fixed `[[limits]]` rule set `BudgetEngine` seeds in its constructor — they can't be enumerated from config. `UserLimitEngine.seedFromLedger()` (run once at startup before `start()`, wired in `app.ts`) instead reads the distinct spend identities present in the current windows (`Storage.listUsageIdentities` — non-null `trigger_sender_id` only, `DISTINCT` narrowed to the room/space dimensions any rule actually uses via `usesRoom`/`usesSpace`) and **replays each through the same `resolve()`→`meterFor()` path the live gates use**, so the meterKey/seed logic is identical (no drift) and each meter re-sums its true accumulated spend. Zero-spend meters (an unused sub-cap, a cap-0 ban) are then pruned — only *consumed* budget is pre-materialized; enforcement is unaffected (bans still gate at `resolve`, not from a meter). Seeding (and the grouped console view) also **skip non-human senders** via `isEnforceableUser` — a partition is only surfaced when the injected `isUserIdentity` predicate accepts the sender id (today: `id.startsWith("@")`, the Matrix-only case) AND it is not one of the agent's own account ids (`selfUserIds`, built from `config.matrix.accounts` at startup — the provider `getSelf()` API is not available at this pre-`start()` seeding point, so the Matrix config remains the authoritative source for the self-id set here). Per-user limits govern only human triggers that pass Gate A; proactive / background / self / system sessions skip it (§8g) and never `record` to a per-user meter at runtime, so the bot's own account and non-MXID system senders (e.g. `"system"`) must not appear as rate-limited "users" — the seed's ledger re-sum would otherwise re-materialize them. The one gap: a shared pool whose window spend is exclusively autonomous (no human trigger, e.g. proactive-only) re-materializes on the next human turn that resolves it, not at startup. Without this seed a partition partially consumed before a restart read $0 until a live turn lazily re-created its meter.

**Console (§14).** `GET /api/usage/budgets` stays a small always-polled payload: the §8e `rules` **and** `userSelections` (each live per-user session's currently-selected model) — both few. The **unbounded** per-user / shared-pool meters move to a **paginated, grouped** endpoint so the view scales to any number of users: `UserLimitEngine.groupedStatuses()` folds every materialized meter into partition groups, splits them into **individuals** (👤 per-user) vs **shared pools** (👥), and sorts each side **hottest-first** (worst state, then peak fill, then key — the *same* order the client's `buildLadder` renders in, so a page is a contiguous top-slice); `GET /api/usage/user-limits?scope=individuals|shared&page=N` returns one page (all meters for the page's partitions, `USER_LIMITS_PAGE_SIZE = 20` **groups** per page) plus both scope counts (`totals`, for the tab badges + pager). The "Per-user limits" section renders a **scope tab** (Individuals / Shared pools) over partition **strips** (`console/src/lib/user-ladder.ts`, pure + unit-tested): one strip per partition, each cap its own bar. A user strip's header renders the sender as **"Display Name (handle)"** — the handle is the Discord unique username when resolved, else the raw partition key (for Matrix the key IS the human-meaningful MXID) — truncating long names with the full label + raw id on a hover tooltip; the labels ride on the meters (BFF-resolved per page via `Storage.getUserLabels`, see the endpoint table) and a sender with no known name falls back to the bare id exactly as before. The live `userSelections` chips carry the same labels. A **single-model** cap is a **health-colored** bar (green/amber/red by its own fill; an over-cap bar clamps to 100% and labels the true `>100%`) — the red bar *is* the exhausted cap. A **composite** cap (≥2 models, e.g. `sol + terra`) is a **segmented** bar so its composition stays visible — one slice per member (per-member spend read from the sibling single-model caps), colored from a **non-health palette** (blues/purples/teals) so model identity never masquerades as health; the composite's own fill health shows via its percent label. Caps order by `orderIndex`, the backend's **preference-order key** (`ResolvedConstraint.orderKey` = `maxPrefIndex*1000 + memberCount` over the rule's `models` list) — singles at their model's preference position, a composite right after its last member (e.g. `sol, terra, sol + terra, glm`), **not** by fill % or authored constraint index. There is deliberately **no per-user aggregate state badge** — a maxed sub-cap means "degrade to the next tier", not "user blocked", so a single worst-of badge (over a bar showing a different cap) misleads; each cap speaks for itself. The tab + `Prev`/`Next` pager change the query's scope/page so each slice refetches. The **global `[[limits]]`** section reuses the same composite treatment: a multi-model rule (e.g. `openai-56-daily` = `sol,terra`) carries a per-model breakdown in `RuleStatus.components` (a ledger `sumUsageCost` per constituent model, off the hot path in `ruleStatuses`), which the console renders as the identical segmented bar + color legend (`buildSegments`, shared). Meters are ledger-seeded at startup (above), so a partition partially consumed before a restart shows immediately rather than reading $0. A refused human trigger emits `usage_limit_blocked` with a `gate: "user_admission" | "user_preflight" | "user_resume"` discriminator.

---

## 9. Context Assembly

This is the most critical subsystem. It reads the timeline and renders it into token-budgeted, deterministically structured LLM context.

### Context layout

```
[System prompt]                    — assembled from workspace files + skills (AgentState.systemPrompt)
[Compact tier messages]            — simplified single-line format (user/assistant turns)
[Rich tier messages]               — full XML with structured metadata (user/assistant turns)
[Final user turn]                  — <system> satellite block + trigger group messages (rich XML) + image blocks
```

Two optional layers sit between the system prompt and the compact tier (omitted from generation/summarization builds): the **recent-diary layer** (§9c read side) immediately after the system prompt, then the **summary layer** (§9b) — so the full top-to-bottom order is system → diary → summaries → compact → rich → final.

The system prompt is assembled from workspace markdown files (`AGENTS.md`, `SOUL.md`, `TOOLS.md`) and the skills index at session creation time (see §9a). It is set via `AgentState.systemPrompt` and handled by pi-agent-core separately from the message array. All historical chat events are rendered as their original role (user messages as user turns, assistant messages as assistant turns). The final user turn contains (when present and in this order) the §9d auto-retrieval `<retrieved_memory>` block, then the satellite block wrapped in `<system>` tags (runtime state, tail instructions, session-specific instruction), then the trigger group messages rendered in rich XML, with multimodal image blocks appended when applicable. Auto-retrieval rides here — inside the already-volatile final turn — so it costs the prompt cache nothing (§9d).

`build()` is called **once per session, at creation**, and is the single source of truth for the prefix. The factory pops the final user turn off the build output and delivers it as the first turn of the live transcript; the prefix above it is frozen and append-only (see §8, "Frozen context"). `build()` itself is unchanged and is also what the observability console's room-preview endpoint calls to render "what the next session would see."

### Timeline-to-turns rendering

The timeline is a flat event stream. The context builder renders it into LLM-compatible alternating user/assistant turns:

- Consecutive same-role messages are merged into one turn, separated by `\n\n---\n\n`
- Turns spanning the compact/rich boundary get tier `"mixed"`
- Role alternation is enforced at the output layer, not at the compaction layer

### Rich message format (XML)

Messages in the rich tier use structured XML. The `sender` attribute carries the **human-facing handle** — `username ?? id` (`SenderInfo.username` when present, otherwise the raw provider id). For Matrix this is the MXID (username is unset, so behaviour is byte-identical to before); for Discord this is the stable `@username` handle rather than the snowflake. The `display_name` attribute carries the freely-mutable room/guild nickname, emitted **only when it differs from the handle** — if displayName equals `username ?? id` it is suppressed as redundant. The `external_id` attribute is the sole place the raw provider id (MXID / snowflake) appears in the rendered context. Reply context is rendered **before** the message body. Display names and filenames are truncated to 256 chars, URLs to 2048.

Escaping is split by position (`src/context/xml.ts`). Attribute values use `escapeAttr`, which escapes `&`, `<`, `>`, and the `"` delimiter. Element content (message bodies, captions, summaries, reply snippets, reaction lines, etc.) uses `escapeXml`, which escapes only the structurally significant `&`, `<`, `>` and leaves `"`/`'` verbatim — quotes carry no special meaning in XML character data, so escaping them only spent tokens and obscured ordinary chat text (`he said &quot;hi&quot;`) with no injection-safety gain: breaking out of a `<message>…</message>` envelope still requires `<` or `&`, both escaped.

```xml
<message sender="@alice:server.org" display_name="Alice" time="2026-05-22T12:00:00.000Z" mentions_you="true" external_id="$evt">
<reply_to sender="@bob:server.org" display_name="Bob" time="2026-05-22T11:59:00.000Z" external_id="$parent">
Parent message body

<attachment filename="chart.png" type="image/png" size="50000" path="/local/path"/>

<linked_media filename="diagram.png" type="image/png" path="/local/path"/>
</reply_to>

Message body text

<attachment filename="photo.jpg" type="image/jpeg" size="123456" path="/local/path" image_block="true">
[caption: A scenic landscape photograph]
</attachment>

<attachment filename="card.png" type="image/png" size="80000" path="/local/path" is_character_card="true" card_name="Miku"/>

<linked_media filename="reference.jpg" type="image/jpeg" path="/local/path">
[caption: A reference image]
</linked_media>

<link_preview url="https://example.com" title="Example Article">
Article description text

<preview_media filename="og-image.jpg" type="image/jpeg" path="/local/path"/>
</link_preview>

<reactions>👍×3 :blobwave:×1 😮×1</reactions>
</message>
```

Tag types:
- `<attachment>` — direct attachments on the message or reply
- `<linked_media>` — media URLs found in the message/reply body text (downloaded by enrichment)
- `<preview_media>` — media from link previews (OG images); replaces the earlier pattern of using `<attachment>` inside `<link_preview>`
- `image_block="true"` — marks the attachment whose image data is sent as a multimodal block (only present when model is multimodal)
- `is_character_card="true"` / `card_name="..."` — detected PNG character card metadata
- `<reactions>` — View A reaction counts (`display×count`, deduped by distinct sender). Rich tier only; the compact renderer omits it. See **§9f**.

An **unresolved** reply context (enrichment hasn't run yet, or resolution degraded to a stub row — see §7a) renders as `<reply_to external_id="$parent">` containing the literal placeholder `[original message unavailable]` instead of an empty quote block, so the model is told the context is missing rather than shown a blank quote.

**X.com previews** (`source_kind = "fx_twitter"` with a parseable `payload_json` — §7a "X.com enrichment via FxTwitter") branch into a structured, X-specific rendering instead of the flat form above; without a payload (failed fetch, legacy row) they fall back to the generic `<link_preview>`:

```xml
<link_preview url="https://x.com/FRIERENanime_/status/2065…" kind="x.com">
<tweet author="Frieren Daily" handle="@FRIERENanime_" time="2026-06-11 21:14" stats="12 replies · 340 retweets · 4,521 likes · 120,034 views">
Tweet text here…

<tweet_media kind="video" type="video/mp4" duration="18s" path="msg-attach/x-video.mp4">
[caption: An animated scene of …]
</tweet_media>

<quoted_tweet author="Other Person" handle="@other" time="2026-06-10 09:01" stats="…">
Quoted text…

<tweet_media kind="mosaic" photos="4" type="image/jpeg" path="msg-attach/x-mosaic.jpg" image_block="true">
[alt: per-photo alt texts when present]
[caption: A four-panel collage showing …]
</tweet_media>
</quoted_tweet>
</tweet>
</link_preview>
```

- Media tags render **inside the node they belong to** — provenance (main tweet vs. quoted tweet) is structural, not annotated. Both tweets are first-class; the quote nests one level.
- `kind` is `photo` / `mosaic` (`photos="N"` collage count) / `video` / `gif` / `video_thumbnail` (the fallback frame of a failed/oversize video). In individual-photos mode each photo gets `index="2/4"` so captions correlate positionally. Failed media slots render visibly as `<tweet_media kind="…" status="failed"/>`.
- Polls render as `<poll total_votes="…">` with one line per choice (incl. percentage); community notes as `<community_note>` — both inside the owning tweet node.
- Truncated text (`max_text_chars`, payload `textTruncated`) is followed by the literal hint `[truncated — full text available via the x_fetch tool]` — the model's signal that `x_fetch` (§10) has more.
- Search snippets keep using the flat `description` (the FTS source; unchanged plumbing).

### Compact message format (one-line)

```
[2026-05-22 12:00] Alice (@alice:server.org): Message body [attachment: photo.jpg /local/path caption=A scenic landscape] [linked_media: ref.jpg /local/path] [link: Title — description]
```

With a reply:

```
[2026-05-22 12:00] Alice (@alice:server.org)

(Replying to: > [From: Bob (@bob:server.org) At: 2026-05-22 11:59]: Parent message body)

: Message body [attachment: photo.jpg /local/path]
```

The reply block uses the format `\n\n(Replying to: > [From: DisplayName (userid) At: time]: reply body)\n\n`. Parentheses in display names are backslash-escaped to avoid breaking the `(Replying to: ...)` delimiters.

**Truncation limits**: body 6000 chars, reply body 4096 chars, attachment caption 300 chars, link description 1000 chars. Truncation appends `...`.

**X.com previews** truncate much earlier than the generic 1000-char link form — far too generous for aged-out messages. A preview with a parseable payload renders as one line with tweet text cut at **280** chars and quote text at **140** (renderer constants like the caps above, not config):

```
[tweet: Frieren Daily (@FRIERENanime_): "Tweet text…" · video: an animated scene of … · 1 photo | quoting Other Person (@other): "Quoted text…" · mosaic(4): a four-panel collage of …]
```
