Default is 5 MB base64 (≈ 3.75 MB raw), safely under Anthropic's per-image cap. `media.image.max_bytes` is deliberately excluded — that setting is the captioning pipeline's re-encode target (~1 MB) and capping `read_image` to it would reject any 1–3.75 MB workspace image. SVGs that embed a raster via `<image href="data:image/...">` are rejected before rasterization (same `containsEmbeddedRasterDataUri` gate the captioning pipeline uses); other SVGs are rasterized buffer-mode so librsvg cannot follow sibling-file references. Rejects directories and other non-regular files. Description directs the agent to save URLs to the workspace via the `media` tool rather than the (incorrect) `web_fetch`, which returns text/JSON, not bytes. |

### Shell tool

| Tool | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `bash` | Run a shell command inside the Docker sandbox container (`/bin/sh -lc`). Only registered when `[sandbox].enabled` is true (§11a) — there is no host-side fallback, because running arbitrary shell in the harness process would escape the workspace. Schema: `command`, optional `cwd` (workspace-relative, `..`-escapes rejected), optional `timeout_ms` (1–600000). `executionMode: "sequential"`. `timeout_ms` self-limits the command **inside** the container (coreutils `timeout` wraps it; §11a) and the per-call `AbortSignal` (steer/cancel) kills the in-container process group **out-of-band** — neither relies on SIGTERM of the local `docker exec` client, which does not propagate into the container. Output is byte-capped (`max_output_bytes`), decoded on a complete UTF-8 boundary (no `�` at the cut), and formatted as an `exit: N` header plus stdout/stderr, with `[output truncated]` / `(timed out)` markers. (PTY, background, and host/elevated execution modes from the OpenClaw exec tool are intentionally not ported in v1.) |

### Media tool

| Tool | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `media` | Analyze media (images, videos, audio) with a multimodal model. Accepts workspace paths or URLs (single via `media`, multiple via `media_items` up to 20). Auto-detects modality from MIME type, file extension, or file magic bytes (falls back to error if unrecognized). Optional `prompt` for targeted analysis, optional `start_time` (seconds) for video/audio to select the starting point of the processed segment. Routes to per-modality inference clients sharing the captioning model config. When the session's **reply model** has native vision, the description notes that images in the prompt are already visible. |

### Memory tools

| Tool | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `search_memory` | Ripgrep over `memory/*.md` files — exact string/regex match (a URL, an exact phrase). |
| `recall_memory` | Hybrid semantic+BM25 recall over `memory/*.md` (§9d) — natural-language `query`, ranked by relevance with temporal decay, optional `room`/`after`/`before` filters. Answers *"what do I remember about X"*; results are source-cited `memory/<file>.md:<lines>` so the agent opens the full block with the editor/bash. Registered for chat sessions only; only present when `[retrieval].enabled`. |
| `write_memory` | View/str_replace/insert on today's daily memory file (`memory/YYYY-MM-DD.md`). Auto-creates with header if absent. No `create` command — only the daily file pattern. Routed through the single-writer `MemoryFileWriter` (§9c) so chat edits serialize with the diary worker's appends. A successful mutation triggers a §9d reindex of the file. |

### Danbooru tool

| Tool | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `danbooru` | Search Danbooru posts with structured query parameters, preview a post (returns image block), or download an asset to workspace. Supports `includeTags`/`excludeTags` for structured tag queries, `includeRatings`/`excludeRatings` for rating filters, `order` for 85+ sort modes (with optional `default_order` config fallback when `order` is omitted), `page` for cursor/numeric pagination, `extraTerms` for advanced Danbooru metatags (whitespace-rejected, count against `max_regular_tags`, cannot duplicate structured `rating:`/`order:`/`limit:` fields). The `max_regular_tags` budget mirrors Danbooru's per-account tag limit, which counts the auto-appended `order:*` metatag as a tag (an anonymous account 422s on `2 tags + order:*`) but exempts `rating:*` — so the budget counts `includeTags + excludeTags + extraTerms + (order ? 1 : 0)` and rating filters are free; a search exceeding it is rejected locally with a clear message rather than 422-ing at Danbooru. HTTP errors surface Danbooru's JSON `message`/`error` body (e.g. the tag-limit reason), not a bare status code. `preview` is **vision-aware** (keyed on the **session's reply model** — the serving session-type model, not `[models.default]`). When the reply model has vision, it runs fetched bytes through `conditionImageBufferForInference` so the inline base64 emission always lands under the reply model's `image_input_bytes` cap (measured as base64 bytes; the budget is converted to a raw-output target via `raw = floor(base64 * 3 / 4)` before being passed to the conditioning loop) — independent of the larger `downloadSizeLimit` hard fetch ceiling. When the model has **no** vision, `preview` must not emit an image block the model can't read (nor narrate one as if it could): it routes the fetched asset through the shared **image captioning client** (the same `InferenceClient` backing the `media` tool) and returns the caption text plus asset URLs and a pointer to the `media` tool for specific follow-up questions — the non-vision equivalent of handing the image to a model that can see. The non-vision default variant is `sample` (the 180px `preview` thumbnail captions poorly); the vision default stays `preview`. If captioning is unconfigured, it degrades to URLs only (no fetch). Tool/preview descriptions and the result text adapt to the vision flag. Result text across search/preview/download is terse (final query + per-post lines, or caption + URLs) — the prior step-by-step narration was dropped as wasted tokens; the structured query breakdown remains in the tool-call `details` for the console. `download` writes the raw fetched bytes to disk using `fs.open(path, "wx")` exclusive create (collision suffixes `-1`..`-100`, then a random nonce). Explicit `filename` extensions are allow-listed (jpg/jpeg/png/gif/webp/mp4/webm/zip/swf); anything else falls back to the post's `file_ext`. Auth via config-level `login`/`api_key` (Basic auth) — refused at tool-construction if `base_url` is plaintext `http://`. Both the JSON API fetches (30s `AbortController` timeout + 4 MiB response cap + identifying `User-Agent`) and the binary asset downloads (via `FetchClient`) route through the shared `guardedFetch` (SSRF + unconditional 429/503 backoff) and are paced by a **tool-owned `DanbooruRateLimiter`** spanning the API + CDN hosts as one account-level budget (`min_request_interval_ms`/`max_in_flight`, defaults 500 ms / 2 in flight) — the generic per-host limiter can't model that single-budget-across-two-hosts shape. The limiter is race-free under concurrency: each acquirer **reserves its start instant synchronously** (next free slot in the pacing schedule, so concurrent callers can't read the same stale "last start" and begin 0 ms apart), and slot admission is **FIFO with direct handoff** — `release` transfers the slot to the head waiter without decrementing the in-flight count, so a fresh caller can never double-grant a freed slot past `max_in_flight` or overtake queued waiters. Both paths honor `[network] http_proxy_url` via a shared undici `ProxyAgent` dispatcher when configured. Search results surface `createdAt`/`fileSize`/`source` per post (source is uploader-controlled, exposed as data only) plus the post's **`characters:`/`series:` tags** (the curated identity labels — the signal for picking the right result) ahead of the general descriptive tags; the search line's character list is capped (`+N more`) since it is a multi-post scan view. `preview` (both the vision image-block path and the non-vision caption path) returns the **full** "character first" tag block — character → copyright → artist → a capped slice of general → meta (the identity categories uncapped) — so the model has Danbooru's authoritative "who/what is in this image" labels without a second `media` round-trip; the per-category tags are also emitted under `details.tags`. Tags are uploader/community-controlled text, surfaced verbatim as data only (never fed to a fetcher), the same as `source`. **Tag suggestion ("did you mean")** closes the common failure where the model guesses a tag that doesn't exist (e.g. `mordred_pendragon_(fate)` when the real tag is `mordred_(fate)`): a `resolveTagSuggestions` engine resolves a free-text guess/keyword into real, ranked tag names by hitting Danbooru's `/autocomplete.json` (`search[type]=tag_query`, alias-aware) and, as a backstop, `/tags.json` (`name_matches=*token*&order=count`). It first autocompletes the whole normalized query; if that finds no exact tag it autocompletes each significant **word** (a guess like `mordred_pendragon` autocompletes to nothing as a whole because "pendragon" isn't in the real tag, but each word resolves on its own); if still empty it runs a substring wildcard. Candidates are deduped (deprecated tags dropped), then ranked by how many query words each contains (so `mordred_(fate)` beats a generic high-count `fate_(series)`) then by post count, capped at `max_suggestions` (default 6). The engine is exposed two ways, with the zero-result path as the primary one (it costs no extra tool call — the search the agent already ran surfaces the correction): a **zero-result `search`** (when `suggest_on_empty`, default true, and positive tags were given) auto-resolves each `includeTag` (capped at 4) and appends a "Did you mean?" block; and **`action: "tags"`** with a `query` param is a secondary, explicit lookup that resolves a guess into real tags without searching (for browsing/disambiguating candidates). Tool and skill guidance steer the agent to search-first rather than spend a round-trip looking a tag up speculatively. In the recovery block each tag is "valid" when it appears among its own suggestions (so a real tag is never "corrected"), and when *every* supplied tag is valid the output instead notes the empty result is a *combination*/filter problem, not a typo. The recovery's suggestion requests share the tool-owned `DanbooruRateLimiter` budget like every other call, and a failure in the (best-effort) suggestion path never turns a successful-but-empty search into a tool error. The structured recovery report is also surfaced under `details.suggestions` for the console. Config: `[danbooru]` section (`suggest_on_empty`, `max_suggestions`). |

### Find source tool

| Tool | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `find_source` | Reverse-image search via **SauceNAO** (`src/tools/find-source.ts`; spec SAUCENAO-SOURCE-LOOKUP) — the **inverse** of `danbooru` (`danbooru` goes tags → images; this goes image → source URL + artist). Takes one `image` param: a workspace-relative path (resolved + traversal-rejected like `read_image`, then read and **conditioned** through `conditionImageBufferForInference` to `max_image_bytes` ≈ 4 MB) **or** an http(s) URL (scheme-validated, passed to SauceNAO as `url=` for server-side fetch — no local read). Calls `GET/POST {base_url}/search.php` via the shared `guardedFetch` (SSRF + per-host 429/503 backoff) with `api_key`, `output_type=2`, `db` (default 999 = all indexes), `numres`=`limit`, `minsim`=`min_similarity`; an upload rides a multipart `file` field (FormData → undici sets the boundary) while the rest stay query params, a URL is a plain GET. Honors `[network] http_proxy_url` via a shared undici dispatcher and bounds the call with a `timeout_ms` `AbortController` composed with the agent abort signal. **Account-quota rate limiting (§4-style):** a process-wide **`SauceNaoRateLimiter`** (`src/saucenao/rate-limiter.ts`, constructed once at app startup, injected into every session — the SauceNAO free quota is per-account/global, so a per-session limiter wouldn't bound it) enforces a sliding short window (`short_window_max`/`short_window_ms`, default ~6/30s); `acquire` waits up to `max_wait_ms` for a slot, else returns a **soft** "try again shortly" result (not an error) so the agent self-throttles instead of blocking the turn. After each response the limiter is **reconciled** from SauceNAO's authoritative `short_remaining` (and the daily `long_remaining` is surfaced, not enforced). Composes with — and is orthogonal to — the per-host HTTP limiter and the SSRF guard. **Status handling:** `header.status < 0` → tool error with SauceNAO's message; `> 0` → results returned with a "partial (some indexes unavailable)" note; HTTP 429 → soft rate-limited result with remaining counts. Results are normalized (tolerant — every field optional), dropped below `min_similarity` (default 55), sorted by perceptual-**similarity %** desc, capped to `limit` (≤ `max_results_limit`). **Output is verification-centric and vision-adaptive:** the text always leads each candidate with its **similarity %** (the identity signal — ≥ ~80% = same image, labelled strong/plausible/weak) plus source type (`index_name`), artist (`member_name`/`author_name`), title, source link(s) (`ext_urls`, http(s)-filtered), notable ids (`pixiv_id`/`danbooru_id`/…/`source`, surfaced verbatim as data only), and the matched thumbnail URL, with a quota header line. By deliberate design SauceNAO thumbnails are **never auto-captioned** (the divergence from danbooru's non-vision caption path): a generic caption of a thumbnail does not establish same-image identity — the similarity score does, far more reliably. On a **vision** model, `view: true` additionally inlines the top matched thumbnails (capped at `view_max_blocks`) as image blocks — fetched via `FetchClient`, conditioned to the same per-model `image_input_bytes` base64 cap as `read_image`, with a graceful fall-back to the URL on fetch/condition failure — so the model can *see* the candidate and confirm it matches the query image it can also see. On a **non-vision** model `view` is ignored with a note ("relying on similarity score"); the skill guides verification via the existing `media` tool on the source URL instead. **Ephemeral** — output lives in the session rollout only; nothing is persisted. Construction throws if `api_key` is missing (matches `image_generate`), but the app never reaches that path without a key: `[saucenao].enabled` is **default ON** for out-of-box capability, yet the tool is registered only when enabled **and** a non-empty `api_key` is present (the operative `sauceNaoConfig`/`sauceNaoEnabled` gate at app wiring). Enabled **without** a key is a **graceful soft-disable** — app wiring logs one `saucenao_disabled_no_api_key` warning and simply does not construct/register `find_source` (no crash, so a key-less public deploy starts cleanly). Config: `[saucenao]` (§4). Persona docs: TOOLS.md/AGENTS.md + the `saucenao` skill. |

### X fetch tool

| Tool | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `x_fetch` | Fetch a tweet on demand via the FxTwitter API (§7a "X.com enrichment via FxTwitter") — the generic `web_fetch` is blocked by X server-side. The model's manual path for (a) reading tweets whose preview text was `[truncated]`, (b) tweets that never went through enrichment (e.g. URLs found elsewhere), and (c) **individual tweet media** — including the photos behind an enrichment mosaic. Accepts any recognized X/FxTwitter status URL or a bare numeric status id. Builds one canonical text document — header (author, handle, time, stats), **full** tweet text, poll/community-note blocks, a **numbered media listing** (index, kind, dimensions/duration, alt text, direct URL), then the quoted tweet in the same layout (indices continue across the quote) — bounded by `tool.max_total_chars` (default 32768) and returned as the `[offset, offset + max_chars)` window (`max_chars` default `tool.default_max_chars` 4000, hard cap `tool.max_chars_limit` 16000); `details` carries `{ totalChars, nextOffset, truncated }` and the window text ends with `[truncated — continue with offset=N]` when more remains. Media access escalates by listing index: `download_media` (indices or `"all"`) saves the originals (photos at full resolution, video/GIF mp4s) under `downloads/x/{statusId}/` via `FetchClient` (exclusive-create with collision suffixes), returning workspace-relative paths for `read_image`/`send_message`; `view_media` (indices or `"all"`, clamped to `tool.max_view_blocks` per call with a note) returns items inline as image blocks — photos directly, videos/GIFs as their thumbnail frame (a video cannot be an image block) — conditioned through `conditionImageBufferForInference` under the same per-model `image_input_bytes` base64 cap as `read_image`/danbooru `preview`. A fully **ephemeral** tool: output lives in the session rollout only — no enrichment rows, no captioning; downloads are ordinary workspace files. All fetches ride the shared `FxTwitterClient`/`FetchClient` (guardedFetch, proxy, `media.download_size_limit`). API errors (404/private/suspended) surface FxTwitter's message; out-of-range media indices error naming the valid range. Registered only when `[fxtwitter.tool].enabled` (independent of the enrichment enable). Config: `[fxtwitter.tool]` (§4); cross-field `default_max_chars <= max_chars_limit <= max_total_chars` validated fail-fast at app wiring. |

### Image generation tool

| Tool | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `image_generate` | Generate or edit an image with Google's Gemini "nano banana" models, via the Gemini API base (`GEMINI_BASE_URL`; an operator gateway may proxy it). One `prompt` generates from text; passing `images` (1–6 workspace paths or http(s) URLs) switches to **edit mode** — the same `:generateContent` endpoint, with each reference prepended as an `inlineData` part before the text instruction. `model` selects `pro` (`gemini-3-pro-image`, default — best quality + text rendering) or `flash` (`gemini-3.1-flash-image`, faster; also allows `image_size:"512"`). Optional `aspect_ratio` and `image_size` map to `generationConfig.imageConfig.{aspectRatio,imageSize}` (aspect honored on 3.x models, size loosely). The request **always sets a high `maxOutputTokens` (default 32768)** — Gemini emits the image *as output tokens* (~1290 for 1K), and many gateways default the cap low, so without this every call returns HTTP 200 with no image (`finishReason:"MAX_TOKENS"`). Auth is `Authorization: Bearer ${api_key}` (when an operator gateway fronts Google it injects real Google creds; Google's native `x-goog-api-key` is not used here). The decoded image is saved into `<workspace>/<output_subdir>/` (default `generated-images/`) via `fs.open(path,"wx")` exclusive create (collision suffixes `-1`..`-100`, then a random nonce); the tool returns the workspace-relative path plus an inline preview block conditioned via `conditionImageBufferForInference` to fit the per-image `image_input_bytes` cap. **Delivery is two-step**: the tool never posts to Matrix — the model must call `send_message` with `media` set to the returned path. Reference-image URLs route through `FetchClient`, which applies the shared egress guard (private/metadata block + per-hop redirect revalidation when enabled — see "Egress guard & SSRF protection" above), so a public URL can't 302 to a private/metadata host. Each reference (URL or workspace path) is bounded to a 6 MiB per-image budget: under-budget refs are sent as their original bytes + detected MIME untouched (preserving editing quality), and only over-budget refs are run through `conditionImageBufferForInference` (resize + JPEG) to cap tokens/memory. The generation POST itself also routes through `guardedFetch` (per-host backoff + SSRF), and honors `[network] http_proxy_url`. Output MIME is whatever the response's image part declares (`inlineData.mimeType`, falling back to `image/png`); the on-disk extension is derived from that MIME, constrained to a vetted allow-list (`png`/`jpg`/`webp`/`gif`, default `png`). All outputs carry a SynthID watermark. Not registered unless `[image_gen]` is configured. Config: `[image_gen]` section. Persona docs: TOOLS.md + the `image-gen` skill. |
| `x_search` | Search X.com (Twitter) via **Grok as a discovery sub-agent** (`src/tools/x-search.ts`). One OpenRouter chat-completions POST (`{base_url}/chat/completions`, reusing `OPENROUTER_BASE_URL` + `LLM_API_KEY` — no new credential/egress host) sends a fixed system scaffold (force a live cited search; never fabricate) + the agent's `query`, with `plugins:[{id:"web"}]` (Grok's `x_search` **and** auxiliary `web_search` server-side tools) and a top-level `x_search_filter` carrying `allowed_x_handles` **xor** `excluded_x_handles` (max 10 each, validated), `from_date`/`to_date` (YYYY-MM-DD), and the **corpus-vision** flags `enable_image_understanding` / `enable_video_understanding` (both **config-only, default on** — they let Grok analyze images/video it encounters *during* the search; deliberately **not** model-facing knobs). `effort` (`fast`→`model`, `deep`→`deep_model`) picks the Grok tier. The agent may also attach up to **4 `images`** (workspace paths **or** http(s) URLs) — each loaded to a base64 `data:` URL (URLs via the shared `fetchClient` + `download_size_limit`; local paths confined by `resolveWorkspacePath` + `stat`-capped; MIME sniffed from content-type/magic-bytes→extension) and appended as `image_url` blocks on the user turn, so the user `content` becomes a **multimodal array** (`[{text}, {image_url}…]`; text-only stays a scalar string). This gives Grok **vision over the attached image** so it can recognize and search X/web for a media's subject — a **fallback for sourcing media when `find_source`/SauceNAO comes up empty** (visual recognition + text search, *not* a true reverse-image match). A failed image load is a **hard error** (the model is told its image never reached Grok — no silent degrade); attached image refs partition the short-TTL cache key. Grok returns a prose synthesis + citation URLs (extracted tolerantly from message `annotations` of type `url_citation` **and/or** a top-level `citations` array). The tool then **grounds** that output with miku's own stack: it `parseXStatusUrl`s the citations (keeping real `x.com/.../status/…`, deduped by id), re-fetches the first `hydrate` (default `hydrate_default` 5, cap `hydrate_max`) through the shared **`FxTwitterClient`** (verbatim text + numbered media — §7a), and **inline-captions** the first `caption_top` (default 4) cited **images** via the image caption model — the exact `media`-tool path (`InferenceClient.caption({…, context:"tool"})`); remaining/video media are listed with a one-line "call the `media` tool" hint. A citation that fails to parse or fetch is **dropped and counted** (anti-hallucination signal in the coverage line — no silent truncation). Both Grok's synthesis and every hydrated tweet body are externally-sourced and **wrapped untrusted** (`<untrusted_x_search source="grok-synthesis|tweet" …>`, bodies via `escapeXml`, attrs via `escapeAttr` — a tweet quoting the closing tag can't forge the boundary); structural strings the tool emits (indices, dates, the coverage line, captions we generated) are trusted and unwrapped. Output is a synthesis block + a Sources block (per-citation index, `@handle`, date, canonical URL, windowed verbatim, captioned/listed media) + a coverage line (`Grok cited N posts; hydrated H, D unreachable (dropped); captioned C images`). **Ephemeral, like `x_fetch`** — session rollout only; nothing is written to `media_assets`/`link_previews` (a tweet Grok surfaced was never posted in-channel, so persisting it would fabricate lived context — §5). **Billed as a sub-agent (§8c):** the Grok call (`parseOpenAiUsage` → `[x_search.cost]`, `ref:"grok"`) **and** each inline caption (`ref:"caption:<statusId>"`) each append a `tool_invocations` row and feed the per-session cost ceiling (§8d) — this gives tool-context captions a durable home. **Concurrency:** the Grok call goes to OpenRouter (a different provider lane than the agent loop), so it is **not** admitted through `LlmScheduler` — only its per-host `guardedFetch` slot + a wall-clock `timeout_ms` (default 60s, composed with the agent abort signal); captions ride the caption client's own scheduler admission. Every failure path degrades gracefully (Grok error/timeout, no citations, per-item hydration/caption failure) — never throws. A short-TTL in-memory cache (`cache_ttl_minutes`, default 10, keyed on normalized query+filters+model+attached-image-refs) dampens duplicate calls to one Grok synthesis (hydration/captioning always re-run; `details.cached` reports hits). Use for **discovery** ("what's X saying about…", "find posts from @… about…"); use `x_fetch` for a known URL. Registered only when `[x_search].enabled` (default true when the block exists). Config: `[x_search]` (§4). Persona docs: TOOLS.md/AGENTS.md/TAIL.md. |

### User profile tools

| Tool | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `user_profile_read` | Read a user profile from workspace. Profiles are YAML-frontmatter markdown files under `users/<provider>/` keyed by provider and sender identity hash. Supports reading specific sections or the full document, with configurable excerpt length. Auto-resolves the requesting user's identity via context closure (provider, senderId, displayName). Legacy path discovery migrates profiles from older Matrix-specific paths. |
| `user_profile_edit` | Edit a user profile via patch operations: `set_section` (replace a named section), `append_section` (add content to a section), `delete_section`, `set_frontmatter` (update metadata). Creates profiles on first edit with standardized frontmatter. Validates section names against the canonical set (no `#` or newlines). Writes are serialized per profile via an in-process mutex and committed via temp-file + `rename` for atomicity. `metadata.version` is bumped monotonically on every save. Section bodies containing lines that start with `# ` are escaped (zero-width-space prefix) on write so the heading parser does not partition them on the next read. Config: `[user_profiles]` section. |

**Lookup safety**: profile lookup is scoped to direct children of `users/<provider>/` only. Files in sibling directories (e.g. `users/notes/`) and in deeper subdirectories (e.g. `users/matrix/sub/foo.md`) are ignored even if their frontmatter matches — this prevents workspace-planted markdown from hijacking lookups, and bounds the scan cost to the immediate provider directory. The legacy flat layout (`users/<localpart>__<homeserver>.md`) is still honoured by exact-path probe for the one-time migration.

**Cross-user target policy**: `user_profiles.allow_cross_user_targets` (default `true`) gates explicit targets. When `false`, an explicit target whose (provider, sender_id) doesn't match the trigger sender is rejected. Set to `false` in deployments where the agent should never read or write a profile for a user other than the message-sender currently being responded to.

### Character card tools

| Tool | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `character_card_create` | Create a Character Card v2 PNG. Accepts character data fields and an image source (URL or workspace path). Embeds card JSON as PNG tEXt metadata using `character-card-utils`. Image is processed via `sharp` (resize, format conversion). Output path has collision avoidance. Config: `[character_card]` section. |
| `character_card_read` | Read a Character Card with bounded output. Modes: `summary` (character stats, field sizes, book entry index), `field_excerpt` (paginated view of a text field with line numbers), `book_index` (list lorebook entries with sizes), `export_text` (write a full text field to a workspace file), `export_card_json` (write normalized card JSON to workspace). Configurable excerpt window via `default_excerpt_chars`/`max_excerpt_chars`. |
| `character_card_edit` | Edit a Character Card via patch operations on text fields and lorebook entries. Operations: `set` (replace text), `str_replace` (find-and-replace), `append`, `prepend`, `delete_book_entry`, `add_book_entry`, `set_book_entry_keys`, `set_book_entry_enabled`. Re-embeds modified card into the PNG. |

**Path safety**: every card-reading code path (`character_card_read.path`, `character_card_create.imagePath`, edit `*_from_file` / `*SourcePath` operations) is routed through the same `normalizeWorkspaceOutputPath` + `assertPathInsideWorkspace` guards that the write paths already use. Absolute paths and `..` traversal are rejected — these tools cannot read outside the agent workspace.

**Untrusted content labeling**: `character_card_read`'s text-excerpt views (`field_excerpt`, `alternate_greeting_excerpt`, `book_entry_excerpt`) wrap externally-sourced card text in `<untrusted_card_field name="...">…</untrusted_card_field>` blocks (attribute escaped via `escapeAttr`, body via `escapeXml`). This mirrors the rich-XML message pattern in section 9: structural strings the tool emits itself (paths, counts, headings) are trusted; the card text that landed in those fields from external uploads is wrapped so the system prompt can teach the agent to treat content like `system_prompt` / `post_history_instructions` as data, not instructions.

**PNG hardening**: card decode/encode paths run a pre-check (`validatePngChunkSizes`) before invoking `png-chunks-extract`, rejecting any chunk whose declared length exceeds 16 MiB or whose record extends past the buffer. This blocks a DoS where a 100-byte hostile PNG declares a 4 GiB chunk length and triggers the library to allocate against the declaration before the CRC fails. Sharp pipelines apply the same format-aware `limitInputPixels` split as the captioning path (`SVG_MAX_INPUT_PIXELS` for SVG input, `RASTER_MAX_INPUT_PIXELS` for raster) so a legitimate large avatar isn't rejected by the tight SVG decode budget, and `imageUrl` fetches route through `FetchClient`'s shared egress guard to defeat SSRF to RFC1918 / metadata services. Sibling tEXt-chunk decode failures no longer abort `chara` lookups — only the `chara` chunk's own decode failure is fatal.

### MCP remote tools

Remote MCP (Model Context Protocol) HTTP servers provide additional tools without requiring custom tool implementations. Servers are defined in TOML config and tools are discovered at startup.

**Config**: `[mcp.servers.<name>]` with `url` (required), `transport` (`"streamable-http"` default or `"sse"`), and `headers` (key-value pairs, supports env var substitution).

**Shipped default — keyless Exa web tools**: `00-defaults.toml` ships `[mcp.servers.exa]` pointing at the public Exa endpoint (`https://mcp.exa.ai/mcp?tools=web_search_exa,web_search_advanced_exa,web_fetch_exa`), so a fresh deploy gets live web search/fetch out-of-box. The Exa public endpoint needs **no API key** (no `headers`/env var), so it never trips the loader's missing-env fail-fast. These Exa tools are the intended replacement for the native `web_search`/`web_fetch`, which are in `[agent].disabled_tools` by default — the generation session types reference `mcp_exa_web_fetch_exa` directly (§ generation sessions). Credentialed MCP servers (with an `Authorization` bearer `headers`) are added only in local config, where the secret lives in `.env`.

**Client pool**: `McpClientPool` in `src/mcp/client-pool.ts`. One `Client` per configured server. The initial connection (and `initialize` handshake) for every server happens at startup and is shared across all sessions. If a server fails to connect, it is logged and skipped — the agent starts without that server's tools (and they cannot be added later, since the agent's tool set is fixed at startup).

**Session recovery**: Streamable HTTP MCP sessions are *stateful* — the server mints an `Mcp-Session-Id` during `initialize` that the SDK replays on every request, and the SDK never re-initializes on its own. A server restart, session timeout, or a non-sticky load balancer routing to a replica that never saw the handshake makes the cached session stale, and every subsequent call returns `Bad Request: Server not initialized` (HTTP 400) or a 404. To keep a transient server blip from permanently disabling a server's tools, the pool supports reconnection:
- `getClient(name)` returns the **live** client; the adapter resolves it per call rather than capturing a reference, so a swapped-in client takes effect immediately.
- `reconnect(name)` closes the dead client and re-runs `connect` + `initialize` + `listTools`, swapping the fresh client and tool list into the entry. Concurrent reconnects for the same server are coalesced to one in-flight attempt. On failure the stale entry is left in place (so the next call retries) and `mcp_server_reconnect_failed` is logged; on success, `mcp_server_reconnected` (with `toolsChanged` if the discovered tool set differs).
- `isSessionTerminatedError()` classifies the lost-session signal narrowly (HTTP 404; HTTP 400 whose body mentions "not initialized"/"session"; or those phrases in a wrapped/SSE error). Generic 4xx/5xx, network blips (`ECONNRESET`), and ordinary tool failures are **not** matched — those leave the server-side session valid, so reconnecting (minting a new session) would be wrong.

**Tool adapter**: `adaptMcpTools()` in `src/mcp/tool-adapter.ts`. Each MCP tool definition is wrapped as an `AgentTool`:
- **Naming**: `mcp_<server>_<tool>` — the server key from TOML config is the namespace prefix. Compatible with the existing session-type `tools` allowlist.
- **Schema**: `Type.Unsafe(inputSchema)` wraps the MCP server's JSON Schema as a TypeBox TSchema. Client-side validation is skipped; the MCP server validates its own inputs.
- **Execution**: resolves the live client via `pool.getClient(server)`, then `client.callTool()` with `AbortSignal` propagation. If the call fails with a lost-session error (and was not aborted), the adapter calls `pool.reconnect(server)` and retries **once**; if reconnect fails it surfaces the original (more informative) session error. MCP `isError` responses are converted to thrown errors — they are not session failures, so they are not retried — which pi-agent-core converts to error tool results.
- **Content mapping**: MCP text and image content blocks map directly to `AgentToolResult` content. Unknown content types (including audio) are serialized as JSON text, since `AgentToolResult` only supports `TextContent | ImageContent`.

**Lifecycle**: Pool starts after factory creation, before provider subscription. Pool stops during shutdown after enrichment pool, before fetch client cleanup.

**Secret registration**: All header values are registered as secrets via `registerSecret()` during pool startup, ensuring auth tokens are redacted in logs regardless of header key name. This explicit registration is load-bearing — the config loader's key-name regex does not match common HTTP header names like `Authorization`.

**No SSRF validation**: MCP server URLs come from TOML config (a trusted source, not user input) and are not validated against internal/metadata IP ranges. This is intentional — unlike `web_fetch` which processes untrusted user-supplied URLs, MCP server URLs are operator-configured infrastructure endpoints.

---

## 11. Observability

### Structured logging

All log output is JSON, one object per line. Each record has: `time` (ISO 8601), `level`, `component` (dot-separated hierarchy like `mikuswarm.matrix`), `message` (stable snake_case identifier), plus arbitrary structured fields. Errors are always stringified at the call site.

Log levels: `debug` (10), `info` (20), `warn` (30), `error` (40). Minimum level from config.

All log output passes through the redaction registry — any registered secret is replaced with `[REDACTED]`.

The in-process observability API emits `console_request_completed` at debug level for
every request (`method`, pathname, status, `durationMs`) and promotes requests taking
at least 250 ms to the warning-level `console_request_slow`. The pathname deliberately
excludes the query string so operator search/filter text never lands in timing logs.
The SvelteKit BFF independently emits `agent_api_request_slow` at 250 ms around its
upstream fetch plus Effect Schema decode, which separates agent-side handler time from
the complete BFF hop when diagnosing a slow console view.

### Context dumps

On every model call, the full assembled context is written to a JSON file:

- Filename: `{timelineKey}.{sessionId}.{createdAt}.json`
- Contains: session ID, timeline key, trigger event ID, token estimate, cache boundaries, image blocks, and every message with role, tier, token estimate, and content
- Cache boundaries come from the single exported `CACHE_BOUNDARIES` const (`src/context/dump.ts`); the dump and the `/api/rooms/:key/context` endpoint reference the same const so they cannot drift (the value is an aspirational placeholder until real cache-breakpoint logic exists)
- Secrets are redacted in the serialized output

This makes the "what did the model actually see?" question trivially answerable.

### Observability console (in-process read API + SSE)

An optional HTTP + SSE server (`src/observability/server/`) that exposes the agent's internal state for inspection — the durable session records (frozen context snapshot + transcript), the live context any room would build next, and a live stream of a running session's events. It is the first consumer of the persisted sessions (see §8) and the backend for the console UI. The surface is **read-only except for the operator mutations** — `POST /api/sessions/:id/abort` (the Stop button; §8 "Interrupting a session", §13), `POST /api/sessions/:id/resume` (manual resume-in-place of a parked `failed-resumable` or `interrupted` session; §8 "Session parking & manual resume" / Decision D — long-polls until the resumed run settles; 409 on a re-park, a non-resumable status, a synthetic session type (summarize/condense/diary), a resume already in flight, a busy timeline slot, or nothing to redo; 503 when no resume action is injected), and the pipeline retry actions. Originally **Part B Phase 2** (read API only); the broader admin set (delete/reset/re-run) remains deferred (see "Appendix: Deferred designs" for the admin/pause-resume direction).

**Why in-process** (not a sidecar reading SQLite + dumps): the room view is *hypothetical* ("what the next session would see") and has no stored artifact — it must be produced by the real `ContextBuilder.build()`. Live session streaming needs the in-memory `Agent` (`subscribe`). A sidecar would have to re-implement context assembly (a fidelity risk — the whole point is byte-fidelity) and still couldn't see live state.

**Config** (`[observability.server]`, off by default; bind localhost):

```toml
[observability.server]
enabled = false
bind = "127.0.0.1"
port = 8799
# auth_token = "${MIKUSWARM_CONSOLE_TOKEN}"   # when set, required as a bearer token; must be non-blank (omit the key to disable auth)
```

**Endpoints** (all `GET` except the final `POST`):

| Path | Returns |
|---|---|
| `/api/rooms` | **rooms**, reverse-chron: `timelineKey`, `provider`/`accountId` (the key's first two segments, parsed server-side via `parseTimelineKey` so the console can group rooms per account without re-implementing the key grammar; null for a malformed key), display name, lifecycle state, last-activity ts, event/session counts. The display name is the cached human room label from `room_metadata` (resolved by `RoomLabelCache`, see §6 "Room-label resolution"), falling back to the raw `timelineKey` when no label has been resolved yet (an idle room with no recent activity shows its id until its next message or the next startup backfill). The row set is the **union** of timelines with `timeline_events` *or* `agent_sessions` rows, so a timeline whose events were pruned (retention/§13 delete-events) but whose sessions persist still appears (its sessions stay reachable via drill-down). **Thread sub-timelines are folded into their parent room**: a thread (`<roomKey>:thread:<root>`) is a first-class timeline with its own context/summarization, but it resolves to its parent room's label and would otherwise list as an indistinguishable duplicate "room"; so each anchor is mapped to its canonical room key (`:thread:<root>` stripped) and the union is **grouped by room** — one row per room, `event_count`/`session_count` summed and `last_activity_at` maxed across the room and all its threads, with `displayName`/`timelineState` taken from the room key. The room→session drill-down matches the same room+threads set (below), so the aggregated count stays consistent and thread sessions remain reachable. `last_activity_at` per timeline falls back to the latest session activity (`max(created_at, updated_at)`) when no events survive, keeping reverse-chron ordering sensible |
| `/api/rooms/:key/context` | the live `ContextBuilder.build()` for the room in **preview** mode (synthetic trigger = most recent event); the trigger-dependent final user turn is flagged `preview: true`; also carries tier token totals and `cacheBoundaries`. A synthetic `tools` item (the tool-definition block + per-tool breakdown) is prepended **above** the system message when tools resolve (§10a "Tool-definition block"); the `system` message additionally carries `segments` — the per-file/skill system-prompt token breakdown (live-preview-only, never on persisted snapshots; see §10a "System-prompt segment breakdown") |
| `/api/rooms/:key/sessions` | persisted `agent_sessions` for the room, reverse-chron (list/meta shape; no `contextDumpPath`). Both filtered and unfiltered SQL paths project only the metadata consumed by `sessionMeta`; they deliberately do **not** materialize `context_snapshot_json`, `context_dump_path`, or `transcript_json`. Those heavyweight columns remain available unchanged through the single-session detail endpoint below, so clicking a session still renders its complete frozen context and rollout. The match is the room key **plus its thread sub-timelines** (`<key>:thread:%`), so a session that landed on a thread timeline is reachable from its room — mirroring how `/api/rooms` folds threads into the room (a sibling room's threads never leak in; the room key's LIKE metacharacters are escaped). Optional **filters** (§11 "Sessions filter"), AND-combined: `q` (free-text FTS5 search over the session's **user messages** — its trigger message `trigger_body` via `agent_sessions_fts` **OR** any of its interjection bodies via `session_interjections_fts`, OR'd so the "timeline message → session" debug path finds a session by either, returned once regardless of how many interjections match), `status` (one or more lifecycle statuses, repeatable or CSV; values outside the known enum are ignored), `type` (one or more session types, repeatable or CSV). With no params this is byte-identical to the original unfiltered list (`getAgentSessionsByTimeline`); with any param it runs `searchAgentSessionsByTimeline`. A non-empty `q` that tokenizes to nothing usable (pure punctuation) returns `[]` rather than falling back to "all". The `q` text is sanitized by `sanitizeTriggerFtsMatch`, which quotes each term to neutralize FTS5 operators (user input can never inject syntax) and — because this powers a **search-as-you-type** box, unlike the agent-facing `search_messages` where a bare term is an exact-token match — makes **every term an implicit prefix** query, so typing "roc" finds "rocket" (FTS5 prefixes match from the start of a token only) |
| `/api/rooms/:key/session-facets` | `{ types: string[] }` — the distinct `session_type` values present in the room (and its thread sub-timelines, matching the sessions drill-down), sorted, to populate the sessions filter's type menu. Statuses are a fixed enum the client already knows, so only the open-ended types are served |
| `/api/sessions/:id` | the session record + frozen `contextSnapshot` (input context) + persisted `transcript` (rollout) + `rolloutStartIndex` (where the rollout begins inside `transcript`, i.e. past the **leading** run of head final-user-turn `triggerGroup`/`satellite` messages — `rolloutStartIndex` skips only that leading run, so a **resumed** session's appended `triggerGroup` (§8 "Resumable sessions"; built by `buildResumeTurn`) is a *non-leading* final turn that lives **inside** the rollout slice. The rollout renderer therefore renders a `triggerGroup`/`satellite` message it encounters via the same verbatim `MessageBlock` as the head kickoff — tier gutter + XML highlight — rather than the raw-JSON fallback; without that branch a resume turn rendered as a JSON dump of the message object) + `contextDumpPath` (on-disk dump path for parity debugging; detail-only). The `contextSnapshot` is **normalized to the same context wire shape as `/api/rooms/:key/context`** (`renderContextMessage`): the persisted JSON drops optional `ContextMessage` keys (`tier`/`timestamp`) and holds already-externalized image refs under `imageBlocks`, so the handler re-renders each message with present-or-null `tier`/`tokenEstimate`/`timestamp` and refs under `imageRefs` — the one strict `ContextMessageWire` shape the console's BFF schema decodes for both endpoints. A synthetic `tools` item is prepended above the system message when the session's type resolves a tool block (`factory.toolBlockFor`; recomputed for display, its estimate already inside the row's frozen `tokenEstimate` — §10a "Tool-definition block") |
| `/api/sessions/:id/stream` | **SSE** of live `AgentEvent`s for a running session; closes on **run settlement** (`SessionManager.onSettle`), *not* on `agent_end`. A single `run()` drives several agent-loop invocations — the kickoff plus any forced-completion prompts — each emitting its own `agent_end` (with the injected user turn that begins the next turn in between); closing on the first would truncate the live view after one turn. The terminal `completed`/`failed-resumable`/`discarded` markers all evict the agent, which fires `onSettle` once (after the terminal status is persisted, so the client's settle-driven refetch reads the terminal record). A session that is terminal/evicted — *or* still in the manager map but whose run has already settled (the late-subscribe race) — emits one `not_live` event and closes (the console then renders from `/api/sessions/:id`). A live stream opens with one **`rollout_seed`** event — the canonical accumulated `agent.state.messages` (the same array the per-turn transcript flush serializes) plus the session-detail `rolloutStartIndex` — because `Agent.subscribe` delivers only future events: without the seed, a console attaching mid-run rendered an empty rollout until the next turn landed. The handler runs synchronously from subscribe to seed-send, so no live event can interleave before it (the client always folds seed → events, race-free). The seed's **leading final-turn messages** (the slice below `rolloutStartIndex` — the trigger turn) are not part of the rollout: `LiveRollout` hands them to `SessionView` (`onHead`) which renders them in the **verbatim input view** while running. Without this the trigger turn would be missing from the verbatim view until the run completes — the persisted transcript head isn't flushed until the first `turn_end` (§8 "Session capture") and the session query is not refetched mid-run (only on stream end). **Injected user turns** committed mid-run — interjections (`{type:'interjection'}`) and forced-completion prompts (`{role:'user'}`) — surface via the `message_start` events pi-agent-core emits for each (it emits `message_start`/`message_end` carrying the message before pushing it to state; the following `turn_end` carries the *assistant* reply, not the user turn). `LiveRollout` folds those into the rollout (filtered by `isInjectedUserTurn`, excluding the `triggerGroup`/`satellite` head an early attach could surface); without it an interjection injected after attach was invisible until completion. A `message_start` that merely re-delivers a turn the **seed** already carried (a commit landing just before attach, or a re-seed on reconnect) is dropped within the post-seed window via `isDuplicateInjectedTurn`, so it never renders twice. Additionally merges the Layer-0 tap's **tentative** events (spec LLM-FAILURE-HANDLING §4.2): `tentative_event` (a raw per-attempt LLM stream event, not yet committed — this is how tokens stream live now that Layer-0 buffers attempts to the terminal event) and `attempt_discarded` (the console clears the partial and shows "attempt n failed, retrying"). Distinct event kinds so clients can never confuse tentative output with authoritative `message_update`s |
| `/api/media/:ref` | image bytes for an externalized image ref (`:ref` = the media asset id), resolved beneath the workspace root with a path-traversal guard |
| `/api/summaries/:id` | a summary + its lineage (covered events / child summaries) for the detail column |
| `/api/scheduler` | **scheduler snapshot** (spec LLM-FAILURE-HANDLING §9.1): per-group budget state — `active`/`queue` entries with session/type/model/priority/key attribution plus held/waiting times, throttle `backoffUntil`, sticky escalations (the scheduler-wide map is **filtered per group to the keys with an active/queued entry in that group**, so an escalation appears only under the group actually holding it — never duplicated across cards) — beside per-model health (`healthy`/`unhealthy`, consecutive-failure streak, probe-in-flight, `nextProbeAt`, last failure, waiter count). The "who is waiting on what, and which model is down" feed; polled (SSE optional later). `503` when no scheduler is wired |
| `/api/llm-requests` | the in-memory **Layer-0 attempt ring** (spec §9.2; capacity `observability.llm_request_ring_size`, default 500), newest-first: `{ts, sessionId, sessionType, group, model, priority, attempt, admissionWaitMs, durationMs, outcome, status?, class?, errorMessage?}` (`errorMessage` carries the raw upstream error text — secret-redacted — rendered as the outcome tooltip). Deliberately not durable — the LLM gateway holds the authoritative wire log; this adds what the gateway cannot see (attribution, admission wait, attempt numbering, failures that never reached the wire). `503` when no ring is wired |
| `POST /api/sessions/:id/abort` | **operator Stop button** (§8 "Interrupting a session"): aborts the live run and marks the session `interrupted` via `SessionManager.interrupt()`. `200 { sessionId, status: "interrupted" }` on success; `404` for an unknown id; `409` when the session exists but isn't actively running — idempotent-friendly, so a double-click or a click on an already-finished session is benign. The `409` uses the server's **standard error envelope with structured details**: `{ error: { status: 409, message, sessionId, sessionStatus } }`, where `sessionStatus` is the session's current lifecycle status (e.g. `completed`, `interrupted`) — deliberately named distinctly from the HTTP `error.status` so the two never collide (this was the one route that previously hand-rolled a mixed `{ error, sessionId, status }` shape). The `message` embeds `sessionStatus` too, because the BFF surfaces the raw non-ok 409 body **text** as the operator-facing error message (`console/src/lib/server/api/client.ts` reads `res.text()` into `ApiError.message`), so the message must read sensibly on its own. One of two mutating routes; bearer-auth gates it like every other, **and** it requires the `x-console-request` CSRF-guard header (see below) — without it the request is rejected `403` before dispatch |
| `/api/pipelines` | **pipeline monitor dashboard** (§11 "Pipeline monitor"): one row per pool (`enrichment`/`captioning`/`summarization`/`diary`) — `{ pool, enabled, workerCount, maxRetries, inFlight, counts }`. `counts` are six DB-derived status buckets (`pending`/`processing`/`retrying`/`done`/`failed`/`skipped`); `inFlight` is the one live number (the pool's `activeWorkers.size`). A pool disabled by config reports `enabled: false`, `inFlight: 0` but still surfaces its historical `counts` |
| `/api/pipelines/:pool/items?status=&room=&cursor=&limit=` | keyset-paginated `PipelineItem[]` + `nextCursor`, reverse-chron on `(updatedAt, id)` (diary sorts on `latest_timestamp`). `status`/`room` are indexed filters; `status=pending`/`status=retrying` are pseudo-filters matching the same predicate as the count buckets (retrying = a `pending` row with prior attempts) |
| `/api/pipelines/:pool/items/:id` | the pool-specific detail union: enrichment → produced `mediaAssets`/`linkPreviews`/`replyContext`; captioning → source `media` (bytes via `/api/media/:ref`) + caption/model; summarization → resulting `summary` + lineage + best-effort draft/error + `sessionId`; diary → source `summary` + `sessionId`. For the LLM-backed pools `sessionId` links the latest synthetic `agent_sessions` run so the client reuses `SessionView` |
| `/api/pipelines/stream` | **SSE** firehose of `PipelineActivityEvent`s across all pools (`{ pool, id, kind: claimed\|completed\|failed\|retried\|skipped, status, attempts, room, ts }`, one `event: activity` record each); redacted + heartbeat like the session stream; opens-and-idles when no activity bus is wired |
| `POST /api/pipelines/:pool/items/:id/retry` | **manual retry** (§11 "Pipeline monitor"): resets a terminal *safe* item to `pending` (attempts→0, error cleared) and pokes the pool's wake seam. `200 { pool, id, status: "pending" }`; `404` unknown item/pool; `409` when not retryable — `processing`, or a deferred-unsafe state (summarization `complete`, diary `done`). The `409` uses the standard error envelope with details `{ pool, id, itemStatus }` and a self-describing message. The second mutating route; same CSRF guard |
| `POST /api/pipelines/:pool/retry-failed` | **bulk retry** — reset every `failed` item in a pool to `pending`; `200 { pool, retried }`. Same CSRF guard |
| `/api/usage/summary?window=` | **Usage & Cost** (§8e/§8f): totals by class + by model over a window (`today`/`24h`/`7d`/`30d`/`month`/`all`), from `getUsageSummary` over `usage_events`. Also returns `now` (the server clock the window was computed against) and `firstTs` (earliest event *within* the window, or null) — the console divides spend by the **actual elapsed data range** `now − firstTs`, not the nominal window width, so per-period averages don't pretend to data that doesn't exist (a 30d view a week into history averages over ~7d; a month view on the 15th over ~15d not 31). The calendar-aligned `today`/`month` windows snap to the **UTC** boundary (a display convenience — budget *rules* use their own configured tz, §8e) and the console marks them "UTC" so an operator isn't misled when they disagree with a tz'd rule |
| `/api/usage/timeseries?window=&groupBy=class\|model` | stacked spend series for the chart, bucketed by range: hourly ≤24h, daily for bounded windows, and **span-scaled** for `all` (day ≤31d → week ≤52w → month ≤36mo → year) so the histogram stays legible as history grows — `all` derives its span from the earliest event (`minUsageTs`). The leaderboard endpoint uses the identical width so the cards' sub-period averages re-bin consistently |
| `/api/usage/sessions?limit=` | recent sessions joined with their per-class `usage_events` rollup (agent-LLM vs tool cost, tokens, `status`, channel, type, trigger). Each row carries `channelLabel` — the human `Name (Space)` room label coalesced from `room_metadata` (else the raw timeline key) — beside the raw `timelineKey` |
| `/api/usage/tool-calls?limit=` | recent paid `tool`/`caption`/`embedding` events, newest-first; each row left-joins `room_metadata` to add `channel_label` (null only when the event has no `timeline_key`) |
| `/api/usage/leaderboard?window=&limit=` | **per-actor spend leaderboard** (§7.1 leaderboard tab): a humans-only ranking + a separate system/self block. `users` are real trigger senders (system session types excluded), ranked **contiguously 1..N** by spend with zero-spend dropped — up to `limit` (default 1000) so the console paginates to the lowest non-zero spender — each with totals/events/distinct-sessions, active range, contiguous `rank`, and (top-10 only) its own per-bucket `series`. `systemActors` are the non-human/self workloads (Summarization = summarize+condense, Diary, Proactive), attributed by `session_type`, each with a **`comparisonRank`** = where it would sit among users, and its own series. Also returns `userStats` (count/average/median over the non-zero humans, for the System & self reference cards), `grandTotal` over **every** event (incl. non-attributable null-sender spend) as the share denominator, and `bucketMs`/`now`. Background caption/embedding (null sender + null type) belongs to neither list but counts in `grandTotal`; display names resolve to each sender's most-recent non-null `agent_sessions` name |
| `/api/usage/budgets` | every configured `[[limits]]` rule's live status from `BudgetEngine.ruleStatuses()` (spent/cap/fraction/state/window/resets/scope, plus `components` = per-model spend for a multi-model rule so its bar segments), plus `userSelections` (live per-user model picks, each labeled with the sender's `displayName`/`username` via `Storage.getUserLabels`); each empty when none configured / not wired |
| `/api/usage/user-limits?scope=individuals\|shared&page=` | one hottest-first page of per-user / shared-pool meters from `UserLimitEngine.groupedStatuses()` (grouped by partition, split individuals/shared, non-human senders excluded, 20 groups/page), each meter spent/cap/state/resets/`orderIndex`/scope, user-partition meters additionally labeled with the sender's `displayName`/`username` (`Storage.getUserLabels`: `user_identities` for Discord username + nick, falling back to the latest `agent_sessions.trigger_sender_display_name` — the Matrix name source; nulls when unknown) (ledger-seeded at startup §14) — rendered as scope-tabbed, paginated strips of per-cap health bars; plus `totals` (per-scope group counts). Empty when the feature is off |

The **Usage & Cost** console page (`console/src/routes/usage-cost`, in the TopBar nav) renders these behind the polled remote-function pattern: a window + breakdown (class\|model) selector; a **Total spend** card that also carries the **sub-period averages** (per hour for day-scale windows, per day + per week for larger ones, per day/week/month/year for All time, each shown only while it's coarser than the buckets and the data range covers it) — each an average plus a min/max/σ spread, computed in `$lib/spend-averages` (pure, unit-tested) over the actual elapsed range from `firstTs`/`now` (the spread is across *fully-elapsed* periods, zero-filled; a sub-period longer than the elapsed data is suppressed rather than extrapolated); by-class and top-model cards; a stacked spend-over-time chart (inline SVG stacked-bar — the spec's LayerChart dependency was deferred to avoid the console's first charting lib) with a **real y-axis** (rounded USD gridlines + labels via `niceTicks` in `$lib/spend-chart`) and a **per-column hover tooltip** (each group's value + %, the column total, and the bucket's time span — so the chart reads in absolute terms, not by colour/legend alone); a **Limits** section — now *below* the chart — showing **every** configured rule (fill bar, window, resets-in countdown, scope summary, ok/near/blocked badge — blocked sorted to top, the operator's single source of truth and the `usage_limit_blocked` log join key); and — below Limits — a **tabbed detail area** switching between *Recent sessions*, *Recent paid calls*, and a *User leaderboard*. The active tab, the window, and the class\|model breakdown all live in **query params** (`?tab=&window=&group=`, URL-as-source-of-truth like the rest of the console, read from `page.url` and written via `goto(replaceState)` so a filter toggle doesn't pile up history) — the view is deep-linkable and survives refresh; each tab-bound feed only polls while its tab is visible. The window selector includes an **All time** range alongside the bounded ones. *Recent sessions* and *Recent paid calls* render the channel via a shared `ChannelCell` (the human `Name (Space)` label as a popover trigger; the popover shows the raw timeline id + a copy-to-clipboard button), and *Recent sessions* also surfaces a `status` column so a $0 row — e.g. a coalesced/early-exit session that issued no billable LLM call — is explainable rather than mysterious. The **User leaderboard** is a real-users-only ranking: the top-10 humans rendered as cards (an `auto-fill` grid) above a **client-side-paginated** table (rank, user, spend, share-of-total, events, sessions, first/last seen) that reaches the lowest non-zero spender. Non-human/self actors are pulled out into a distinct **System & self** section — Summarization, Diary, and Proactive cards, each marked (bot icon + violet accent) and showing a *comparison* rank ("would be #N among users") rather than a leaderboard position, beside **Average user** / **Median user** reference cards (over the non-zero humans). The Total card and every actor card are one shared `SpendSummaryCard` component (label + total + range note + the sub-period averages table — leaderboard mode adds a rank badge, share-of-total chip, and event/session counts; `system` mode swaps in the bot icon + comparison-rank chrome), so a card cannot drift from the Total card's presentation. Background polls update data in place with no per-refetch indicator, and window / breakdown / leaderboard switches use TanStack `keepPreviousData` so the cards/chart never blank-flash between cache entries.

**Fidelity guarantee.** `/api/rooms/:key/context` calls `AgentSessionFactory.buildPreview()`, which shares the *single* `ContextBuilder.build()` call site with the live session path (`buildContext`), so the preview cannot drift from what a real session would build. Everything through the rich tier is real and current; only the final user turn is synthetic, and it is labeled `preview: true`.

**Auth & redaction.** When `auth_token` is set it is required as a bearer token on every request and SSE connection — accepted via `Authorization: Bearer <t>` **or** a `?token=<t>` query param (the browser `EventSource` API can't set headers). Fail-fast (issue #5): an **absent** `auth_token` means auth is intentionally disabled (the localhost-operator default — every request is allowed), but a **present-but-blank** token (empty or whitespace-only, e.g. `${MIKUSWARM_CONSOLE_TOKEN}` expanding to "") when the server is enabled is a configuration error rejected at load time rather than silently opening the console. The schema enforces `minLength: 1` on the token; a cross-field check in the config loader catches whitespace-only values. Every JSON body and every SSE payload passes through `redactSecrets`; image blocks are externalized to refs (no base64 over the wire), identical to the persisted transcript, so the live and historical rollout views match.

**CSRF guard (mutating routes are console-BFF-only).** The mutating routes (`POST /api/sessions/:id/abort` and the pipeline-monitor retry routes `POST /api/pipelines/:pool/items/:id/retry` + `POST /api/pipelines/:pool/retry-failed`) are reached **only** by the console BFF's server-side `fetch` (§11 "Console frontend") — nothing browser-facing calls them directly. In production the observability port is **not** exposed to browsers: a reverse proxy fronts the console domain and routes server-to-server to the in-process server. To defend the default localhost-no-token deployment against a CSRF (a page the operator's browser loads firing an auto-submitted form / `no-cors` POST — localhost bind stops network peers but not the operator's own browser), the BFF attaches a constant custom header `x-console-request: 1` to its requests (`console/src/lib/server/api/client.ts`), and the server rejects any mutating request (`POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE`) lacking that header with `403` **before dispatch** (`src/observability/server/index.ts`, the `CONSOLE_REQUEST_HEADER` constant). A *custom* header makes a cross-origin browser request non-"simple", forcing a CORS preflight; this server implements no CORS, so the browser blocks the actual request — and a simple-request CSRF cannot set the header in the first place. The header is a marker, **not** a credential (the bearer token remains the authenticator); the legitimate server-to-server BFF hop is not subject to CORS and passes. `GET` reads (including the SSE `?token=` stream path) need no header and are unaffected.

**Why SSE, not WebSockets.** The only realtime need is *server→client* streaming of a running session's `AgentEvent`s — the console never sends data as the agent (out of scope by design). SSE rides the existing `node:http` server with the same bearer-auth and redaction pipeline and adds **zero dependencies**; the text protocol is trivially consumable by any client (`EventSource`, or the console's `fetch`+`ReadableStream` parser) and matches the "closes on `agent_end`" lifecycle. WebSockets would add an `Upgrade` handshake, a new dependency, binary framing we don't need, and a separate auth path (browsers can't set an `Authorization` header on a WS). WS would only win if the console needed high-rate client→server input (a chat surface), which is explicitly out of scope.

**Liveness & isolation.** `pi-agent-core`'s `Agent.subscribe` delivers only *future* events; the agent is evicted from `SessionManager`'s map only *after* its run settles. So a subscriber can attach while `getAgent(id)` still returns an agent whose run has already ended. The stream handler subscribes to agent events **and registers `SessionManager.onSettle` first** — so a settlement landing between the `getAgent` check and registration still closes the stream — then re-checks `SessionManager.isAgentLive(id)` (session `running` *and* `agent.signal !== undefined`, not mere map presence), self-closing with a `not_live` event if the run already settled (that race fired `onSettle` with no listener attached, so the re-check is the backstop). When the run *is* live, the handler sends the `rollout_seed` catch-up event (see the endpoint table above) so mid-run attaches start from the accumulated state instead of empty, and the stream then stays open across the whole run, closing on `onSettle`. The convention that callers `markRunning` before `attachAgent` is documented on `attachAgent` and defended by `isAgentLive`. Only chat/proactive (`launchSession`) and manual-resume sessions `attachAgent`, and all of them reach a terminal marker → `evict` → `onSettle`; worker-pool sessions (summarize/condense/diary) never attach an agent, so they take the `not_live` path and never reach the spanning logic. The convention that callers `markRunning` before `attachAgent` is documented on `attachAgent` and defended by `isAgentLive`. Separately, the SSE layer is a **read-only observer**: every `res.write` is wrapped best-effort and a write throw (or a `req`/`res` `"error"`) is treated as a client disconnect, so a dead/reset socket can never throw out of the `agent.subscribe` listener and into the live agent loop.

### Pipeline monitor (background-worker observability)

The session views above cover *conversational* agent sessions. The four background worker pools that keep context healthy — **enrichment** (§7a), **captioning** (§7a), **summarization** (§9b), **diary** (§9c) — get their own monitor: a separate console area answering *are they working? what is queued / in-flight / done / failing, and why? what went in and what came out?*

**Unified read model over heterogeneous queues.** The four queues live in different tables (`timeline_events.enrichment_status`, `media_assets.caption_status`, the `summarization_jobs` table, `summaries.diary_status`) with different status vocabularies. The monitor projects them onto one shape. Counts (`getPipelineCounts`) normalize each pool's raw statuses into buckets — `pending`/`processing`/`retrying`/`done`/`failed`/`skipped` plus a captioning-only `deferred` — where `retrying` is a `pending` row with prior attempts and enrichment's never-queued `inactive` rows are excluded. The item list (`listPipelineItems`) projects one row per queue entry into a common `PipelineItem` (`pool`, `id`, raw `status`, `attempts`/`maxRetries`, derived `retrying`, `room`, timestamps, short input/output descriptors, persisted `error`, and `sessionId` for the LLM-backed pools), keyset-paginated reverse-chron on `(updatedAt, id)`.

**Default view hides noise; `deferred` is the captioning "parked" partition.** The raw queues are dominated by rows that are *not actionable signal*: enrichment marks every plain message (nothing to extract) `skipped`, and captioning parks media on non-trigger messages at `pending` indefinitely whenever neither `caption_all` nor `caption_assistant_messages` is set (the pool would never claim them). So the monitor would otherwise read as an all-message timeline. Two mechanisms fix this. (1) **The captioning `deferred` derived status** (`CaptionEligibility`): a fresh (`attempts = 0`) pending asset whose event the pool would never claim — mirroring the `claimPendingCaptions` predicate `trigger_group_id is not null OR caption_all OR (role = 'assistant' AND caption_assistant_messages)` — is surfaced as `deferred` rather than `pending`, both in the projected row's badge and as its own count bucket carved out of `pending` (so the `pending` count/chip is honest backlog). The eligibility booleans ride the pool's `stats()` seam (`PipelineStats.captionEligibility`) and are threaded into `getPipelineCounts`/`listPipelineItems`/`getPipelineItem` for the captioning pool only; absent (no registry, or any other pool) there is no `deferred` partition. The booleans are inlined as SQL literals (trusted config, not user input), so the fragment carries no bind params; captioning's count/list specs join `timeline_events` to evaluate it. (2) **The unfiltered list excludes the noise:** with no status chip selected, the list hides `skipped` (every pool) and `deferred` (captioning). Selecting the explicit `skipped`/`deferred` chip reveals them — the debugging path. Because this default is now the *primary* enrichment view, it is index-backed: the enrichment list spec's `defaultScope` emits the single predicate `enrichment_status not in ('inactive', 'skipped')` (superseding the per-branch `scope`), which matches the partial index `idx_timeline_events_active_updated` (`(updated_at, id) where enrichment_status not in ('inactive','skipped')`) verbatim — so the default view is a bounded keyset walk over the non-noise minority, never a scan past the skipped majority (verified via `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN`). The partial index covers only the minority of rows, so it is small and cheap to maintain. Captioning's `deferred` exclusion is config-relative (a `timeline_events` join), so it stays a residual filter over the much smaller `media_assets` table rather than a static index. `deferred` is config-relative: turning a captioning flag on reclassifies those rows back to `pending` live, no migration. (The retry path reads items without eligibility, so a `deferred` row reads as raw `pending` there — not in `PIPELINE_SAFE_RETRY`, so it is correctly not manually retryable; it auto-runs once eligible.) The summarization input descriptor (`L<level> · <input>→<to> tok`) renders the `<to>` side as `actual/target` once the job completes — a correlated subquery joins `result_summary_id` to `summaries.token_count` (the real produced size), falling back to `target_token_count` alone while the job is still pending/running — so target-vs-actual drift (e.g. tuning `leaf_target_tokens`/`condense_target_tokens`) is visible at a glance. The unfiltered list pages on a `(updated_at/latest_timestamp, id)` index; a **status-filtered** list (the monitor's primary "what is failing?" view) pages on the companion `(status, updated_at/latest_timestamp, id)` index as a covering index search, so neither the unfiltered nor the per-status view ever full-scans the large enrichment/captioning tables. All of this is **DB-derived** — the single source of truth that survives restart.

**Count aggregate cost (perf watch-item).** `getPipelineCounts` is a multi-way `SUM(CASE…)` over a whole pool table (a seventh `deferred` case + a `timeline_events` join for captioning), polled every 5s by the dashboard. Its `pending`/`retrying` buckets split the raw `pending` status on the per-pool attempts column (`enrichment_retries`/`caption_attempts`/`attempts`/`diary_attempts`), which the `(status, updated_at)` keyset indexes do **not** carry — so SQLite plans it as a full table `SCAN`, not a covering-index scan (verified via `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN`). The v10 status index therefore speeds up the status-filtered *lists* but **not** the count aggregate. This is left as a deliberate full scan rather than adding a speculative `(status, attempts)` covering index: counts only need to be DB-derived (spec §3.1), the poll is a single cheap query at 5s, and a dedicated count index would duplicate the attempts column for a query that is not on any hot path. Revisit if the enrichment/captioning tables grow large enough that the 5s scan shows up in profiles — at that point a covering `(status, attempts)` index (or a `count(*) GROUP BY status` reshaping) would let the buckets come straight from an index.

**Pool stats seam.** Each pool exposes a tiny read-only `stats(): PipelineStats` (`workerCount`, `maxRetries`, live `inFlight()` = `activeWorkers.size`, and a `notify()` wake hook). `app.ts` assembles the four into a `PipelineRegistry` handed to the observability server; counts come from the DB, only `inFlight` is read live off the pool. This is the one genuinely-live number the DB can't provide.

**Live activity bus.** A lightweight in-process `PipelineActivityBus` (a `Set`-backed pub/sub, no `EventEmitter`) carries `PipelineActivityEvent`s. Each pool publishes at its existing transition points — **claim** (→processing), **complete** (→complete/done; a best-effort truncated summary is still a `complete` job — `truncated` is a summaries-row status, not a job/activity status), **fail-terminal**, **retry** (→pending), **skipped** — as one-liners *additive* to the existing `onComplete`/`onError` callbacks (which are untouched). `app.ts` creates one shared bus, hands it to all four pools and the server; `/api/pipelines/stream` subscribes and fans out. Same **read-only-observer** discipline as the session SSE: the bus isolates a throwing subscriber per-listener and the SSE `safeWrite` never throws, so a dead console socket can never propagate back into a worker loop's `publish()`.

**Durable captioning retries.** Captioning's retry counter was in-memory only (`failureCounts`), so "what's retrying" was lost on restart and invisible to the DB-derived counts. It is now the durable `media_assets.caption_attempts` column, incremented inside the claim CAS (mirroring `enrichment_retries` / `summarization_jobs.attempts` / `summaries.diary_attempts`); `media_assets` also carries `updated_at` (bumped on every caption write) as the monitor's reverse-chron sort key. The genesis schema defines both columns + the four `(updated_at, id)` keyset indexes and the four companion `(status, updated_at)` indexes so status-filtered lists are index-backed (the count aggregate still scans — see "Count aggregate cost" above).

**Manual retry (the second mutating surface).** `POST /api/pipelines/:pool/items/:id/retry` re-enqueues a terminal item — status→`pending`, attempts→0, error cleared — then pokes the pool's `notify()` seam so it re-claims at once rather than on the next poll tick (the reset goes through the single-writer queue like every write). It is gated by **per-pool safety** (`PIPELINE_SAFE_RETRY`): enrichment/captioning are idempotent (the produce/caption paths overwrite), so `failed`/`complete`/`skipped` are all safe (re-captioning after a model change is a valid use); diary allows `failed`/`skipped` but **defers `done`** (the memory day-file is append-only with no dedup — a re-run would duplicate the entry); summarization allows only `failed` and **defers `complete`** (the summary may already feed a higher-level condensation and a diary entry — regenerating is a cascade delete, not a status flip; a best-effort truncated summary is persisted as a `complete` job, so it is covered by this same deferral — `truncated` is a summaries-row status, never a job status). `processing` is never retryable (stop the linked session instead). Deferred/in-flight states return `409` with the reason. `POST /api/pipelines/:pool/retry-failed` is the bulk wrapper over the unambiguously-safe `failed` state. Lifting the deferred cases needs a summary cascade-delete and a memory-file *replace* op — both real features deferred to "Appendix: Deferred designs".

### Console frontend (SvelteKit BFF, `console/`)

The console UI (spec §14, **Part B Phase 3**) is a **separate** SvelteKit app in the
top-level `console/` subproject — *not* served by the in-process server, and it builds
independently (like the native module; no build step is added to the agent runtime). It
runs as its own Node process (adapter-node) acting as a **typed BFF/proxy** in front of the
in-process read API: the API must stay in-process (it needs the live `ContextBuilder`,
`Agent.subscribe`, and the writer queue, above), and a SvelteKit app cannot share that
process's memory.

- **SvelteKit remote functions** are the typed boundary for request/response: `query` for the
  reads and `command` for admin actions — `abortSession`
  (`$lib/api/admin.remote.ts`) is the first to land, proxying `POST /api/sessions/:id/abort`
  via the API client's `post`; the upstream `409` (not running) surfaces as an `HttpError` the
  Stop-button handler treats as benign. The handler (`SessionView.svelte` `handleStop`) consumes
  the decoded 200 `status`: a success toast fires only when `status === "interrupted"` (the
  documented contract above), and any other status surfaces a neutral warning rather than being
  mislabeled as success. The remaining admin actions stay deferred.
- Remote-function bodies are **Effect** programs (an `AgentApiClient` layer) that decode every
  response through an **Effect Schema**; the same schema validates the remote-function input
  via `Schema.standardSchemaV1`. Decode failures surface as a BFF error, guarding fidelity.
- **Live streams are NOT remote functions.** The session rollout stream and the pipeline
  activity firehose flow through same-origin **SSE proxy routes**
  (`console/src/routes/api/sessions/[id]/stream/+server.ts`,
  `console/src/routes/api/pipelines/stream/+server.ts`) that pipe the agent's event-stream
  bytes verbatim — bearer token attached server-side, `request.signal` forwarded into the
  upstream fetch so a browser disconnect releases the agent's `Agent.subscribe`/activity-bus
  listener — and the browser parses the SSE records itself (`$lib/sse.ts` framing/parsing +
  `$lib/api/live.ts` `fetch`-based async generators that defensively narrow each payload). The
  session generator (`streamSessionEvents`) yields every `AgentEvent` and **returns a terminal
  reason** — `not_live` (the session is terminal/evicted) vs `closed` (a clean settlement) — and
  `consumeSessionStream` drives it with **reconnect + re-attach** (mirroring the pipeline firehose's
  reconnect loop): a dropped connection (proxy idle-timeout / network blip / agent restart) backs
  off and reconnects instead of freezing the live view until a manual refresh, and a clean `closed`
  reconnects once to re-check — the server answers `not_live` if the run is truly done (→ stop, fall
  back to the persisted record) or sends a fresh `rollout_seed` if it **resumed** (resume /
